## F-18 — Fix round 1: engine findings (each with a failing test first)
- **Stale drain (PROBE-1).** `ActiveAttempt.terminalReceived` is set when `finishAttempt` consumes the terminal element (and from the start for the publication of a drained file, which has no stream). `retire` and `applied` record a drain (`DrainingAttempt`, with its driver's key) only for an attempt whose stream is still open; `publish` discards the file when its job was retired before it began. Test: `R-SYN-4: a resolve cancelled while its failed attempt is handled leaves no stale drain, so the next resolve downloads`. New invariant: a drain whose driver ended.
- **Run claims (PROBE-2).** `reroute(_:interest:asset:from:)` releases the run's claim on the old transfer (`releaseRunClaim`), for supersession, a partial move in `applied` and an unbound commit; an asset that left the manifest drops a group member (an `ensureAvailable` group completes) and forgets a heal. `checkRunSettled` treats a claim on a job that lost the run's interest as failed (`.cancelled`), so a missed release can never strand the run. Tests: `R-SYN-6: a run whose job is superseded while it publishes still settles once its asset's new content commits`; `R-SYN-4: a waiter whose asset changed content while its shared job published follows the new content` (the same audit found that `committed` answered such a waiter `.notFound` and counted the run's asset as failed before rerouting it). New invariant: a run claim on a job without the run's interest.
- **`ensureAvailable`'s apply (PROBE-3).** It now calls the run's `applied(_:etag:report:)` (generation, `assetRemoved(.pruned)`, `snapshotChanged`, T21/T22 on jobs) as soon as the store committed, whatever the caller does next, then re-reads the store deadline (RE-S40). Test: `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable's apply announces pruned ids and stops their jobs, as a run's apply does`.
- **Scope during start (PROBE-4).** See f-1. Test: `R-CAR-5: a retention scope set while start is in progress reaches the store`.
- **Cancelled start joiner (PROBE-5).** `ensureStarted` answers `.cancelled` while start still runs. Test: `R-SYN-15: a caller cancelled while start runs gets cancelled, never shutDown`.
- **S1-13 (PROBE-6).** A fetched manifest removes the ids it lists from the negative cache, and an id the newest manifest lists is point-admitted, never answered from the cache. Test: `R-CAR-2: an identifier a run's fetched manifest lists is never answered notFound before the run applies it`.
- **Joiners of parked jobs (PROBE-8).** `addInterest` applies the fail-fast policy to a new interest in a job that stays `blocked(reason)` (or `failed(.whenOnline)`, as `.offline`), so it gets the answer a new job would get. Test: `R-SYN-4: a resolve that joins a job blocked for lack of space is answered at once, as a new job would be`.
- **Also.** RE-S36/RE-S20 lifecycle re-checks after `store.location` and the unreachable branch's `availability`; launch reconciliation's wanted set is computed off the actor (`wantedTransfers`, C2); maintenance announces what deep verification quarantined (`assetRemoved(.quarantined)`, as `reportUnreadable` does); start and sync joiners wake `awaitCondition` waits when they register.
- **Strengthened tests (verifier, reqs lens).** `R-SYN-8: reconcile is committed before the first transfer starts` stamps each transfer's start with the file-system operation count (`OperationStampingSource`) and requires the index rename between the run's start and the first transfer's start (fails under the verifier's mutant M3, checked in this round). `R-SYN-5: no transfer starts before a 429's Retry-After elapses on ManualTimeSource` pins the earliest sleeper at exactly the 429's instant plus 30 s, before and after advancing 29 s (fails under mutant M1, checked). `R-SYN-15: no store mutation or commit happens after close returns` now shuts down with a publication held at its first store unit (`NextUnitGate`), a coalesced commit to follow and a pruned blob's deletion due, takes the baseline at the store's lock release, and requires no owned task and no sleeper afterwards.

## F-19 — Recovery behaviour tests (fix round 1, coverage per file)
- **Decision.** Behaviour tests for paths no test checked: `R-STO-11: a migration call that leaves nothing remaining but cannot finish defers to the next maintenance window, which finishes it`; `R-SYN-16: maintenance finds a corrupted blob, announces its quarantine, and the engine downloads it again when the quarantine ends`; `R-SYN-5: a Retry-After beyond maxRetryAfter fails the waiter with that wait and ends the job for the run, holding nothing` (503 and 429); `R-SYN-5: a prefetch held by an origin throttle starts again exactly when the throttle ends`; `R-SYN-5: a prefetch blocked by an open breaker starts when the cooldown ends, and its success closes the breaker`; `R-SYN-6: a store failure while a run applies its manifest fails the run with the store's error and starts nothing`; and the safety nets: `R-SYN-2: an illegal transition is counted and logged, never applied`, `R-SYN-3: lane queues stay within twice their live entries while queued jobs move between lanes`, `R-SYN-4: a resolve that joins a job waiting to be online is answered offline at once`, `R-SYN-6: a run whose claimed job vanished without an outcome still settles, and counts the asset as failed` (a test seam edits the state no correct path produces), `R-SYN-15: a source built after shutdown began is invalidated at once and start fails with shutDown`.
- **Consequences.** Per-file line coverage of every `Engine/` file is at least 90 % (EngineFailures 90.33, EngineJobs 91.78, EngineRuns 90.73, EngineTimers 92.02, EngineLifecycle 90.18; target 92.76 %).
- **Known, not fixed (open issue).** A run plans from a snapshot: when another interest commits a planned asset between the run's planning view and its materialisation, the run's job downloads it again (a wasted transfer; the store deduplicates the blob). `R-CAR-2: an identifier a run's fetched manifest lists is never answered notFound before the run applies it` scripts a second attempt for that interleaving.


## F-20 — Fix round 2: engine findings (each with a failing test first) and the round's other changes
- **Context.** The round-2 verifiers (correctness and requirements lenses) found nine blocking engine defects and several non-blocking ones; each blocking one came with a probe. The probes became regression tests in `EngineRegressionRound2Tests` and were committed before the fixes (`fd10e55`): all 14 failed on the unfixed engine (13 on their expectations, three of which then hung on a waiter that could never be answered, and one by hanging), and all pass after `16d5585`; the round's later tests were checked the same way against the code they guard (noted with each).
- **The engine follows the store's manifest (PROBE-R2-A, S1-13/T22).** A publication the store answers `.superseded` while the engine's copy of the applied manifest still names that content (a store `apply` whose await has not returned, or one the engine never saw) no longer answers `.notFound`: the interests wait on a fresh, *held* job of the content (`heldForManifest`, never dispatched) until the copy catches up, either when the `apply` in flight returns (`applyAndFollow`, which counts in-flight applies) or, when none is in flight, by a re-read of the store (`catchUpWithStore`, REENTRANCY f-9); then `followApplied` moves them (T22) and the last catch-up releases the held jobs (T11 for any the copy still maps there). The same holds for an id a publication bound before the fact but the store refused to bind. Options considered: re-route to `state.newest` (depends on that bookkeeping and cannot cover a manifest the engine never fetched), always re-read the store (cannot see an apply that has not committed its writes yet), retry at once (a wasted download per round trip). New invariant: held jobs with no catch-up in flight. Tests: `R-SYN-4: a waiter whose job the store supersedes while a run's apply still commits follows the asset's new content` (no second start of the superseded content), `R-SYN-4: a waiter whose job the store supersedes with a manifest the engine never applied follows the store's content`, `R-SYN-4: an id a shared publication cannot bind because the store moved it to new content follows that content`.
- **A purge during a run's apply (PROBE-R2-B).** `runSteps` follows a committed apply (`applied`, then the store deadline) before it checks that the run is still current, as `ensureAvailable` does (F-18). Test: `R-CLN-6: a purge during a run's apply leaves the engine following the manifest the store applied` (engine copy, waiter, `assetRemoved(.pruned)`).
- **Every half-open probe exit is recorded (PROBE-R2-E/E2, §7.7).** `followApplied` records `.abandoned` for a downloading job it supersedes; `retire` records it whether or not the stream already ended; `publicationFailed` records `.abandoned` (superseded, cancelled, closed, not open) or `.neutral` (every other store verdict) before handling the error, and a publication dropped because its job is gone records `.abandoned`. A publication's object success is recorded once the store committed, even when the job vanished meanwhile. Tests: `R-SYN-5: a half-open probe whose publication fails the store's hash check frees the probe slot`, `R-SYN-5: a half-open probe superseded by an apply frees the probe slot`.
- **Re-attached transfers (PROBE-R2-H, R-NET-3).** A run releases only the launch re-attachments it does not plan; one it plans keeps its system interest until the run's own joins the job (materialisation is bounded, S1-15), so the live OS task is joined, never cancelled and restarted. Tests: `R-NET-3: the first run after a relaunch joins a re-attached live transfer it plans, never cancels and restarts it`, `R-NET-3: a launch run that is due joins the re-attached live transfer it plans`.
- **Shared content joined while it publishes (PROBE-R2-G, ADR-020).** `committed` moves an unbound asset whose entry is still this content to a fresh job of it *after* the finishing job is gone (`resolveAll`'s `then`, before the run is checked for settling), so the next publication binds it; the store deduplicates the bytes, at the cost of a second download in this narrow race (the store has no call that binds an id to a live blob; a contract change would be needed to avoid it). Tests: `R-SYN-4: a resolve that joins a shared-content job while it verifies is answered with its location`, `R-SYN-6: a run whose asset joins a shared-content job while it verifies fetches it and completes`.
- **Space (PROBE-R2-C, T17).** ENOSPC is decided on a fresh read: pending reclaim → `awaitingSpace`; a volume that holds what the job needs → a retry with backoff within the lane's attempts (the waiter gets the store's figures when they run out); otherwise `insufficientSpace`. The ledger counts the larger of the entry's size and the size the ENOSPC reported. `insufficientSpace` is re-evaluated at every free-space read (`reconsiderSpaceBlocked`: refresh, a run's read, `ensureAvailable`'s read), by a promoting join (`blockCleared`) and by any join (`failFastJoiner` re-checks before failing fast); the fail-fast answer uses `lastError` only when it is a space error. Tests: `R-SYN-11: an insufficient-space failure on a volume that holds the transfer is retried, never parked`, `R-SYN-11: a job parked for lack of space starts once a fresh free-space read shows room`, `R-SYN-11: a resolve that joins a job parked for lack of space downloads it once the job fits`, `R-SYN-11: a prefetch that joins a job parked for lack of space starts it once it fits`.
- **Partials without a sidecar (PROBE-R2-F).** `resumePoint` discards the attempt's partial whenever no sidecar was read, whatever the transport's resumable flag said (the flag is no longer a parameter). Test: `R-CLN-3: a failed attempt's partial without a sidecar is discarded, not left in staging`.
- **A purge and prefetch groups (PROBE-R2-D).** A purge withdraws only the purged ids from a non-user interest, drops them from the interest's group (and forgets a heal of them). Test: `R-CLN-6: a purge drops the prefetch group members whose interests it withdraws`; the R-SYN-2 model test now also runs purges (scoped and evictable) beside its other operations and fails without this fix (checked in this round: 2 of 2 runs flagged "group … member … without an interest").
- **Events to a stalled subscriber (PROBE-R2-I, C2).** `broadcast` and `pull` mutate each subscriber queue in place through the dictionary; copying it out and back made every publication O(backlog). Benchmark (release): `R-SYN-12: announcing a 10,000-id prune to a stalled subscriber costs at most twice the same sync with nobody subscribed`; measured 0.0070 s unobserved vs 0.0104 s stalled (ratio 1.50) after the fix, 0.0071 s vs 0.987 s (ratio 139) with the old `broadcast` (checked in this round). The 10,000 announcements take about 3.5 ms of one actor turn; announcing in chunks across turns or folding them into one event was not done: the per-id `assetRemoved` is the contract, and chunking would let later events (`snapshotChanged`, `syncFinished`) overtake them. At `maxEntries` (100,000) the turn is estimated at about 35 ms by extrapolation (not measured), next to an `apply` of that size in the store.
- **`ensureAvailable` registration (verifier, C2; measured).** Registering every planned fetch at once held the actor 23 ms at 10,000 missing entries and 435 ms at 100,000 (release, measured in this round with a temporary probe). Its fetches now become members and jobs a pipeline at a time, like a run's (`Group.fetches`/`cursor`, `materializeEnsures` at the top of `pump`; a batch that fails fast inside a pump schedules one refill pump in a later turn, never a loop). A fetch follows its asset's current entry and is dropped when the asset left the manifest. Tests: `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable turns its fetches into jobs a pipeline at a time and completes the whole scope` (peak 8 jobs for 40 fetches), `R-SYN-10: an ensureAvailable whose members fail fast as they become jobs still settles, a batch per actor turn` (hangs without the refill, checked).
- **S1-13 edges (verifier, unproven; fixed).** A point-admission refetch that fails, is invalid, throttled or refused by the breaker still answers from the newest manifest seen: an id it (or the applied manifest) lists is admitted or joined; only the others get the error. `adoptApplied` keeps the newest *fetched* manifest once there is one (an applied manifest never comes from a later fetch), so a point admission, an `ensureAvailable` apply or a store re-read no longer drops a fetched, unapplied manifest from the S1-13 checks. Test: `R-CAR-2: an identifier the newest fetched manifest lists is admitted from it when the refetch fails` (fails on the old code, checked).
- **Smaller fixes.** A heal ends with its job, failed as well as committed (a heal still scheduled for later is kept): `R-SYN-16: a heal whose re-download fails ends, and the engine forgets it`. A drained staged file is kept for any job of its transfer that still wants it and is not running or publishing (queued, parked, waiting for a retry), and `removeJob` discards a kept file nobody will publish (by reading only: a completion racing the engine's cancel cannot be produced with the fakes). `checkRunSettled` and `offerRunProgress` read the job table once instead of once per claim inside a closure (each read copied the whole engine state in debug and sanitizer builds), and `checkRunSettled` sorts the claims only once every one of them is parked. A REENTRANCY comment was added before `publicationFailed` in `publish`.
- **Requirement tests strengthened (reqs lens).** `R-SYN-8: reconcile is committed before the first transfer starts` holds the run's apply inside the store (its planning unit on the bulk executor, then its commit on the metadata executor) and requires that no transfer started meanwhile: it failed 3 of 3 runs under the mutant that dispatches the run's fetches before `apply` (checked in this round), and passes otherwise. `R-SYN-12: a stalled subscriber's backlog never exceeds one slot per progress key and subject during a 10,000-asset sync` samples the backlog through `awaitCondition` (reading the subscriber queues only) instead of `waitUntil`, whose introspection snapshot per mutation dominated the run; the same assertions hold. Measured in this round after the change: 4.0 s debug on a quiet host (8.8 s before), 17.3 s under the thread sanitizer with 0 reports (63 s before, per the verifier), 15.9 s on the iOS Simulator (52.8 s before); the time limit stays 3 minutes, now with about ten times the sanitizer figure as headroom (F-17).
- **Pre-existing WP-F tests changed by this round** (none is a CDN test, so no `MIGRATION.md` entry): `R-SYN-4: a resolve that joins a job blocked for lack of space is answered at once, as a new job would be` and `R-SYN-11: a fetch failing with ENOSPC and nothing to reclaim fails its waiter with the store's figures` now run on a volume that cannot hold what the ENOSPC asks for (on a roomy volume the failure is now retried, so "as a new job would be" means a download); `R-SYN-11: a publication that runs out of space fails a user request with insufficientSpace` scripts the second attempt the interactive policy makes and drives the clock through its backoff (and checks the two starts). The requirement each checks is unchanged.
- **Declined or left open, with reasons.** (1) R-APL-3 end to end: waits for the orchestrator's ruling (contract change request 1). (2) R-SYN-15 S1-09, an engine-side assertion that tells the engine's join-before-close order apart: not added. The verifier's mutants M5/M6 pass because `close()` drains in-flight work, so the file-system record cannot tell the orders apart in this scenario (and, by reading, neither could a check on the reopened store); in it, no `apply` is in flight at shutdown and the pruned blob's deletion is due work, not a unit in flight. (3) F-19's known issue (a run re-downloads an asset another interest committed between its planning view and its materialisation): kept open; the store deduplicates the blob. (4) Per-file coverage margins and P-11's margin: re-measured in this round, no change needed: ZTronAssetSync 93.23 % (6111/6555); per file EngineFailures 90.53, EngineRuns 90.91, EngineLifecycle 91.07, EngineCommands 91.70, EngineDispatch 91.70, EngineJobs 91.77, EngineTimers 92.55, EngineCatchUp 93.10, every other `Engine/` file above 93 %. Release benchmarks on a host at load average 3.7 to 9: P-4 11.49 ms (budget 20.0), ratio 2.03 (max 2.5); P-7 1.5 MiB for 256 MiB and 0.14 MiB for 1,024 MiB (max 48.0); P-11 speedup 3.71 (min 3.0); P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled-prune benchmark ratio 1.50 (max 2). (5) P-13 and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage figure belong to WP-B and WP-G.

## Orchestrator rulings (close-out), Level 4

Recorded from `.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` (authoritative over every draft above); each says how WP-F applied it in close-out cycle 1 (F-21), and, where cycle 2 or cycle 3 touched it, in that cycle (F-22, F-23). Cycle 11 received four new rulings, L4-R16…R19 (the close-out continuation of 2026-10-16, read at the start of cycle 11; L4-R1…R15 unchanged), recorded below and applied in F-31. No new ruling arrived for cycle 2, cycle 3, cycle 4, cycle 5, cycle 6, cycle 7, cycle 8, cycle 9 or cycle 10 (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` read again at the start of cycle 10: L4-R1…R15 unchanged, each recorded below as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"; F-30 says how cycle 10 touched L4-R4, L4-R5, L4-R8, L4-R11, L4-R12, L4-R13 and L4-R15; F-29 says how cycle 9 touched L4-R4, L4-R5, L4-R8, L4-R11, L4-R12, L4-R13 and L4-R15; F-28 how cycle 8 touched L4-R4, L4-R5, L4-R8, L4-R11, L4-R12 and L4-R13; F-24 says how cycle 4 touched L4-R5, L4-R7, L4-R8 and L4-R10, F-25 how cycle 5 touched L4-R4, L4-R5, L4-R7, L4-R8, L4-R10 and L4-R15, F-26 how cycle 6 touched L4-R4, L4-R5, L4-R8, L4-R12, L4-R13 and L4-R15). The rulings cycle 6 did not touch (L4-R1, R2, R3, R6, R7, R9, R10, R11, R14) stand as applied below; every test that pins them passed in this cycle's runs (F-26). No new ruling arrived for cycles 12, 13, 14, 15 or 16: `.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` was read again at the start of cycle 16 (L4-R1…R19 unchanged, each recorded below as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"); F-34 says how cycle 14 touched L4-R8, L4-R11, L4-R12, L4-R13 and L4-R17, F-35 how cycle 15 touched the same five, and F-36 how cycle 16 touched them again.

- **L4-R1 / CCR-1 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** R-APL-3 end to end, approved; `Package.swift` was changed by the orchestrator in `7cf647c` (test-only `ZTronAssetAppleHosted` for `ZTronAssetSyncTests`; Sync's production dependencies stay Model and Storage). WP-F writes its own pack-backend fake and the test under the exact §11 name, gated per test (`@available(macOS 26.0, iOS 26.0, *)`). *Applied:* `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/FakePackBackend.swift` (a subset of WP-E's fake; header names the source and ADR-040) and `R-APL-3: an asset adopted from a FakePackBackend source is served from blobs/ after its pack is released` (`EngineAppleTierTests`), commit `817b4d0`; it fails under mutant M-APL (a resolve that serves the pack path). Closes L4-V3-02 and L4-V3-11.
- **L4-R2 / F-15 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** Approved as decided: a run whose manifest fetch passed `manifestFetchDeadline` ends `.complete` with `manifestFresh == false`, writes no stamp and leaves the sync due; the docs level amends ARCHITECTURE §7.10. *Applied:* no change (F-15 stands).
- **L4-R3 / F-16 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** Approved as decided: one debounced retry of an offline run without a network edge, then edge-only; one bounded retry, not polling (R-SYN-13 holds); the docs level records it in §7.6/§7.10. *Applied:* no change (F-16 stands).
- **L4-R4 / F-20 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** ENOSPC on a roomy volume is retried with backoff, on condition that the backoff is bounded and joins the ordinary retry budget. *Applied:* the bound already exists (`spaceFailed` → `retryLater`, which counts `Job.attempts` against each interest's `maxAttemptsPerRun` and ends the job when no interest is left); pinned by `R-SYN-11: a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while it reads roomy ends in insufficientSpace once the retry budget is spent` (resolve: interactive budget 2; run: background budget 4; no start past the budget), commit `a2e6c23`. The docs level amends the §7.6 `insufficientSpace` row (L4-V3-05). *Cycle 2:* the figures of every `insufficientSpace` the engine reports follow one convention, the bytes needed against the room the decision had above the reserve (F-22), for the same §7.6 row. *Cycle 3:* the convention's third site (a job the ledger parks without an ENOSPC) is pinned by a test that fails under mutant M-JOBS (F-23), and the contract doc comment `ErrorMapping.swift:50-51` joins the docs level's amendments. *Cycle 5 (F-25):* the convention reaches two more sites: an `ensureAvailable` whose member was failed for lack of space answers that `insufficientSpace` (not `.success` with the member missing), and a job that would wait `awaitingSpace` while nothing will run the store's due work is parked `insufficientSpace` with the same figures. *Cycle 6 (F-26):* that last site decides on a fresh free-space read, never on the engine's last one: a job the volume holds by then is re-queued, and one still short is parked `insufficientSpace` with the bytes it needs against the room that read leaves it (an earlier ENOSPC's `required` kept, its `available` re-computed), so the figures follow F-22's convention at the time of the decision. *Cycle 7 (F-27):* the condition did not hold for an ENOSPC parked `awaitingSpace` (pending reclaim): it counted no attempt, and since cycle 6 the settlement's fresh read re-queues at once a job the volume holds, so with bytes the store had not scheduled to reclaim a volume that kept answering ENOSPC while it read roomy restarted the transfer without end (PROBE-C6R-1: 503 starts for 500 failures). Every such attempt now counts toward the same budget as `retryLater` (`countFailedAttempt`, shared): interests at their lane's cap get the figures, and the job ends when none is left. The wait for the reclaim (or for the settlement's read) stands where the backoff would; pinned for a resolve, an ensure and a run by `R-SYN-11: a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while the store holds bytes it has not scheduled to reclaim ends in insufficientSpace once the retry budget is spent` (`8bb89f1`, fix `eb792f3`). *Cycle 8 (F-28):* both cycle-7 verifiers confirm the bound (2 starts for a resolve or an ensure, 4 for a run, then a typed `insufficientSpace`) and note, as F-27 records, that an ENOSPC parked `awaitingSpace` whose bytes nothing reclaims is retried without a timed backoff: the settlement's fresh read re-queues it at once, so its attempts run back to back within `maxAttemptsPerRun` (PROBE-C6R-1 at `02fb6c2`: 2 starts, 0.0 s of manual time, then `insufficientSpace`). No WP-F change: the orchestrator either acknowledges this in ADR-053 and the amended §7.6 `insufficientSpace` row, or rules option (c) of F-27 (a backoff deadline for the settlement's re-queue of a job whose last error came from an ENOSPC, which needs a new transition or deadline kind). *Cycle 9 (F-29):* unchanged; both cycle-8 verifiers report it again as a non-blocking note for the orchestrator. *Cycle 10 (F-30):* unchanged; both cycle-9 verifiers report it again as a non-blocking note (a recorded design choice): the orchestrator acknowledges it in ADR-053 or rules F-27's option (c).
- **L4-R5 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** No job for an id the store holds present at its entry's current content (runs, `ensureAvailable` materialisation, `followApplied`/`reroute`, joins), fed from what the engine learns (apply reuses and relocations, commits since a plan, a presence read where neither is enough); such ids are answered from the store; a failed transfer never marks a present record failed (engine re-check before `recordFailure`; WP-F authorised to add a store guard if the window cannot be closed engine-side); the late-joiner second download (L4-V3-12, "G") is accepted residue for Stage 3 (OI-15). *Applied:* F-21 "Present assets get no job"; commits `3fc568d` (failing tests), `bf46fad`, `3de75ec`. Two deviations, each with its reason in F-21: relocations do not feed the ledger (the store relocates absent records too), and the store guard is **not** added (it contradicts WP-C's pre-existing test of `recordFailure`; evidence in F-21). *Cycle 2:* the ledger is dropped when a store mutation throws (PROBE-C1-N1, failing test `40f408d`, fix `76b9698`); the store guard is still not added: the orchestrator has not ruled on F-21's question, so the one-await window before `recordFailure` stays accepted residue and is recorded as such (F-22). *Cycle 3 (F-23):* a dropped ledger is unknown, not absent: every attempt asks the store, per id, before it asks the source for bytes, and answers present ids from the store (PROBE-C2-2, -4, -6; failing tests `d1560c3`, fix `26e77f3`), so "never creates a job" holds as "never downloads": a job may exist for one read per id; the integrity path re-checks presence before `recordIntegrityFailure` as the 404 path does (PROBE-C2-3 and an integrity twin of the 404 test; fix `82beef0`); `reportUnreadable` forgets, announces and heals every holder of the blob (PROBE-C2-7; fixes `44dfaa8`, `25fd9f7`); bindings learned from a read, a commit or an apply are recorded only when nothing was forgotten meanwhile (`5a53605`). The store guard is still not added (no ruling). *Cycle 5:* unchanged: still no ruling on F-21's question, so the store guard is not added and the shared one-await window stays recorded residue (F-14, F-22); L4-R5's "never creates a job" read as "never downloads" after a dropped ledger stays listed for ADR-053 (paragraph above F-1). The binding-ledger test `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable downloads a member that an apply evicted before it threw` no longer depends on the store's reclaim timing (F-25; mutant M-N1 still fails it). *Cycle 6:* unchanged: still no ruling on F-21's question; the store guard is not added, the one-await window shared by the 404, integrity and pre-transfer reads, "never creates a job" read as "never downloads" after a dropped ledger, and a holder gained between `blobHolders` and the quarantine stay recorded residue for ADR-053 and VERIFICATION "Not verified" (F-14, F-22, F-23, paragraph above F-1). *Cycles 7 and 8:* unchanged (no cycle-7 or cycle-8 commit touches those paths; both verifiers report them again as carried residue without a reproduction). *Cycle 9 (F-29):* unchanged; carried residue, reported again without a reproduction. The bounded release of a prefetch handle (F-29) keeps the member jobs of a released group from starting while only that group wants them, and no path of L4-R5 changed. *Cycle 10 (F-30):* unchanged; carried residue, reported again by both cycle-9 verifiers without a reproduction (the requirements verifier also examined a reroute meeting a released token: `reroute` drops the released token's interest, since `groups[token]` is nil, so no interest leaks). No path of L4-R5 changed. *Cycle 11 (F-31):* the store guard of the second bullet is added under L4-R16 (`recordFailure` leaves a record present at the failed content unchanged; failing test `7cc8e94`, fix `fa7f160`); the presence read before it (f-10) stays.
- **L4-R6 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** `state.freeSpace` keeps the measured reading (only the planner and the ensure's own space decision see the important-for-opportunistic copy); an ENOSPC attempt discards its partial or keeps it through `resumePoint(after:)`. *Applied:* commits `22a6aa0` (failing tests PROBE-R3-1, PROBE-R3-4) and `439a8ac`.
- **L4-R7 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** The two suspicions by reading: probe first; otherwise record why and make the code obviously correct. *Applied:* L4-V3-14 reproduced (store `apply` the engine did not make; failing test `2c88268`) and fixed (`cc2e3d6`); L4-V3-13 could not be produced deterministically (F-21) and the read is now adopted in the turn it returns, keeping the commits counted since the read began (`cc2e3d6`). *Cycle 3:* the cycle-2 verifier's suspicion by reading (a catch-up adopting a view older than an apply that landed during its read) could not be produced (no seam orders the two continuations) and is made correct by construction: every store view is followed through `followStoreView`, which refuses a view read while an `apply` was in flight or before a change of the copy, and a re-read of the store is owed wherever a view was refused (`5a53605`, `73d20b6`; pinned at the function level, F-23). *Cycle 5 (F-25):* the cycle-4 suspicion by reading (two committed applies followed out of commit order) was produced by the cycle-4 verifier; failing test first (`41f2c7c`, through the test seam `8d040a0`), fixed by sending one `apply` at a time to the store and the next only after the engine followed it (`cb0fb44`).
- **L4-R8 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** Deterministic or cheaper tests, never a weaker assertion; a `.timeLimit` rises only with a cost reduction, and the test's in-suite sanitizer time at a load around 25 must stay at or below half its limit (figures in F-17). *Applied:* commit `f21bd87` (F-21, F-17). *Cycle 2:* no test changed; the verifier's loaded figures for the 10,000-asset test are in F-17. *Cycle 3:* no test budget changed; the cycle-2 figures (143.15 s at a load of 14 to 25.5 under concurrent `swift test` runs, 59.6 % of the limit) and this cycle's (48.6 s at a load of 44.5 to 68.9 and 67.4 s at 41.8 to 95.1 beside the simulator runs, 20.3 % and 28.1 %) are in F-17. *Cycle 5 (F-25):* the two tests the cycle-4 correctness verifier saw fail (`R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable downloads a member that an apply evicted before it threw`, once in-suite and two hangs in 208 stressed runs; `R-SYN-12: after a run's apply throws past its in-memory writes, the engine follows the store and announces its prunes`, once in 208) no longer depend on the store's background work, with no assertion weakened (mutants M-N1 and M-SNAP fail them); the hang itself was an engine liveness defect, fixed with a failing test first (`52324a0`). Figures in F-17. *Cycle 6:* no test budget, count or assertion changed; the cycle-5 verifiers' figures for the 10,000-asset test (one in-suite sanitizer run at 249.198 s, 103.8 % of its 240 s limit, beside both verifiers' simulator runs at a 1-minute load of 7.3 to 195.5, median 125.6, with no "Time limit was exceeded" recorded, and 18.311 s at 4.5 to 6.5) and this cycle's are in F-17. The new cycle-6 tests make no timing assumption: the order the verifier forced with a 150 ms sleep inside the engine actor is produced by a store clock that runs ahead of the engine's (F-26). *Cycles 7 and 8:* no test budget, count or assertion changed; the cycle-7 verifiers' figures for the 10,000-asset test (no overrun) and this cycle's are in F-17. The cycle-8 Sync tests wait on engine state only (no clock, no sleep); the two new release benchmarks measure turn lengths by a heartbeat and read P-9's threshold from `QualityBudgets`. *Cycle 9 (F-29):* the two flakes the cycle-8 verifiers found are fixed with no assertion weakened and no `.timeLimit` changed: the cycle-6 clock driver (`EngineRegressionCycle6Tests.driving`) and `EngineHarness.withClock` now stop before they return (failing test `a087b48`, fix `e3b36f5`), and `R-SYN-5: a run whose transfers open the 5xx breaker ends blocked and the pending run's probe closes it` drives the clock only to the retry deadlines until its run has settled (`4ec2d06`); figures in F-17. *Cycle 10 (F-30):* no test budget, count or assertion changed and no run of either verifier recorded "Time limit was exceeded"; their figures for the 10,000-asset test and this cycle's are in F-17 (the requirements verifier's in-suite sanitizer run, 97.243 s or 40.5 % of the limit beside the other verifier's work, is under L4-R8's 50 % with the least margin). The cycle-10 Sync tests make no timing assumption: they order their events inside one actor turn (`readForTests`) or wait on engine state, never on a clock or a sleep, and the three new release benchmarks measure turn lengths by the heartbeat as the earlier ones do. *Cycle 11 (F-31, L4-R17):* both cycle-10 flakes fixed and the class enumerated and converted (the clock drivers count clock moves, wait on conditions, never wake an in-flight fetch's deadline or probe's watchdog, and count sleepers before reading the deadline; sixteen tests or helpers converted, the others reviewed); no assertion weakened, the progress test's count made exact; no `.timeLimit` changed. *Cycle 12 (F-32):* the one overrun of cycle 11's verifiers (R-STO-1's minute in the required sanitizer run) was the live-transport progress waits, a flake: their stalled prefixes now end on the transport's progress quantum; no assertion and no `.timeLimit` changed; figures in F-17. *Cycle 14 (F-34):* no overrun in any run of the cycle-13 verifiers or of this cycle; the model test's invariant flake under a slow store is fixed (F-34); no assertion and no `.timeLimit` changed; figures in F-17. *Cycle 15 (F-35):* the cycle-14 requirements verifier's overruns of the store-work test (5 of 69 stress iterations) and its hang of a whole test process (`syncDriven`) are flakes, fixed under L4-R17; no assertion weakened and no `.timeLimit` changed; figures in F-17. *Cycle 16 (F-36):* no committed test overran in any run of the cycle-15 verifiers or of this cycle; the new tests order the re-admission follow-up through a held seam instead of the probe's race, and no `.timeLimit` or assertion changed; figures in F-17. *Cycle 17 (F-37):* no committed test overran in any run of the cycle-16 verifiers or of this cycle; P-11 missed its minimum on loaded hosts in both verifiers' runs and on the pre-cycle-16 commit too, a load miss, not blocking; it passed here (3.457); no `.timeLimit` or assertion changed; figures in F-17.
- **L4-R9 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** `diagnostics()` answers while the engine is degraded or its store is unavailable (last-known generation, 0 if never read; last-known stamp or `nil`; no signature change); after `shutdown()` it fails `.shutDown`; `metrics()` unchanged; one `R-OBS-3:` test. *Applied:* failing test `a012012`, fix `335ab57`. *Cycle 2:* the last-known generation is never older than one the engine announced (failing test `a4545fe`, fix `b7a3602`).
- **L4-R10 — Orchestrator ruling (close-out).** A store manifest ahead of the engine after a failed index commit is by design (ADR-050); the engine follows the store through catch-up, made complete by L4-R7; no WP-B change. *Applied:* catch-up now announces what it follows (F-21). *Cycle 2:* after an `apply` that throws, the engine re-reads the store once no `apply` is in flight, so it follows such a manifest without waiting for a superseded publication (F-22). *Cycle 5:* catch-up unchanged; the engine's own applies are now followed in commit order (F-25), so a newer store manifest is never replaced by an older report.

## F-21 — Close-out cycle 1: round-3 findings, each with a failing test first, and the rulings applied
- **Context.** Verification round 3 of the main run left 17 findings (L4-V3-00…16, 8 blocking) and the orchestrator ruled on them (L4-R1…R15, above). Every defect below has a regression test committed before its fix (CLAUDE.md P3); every test is gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051).
- **Present assets get no job (L4-V3-07, L4-V3-08, F-19; L4-R5).** *Options.* (a) A presence read (an await) before each job is created: impossible where jobs are created, inside the synchronous `pump`/materialisation/reroute turns. (b) A "bound since the plan" set per run and per `ensureAvailable` group: covers materialisation only, not the reroutes of T22 nor joins. (c) One engine-side ledger of the bindings the engine learns, consulted wherever an interest would create or join a job. *Decision:* (c). `EngineState.boundContent` maps an id to the transfer whose content the store bound it to. It is fed by a commit's locations (`committed`), an apply's reuses (`applied`), and the store reads the engine already makes (a resolve's planning view, a point admission's view, a catch-up's view, a 404's location reads). An entry goes when the engine learns the binding ended or may have: an apply's prunes, evictions and content changes, a purge (at its start and when the store returns), maintenance (at its start and after its eviction step), a quarantine, a recorded integrity failure or 404, a store read that finds nothing, a catch-up's view that no longer shows it present. It is consulted by `materializeRun` (the run counts the asset as fetched), `materializeEnsures` (the member is done), `reroute` (a waiter is answered by a store read, a group drops the member, a run counts it as fetched, a heal is forgotten), `joinGroup` (skipped), a resolve's registration and a point admission's join (answered by a store read), and a heal (forgotten). A waiter answered from the store (`Waiter.fromStore`) holds no interest; an owned task reads `store.location` (RE-S36) and, when the read finds nothing (the binding ended meanwhile), puts it on the job path. *Lifetime:* the ledger is kept whole while a run exists (its plan is a snapshot), while an `ensureAvailable` group has fetches left to materialise, and while a store read whose answer registers an interest is in flight (`presenceReaders`: a resolve from its first read to its registration, a prefetch join, a point admission, `ensureAvailable` until its group exists); otherwise `reconcileIdle` trims it to the ids of jobs (`pruneBoundContent`). It therefore scales with outstanding work and a plan in progress, never with the catalog. *Deviation from the ruling's feed list:* an apply's `relocated` ids are not fed, because `AssetStore+Apply` relocates absent records too (`diff.metadataChanged`): feeding them would make a run skip fetching an absent asset. A relocation keeps the content (and so the transfer), so no job moves for it and nothing is downloaded twice. *Defence in depth (404):* before `recordFailure`, one `store.location` read per id of the job (f-10; O(1) each, where a planning view would scan the blob table on the store's actor, C2): an id the store serves at its current content while the engine's copy maps it to the failed content is answered from the store and left out of the failure; the failure is recorded for the job's other ids even when the job ended during the reads (ADR-032). *Store guard not added:* applied temporarily in this worktree (`record.referencedBlob == nil || record.isStale` in `AssetStore.recordFailure`'s guard), it fails WP-C's pre-existing `R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need`, case `failure` (EvictionOverlapTests.swift:213, :216, :218): that test records a 404 for a present record and expects its blob doomed. The ruling authorises the guard and a new store test, not a change to WP-C's test, so the guard was reverted. The remaining window is one await between a location read and `recordFailure`'s write, in which only a legacy adoption (`migrateLegacy`, `adoptLegacyNow`) or an apply's adoption of an orphaned blob of the same content can bind the id: the job is that content's only download (ADR-020). **Question for the orchestrator:** rule whether WP-C's test case may target a stale record instead, so the guard can go in. *Tests (all five failed first, `3fc568d`; fixes `bf46fad`, `3de75ec`):* `R-SYN-4: a waiter whose asset an apply binds to content already present is answered from the store and downloads nothing` (PROBE-R3-3), `R-NET-6: a 404 for content an apply already bound never fails the asset that holds it` (PROBE-R3-3b), `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable does not download a member another interest committed after its plan` (PROBE-R3-2), `R-SYN-6: a run does not download an asset another interest committed after its plan, and counts it as fetched` (F-19, closed), `R-NET-6: a 404 never marks failed an asset the store bound to that content while the transfer ran` (the presence re-check). The resolve and prefetch-join windows (a commit the engine processes between a store read and the registration) have no deterministic probe with the fakes (the reads have no executor unit to hold); they use the same ledger check. *Accepted residue (L4-R5, Stage 3 with OI-15):* the late joiner of shared content (L4-V3-12, "G") still downloads it a second time.
- **Space and staging (L4-V3-09, L4-V3-10; L4-R6).** `ensureAvailable` plans against important capacity but its measured read is the ledger's base; an attempt that fails with ENOSPC goes through `resumePoint(after:)` (a sidecar keeps the partial for resume, anything else is discarded) before the free-space decision. Tests (failed first, `22a6aa0`; fix `439a8ac`): `R-SYN-11: ensureAvailable's plan against important capacity never admits a prefetch parked for opportunistic space` (PROBE-R3-1), `R-CLN-3: an attempt that fails for lack of space leaves no partial without a sidecar in staging` (PROBE-R3-4).
- **Catch-up announces what it follows (L4-V3-14; L4-R7, L4-R10).** Reproduced with a store `apply` the engine did not make (what an `apply` that throws after its in-memory writes leaves): the engine followed the store's manifest but never announced the prune. `catchUpWithStore` now publishes `assetRemoved(.pruned)` for the ids its copy lists and the view does not (in the copy's order), takes the view's generation and publishes `snapshotChanged`, then follows (T21/T22). The engine's ETag is kept (the view carries none). Test (failed first, `2c88268`; fix `cc2e3d6`): `R-SYN-10: a catch-up with the store's manifest announces the ids it pruned and the store's generation`.
- **Free-space read adopted in its own turn (L4-V3-13; L4-R7).** Not produced: the window lies between the store's measuring unit and the engine turn that adopts the reading (before this cycle it also spanned the planning-view await). No executor unit sits there to hold (`planningView` has none), the result crosses two actor hops, and `FakeFileSystem` counts placed bytes in its capacity at once, so no fake orders a commit into that window deterministically. Made correct by construction (`cc2e3d6`): `readFreeSpace()` adopts the reading in the turn it returns, and the ledger keeps the bytes committed since the read began (`EngineState.committedTotal`): a commit the read may already include is counted twice (understating headroom), never dropped.
- **Deterministic and cheaper tests (L4-V3-00, -01, -03, -15; L4-R8; `f21bd87`).** `R-SYN-12: transfer progress published to subscribers never exceeds 10 Hz per asset under ManualTimeSource` waits, before each round and before the final release, until the collector has pulled everything published (`liveCount == 0`): no emission is coalesced into a later one because the collector was not scheduled, so the lower bound (≥ 2) no longer depends on scheduling; the ceiling is unchanged. `R-SYN-6: a pending run whose gate opens while the blocked run is still finishing starts once it has finished` forces each order once instead of racing 500 iterations: the debounce (1 s) keeps the real alarm asleep on the manual clock; in `gateFirst` a condition wait fires the pending run's deadline (`pendingRunDeadlineFired`, now the alarm's own branch) in the actor turn that settles the run, before the run's task can finish it; in `runFirst` the clock passes the debounce after the run finished. Under mutant M-F3 (F-3's `reconsiderPendingRun()` removed from `finishRun`) `gateFirst` fails deterministically ("the pending run's conditions gate is open but no run started"); `runFirst` passes. `R-SYN-12: a stalled subscriber's backlog never exceeds one slot per progress key and subject during a 10,000-asset sync` reads the queue's high-water mark (`SubscriberQueue.peakLiveCount`, updated at every publication) instead of sampling the backlog after each mutation, which also sees peaks within one actor turn. Its cost is cut in the engine: `pump()` reads its slot figures in one pass per round and counts a start in place (each figure was its own pass, re-evaluated in every loop condition), and `checkRunSettled` stops at the first claimed job still working for the run (the orphan pass runs once no claimed job works). The profile under the thread sanitizer (`sample`, this test alone) showed `pump`'s figure passes at 309 of 3,120 engine-task samples and the orphan filter at 170. Measured alone, load average about 2 to 5: thread sanitizer 17.8 s before, 15.1 s after (−15 %); debug 4.2 s before, 3.8 to 3.9 s after. The time limit rises from 3 to 4 minutes together with that reduction (F-17 has the in-suite figures).
- **Diagnostics while degraded (L4-R9).** `performDiagnostics` answers a degraded engine (and a store whose planning view throws) from the engine's state, with the last-known index generation and stamp (`EngineState.lastIndexGeneration`/`lastStamp`, from the open report, every view diagnostics reads, the stamps the engine writes; a purge clears the stamp); `.shutDown` after shutdown, `.cancelled` for a caller cancelled during start. Test (failed first, `a012012`; fix `335ab57`): `R-OBS-3: diagnostics answer while the engine is degraded, with its error ring and the last-known generation and stamp`.
- **ENOSPC retry bound (L4-R4).** Already bounded (see the ruling); test `a2e6c23`.
- **R-APL-3 end to end (L4-R1).** See the ruling; mutant M-APL (the resolve's fast path answers an Apple-tier asset with `/packs/<remotePath>`) fails the test at the `blobs/` and the "never a pack location" expectations.
- **Timing assumptions found by the simulator run (L4-R8).** The first iOS Simulator run of this cycle (load average 79 to 141, this WP's sanitizer run beside it) failed 1 of 1,008: `R-CLN-6: purge ends the active run with failed(cancelled)` saw one cancel in the control log instead of two. A withdrawal whose job has no handle yet leaves the cancel to the driver once `remote.transfer` returns, after the job is gone, so the test saw the second cancel only by timing. It and two tests with the same assumption (`R-SYN-10: cancelling ensureAvailable cancels transfers wanted by nobody else and throws cancelled`, `R-SYN-4: the transfer is cancelled only after the last of 100 callers cancels`) now wait for the drivers' handles first (`waitForAllHandles`); no assertion changed (`b281a54`; WP-F's own tests, L4-R15). The second run passed 1,008 of 1,008.
- **More behaviour tests for L4-R5 (`6c64fb1`).** `R-CAR-3: a prefetch member whose asset an apply binds to content already present is done, and nothing is downloaded again` and `R-SYN-6: a run whose planned asset another apply binds to content already present counts it as fetched and downloads nothing` fail under mutant M-R5 (the reroute's binding check removed), as do PROBE-R3-3 and PROBE-R3-3b's tests (the latter on its start count only: the 404 presence re-check alone still keeps x present, so the defence in depth holds by itself); `R-SYN-4: a binding the engine remembers but the store no longer holds sends the waiter down the job path, never a wrong answer` pins the store read's fallback (a test seam plants the stale binding).
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`6c64fb1`; run in this session).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 217 tests). Release build with warnings as errors: complete. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS … fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 217 of 217 each (3.9 s); under the thread sanitizer once: 217 of 217, 0 reports, 16.8 s (the 10,000-asset test 16.8 s, load average 3 to 5). Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.28% (6430/6893) OK` (every `Engine/` file at or above 90 %: EngineRuns 90.70, EngineJobs 91.04, EngineFailures 91.46, EngineLifecycle 91.46, EngineDispatch 91.50, EngineCommands 92.15, EnginePresence 92.19); the gate's overall `FAIL` is `ZTronAssetKit 37.82%` (WP-G, L4-R11). iOS device build with warnings as errors: succeeded. iOS 27 Simulator (`897F9D8D-…`, load average 4 to 69): 1,011 of 1,011 passed. Release benchmarks (load average 2.4 to 4.5): P-4 11.56 ms (budget 20.0), ratio 2.008 (max 2.5); P-7 1.42 MiB for 256 MiB and 0.20 MiB for 1,024 MiB (max 48.0); P-11 speedup 3.68 (min 3.0); P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled-prune benchmark ratio 1.45 (max 2). P-13 (WP-B) failed again (`unit max 8.399833 ms … 5.0 ms per batch`), `outside-wp` (L4-R11).
- **Observed outside WP-F (`outside-wp`, not changed).** In debug and sanitizer builds `AssetStore.gainReferenceInPlace` (WP-B, `Sources/ZTronAssetStorage/Store/AssetStore+References.swift:76-86`) copies the whole blob table on each publication (`_NativeDictionary.copy()` under it in 648 of 3,120 engine-task samples of the 10,000-asset test under the thread sanitizer; the test's publications are O(n) each there, O(n²) over the sync). Whether a release build copies too was not checked (no release profile was taken). The 10,000-asset test's remaining sanitizer time is mostly the store's publication path (about half) and `FakeFileSystem` (WP-H).
- **Dispositions of the round-3 findings.**

## F-22 — Close-out cycle 2: the cycle-1 verifiers' findings, each with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 1 (requirements and correctness lenses) left eleven findings, one blocking. No new orchestrator ruling arrived; L4-R1 to L4-R15 stand. Every new test is gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051) and lives in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCloseOutTests.swift`.
- **The binding ledger after a store mutation that throws (blocking; L4-R5, L4-R10, ADR-050).** *Reproduced* (PROBE-C1-N1, deterministic): with `minimumResidency: 0`, a run holding `h` mid-transfer keeps the ledger whole after it committed `z`; `ensureAvailable([w])` plans an eviction of `z`, the store's `apply` evicts `z` in memory and its index commit then fails; the ledger still binds `z`, so `ensureAvailable([z])` counted `z` done without a job and returned `present: 0, missing: 1`. The ledger was fed and pruned only on the success path. *Options.* (a) Drop the ledger in the failing turn; (b) only schedule a catch-up, which filters the ledger against a view, but only when the store's manifest differs (an eviction leaves it equal) and only after an await, during which the stale entry is still consulted; (c) both. *Decision:* (c). `applyAndFollow`'s failure branch (any `StoreError`, `.cancelled` from the commit-window wait included) drops the whole ledger in the turn it returns (`storeMutationFailed`: an interest it would have answered creates a job, which the store's state then decides) and sets `catchUpAfterFailedApply`; the last `apply` in flight to end then re-reads the store (`catchUpWithStore(afterFailedApply: true)`), following a manifest the store wrote in memory as L4-R7 made catch-up do (its prunes announced, its generation taken) and re-reading the store's next deadline. The same invalidation runs on every other store mutation that can throw past its in-memory writes: a purge's and a maintenance step's throwing paths drop the whole ledger, a `reportUnreadable` that throws forgets its id, and the ids of `recordFailure` and `recordIntegrityFailure` are forgotten again after the await, thrown or not. *Found while fixing:* with the ledger fixed, `z`'s new job parked `awaitingSpace` and was never woken: only a committed `apply` refreshed the heap's store entry, so the reap of the blob the failed `apply` doomed never ran (the test hit its time limit); the catch-up's deadline re-read fixes it. And a run's `apply` that wrote a new manifest and threw left the engine's copy behind the store's until some later publication was superseded, so its prunes were never announced (a re-apply prunes nothing). *Tests:* `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable downloads a member that an apply evicted before it threw` (failed first, `40f408d`; fix `76b9698`); `R-SYN-12: after a run's apply throws past its in-memory writes, the engine follows the store and announces its prunes` (`0fde299`, written after the fix; tagged R-SYN-10 until close-out cycle 3, F-23). *Mutants:* M-LEDGER (the drop removed from `applyAndFollow`) fails the first test deterministically; M-CATCHUP (the flag never set) fails the second at its expectations and the first at its time limit. *Not produced:* the cancellation trigger (PROBE-C1-N2 did not reach the state) and the purge, maintenance and quarantine triggers: the binding must be learned inside the mutation's own awaits, which needs an executor schedule across several store calls; the same call on each throwing path makes them correct by construction (F-14).
- **A 404 whose presence reads overlap shutdown (non-blocking, by reading).** The failure of the absent ids is now persisted before the lifecycle guard, as before cycle 1 (the store refuses the write once it closed; a refusal after shutdown began is not reported to the error ring); only the engine-state updates wait for the guard. Not produced: no executor unit sits between the per-id reads and the guard. Commit `76b9698`.
- **The store guard and the 404's residual window (L4-R5, second bullet; F-21's question).** The orchestrator has not ruled on F-21's question. The authorised guard (a record present at `identity` is left unchanged) breaks WP-C's pre-existing `R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need` case `failure`, which records a 404 against a present record at its own identity and expects its blob doomed (`Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/EvictionOverlapTests.swift:184-218`; the verifier confirmed the failure at `:213`, `:216`, `:218`). Changing WP-C's test is not authorised. *Safest default:* no store edit; the one-await window between the last presence read and `recordFailure` stays accepted residue beside L4-V3-12 "G" (no probe produced it: no executor unit sits there), and it can no longer leave a stale binding (the ids are forgotten after the await). The question stays open: either WP-C's case targets a stale record and WP-F adds the guard with a failing store test, or the residue is recorded in DECISIONS/VERIFICATION at merge. *Cycle 11 (F-31, L4-R16):* the guard was added, so the store side of the window is closed for 404s. *Cycle 12 (F-32):* the waiter side is not: a waiter of the failed job for an id bound during the await is still answered `notFound` while the store serves the id (F-14); the code comment at the `forgetBound` call says so since `205e5f7`.
- **Diagnostics' last-known generation (L4-R9).** `makeDiagnostics` reports the newer of the last-read and the engine's announced generation, so a store that stops answering after the engine applied manifests never makes diagnostics older than the events subscribers saw. Test `R-OBS-3: diagnostics never report a generation older than the engine announced when the store cannot answer` (failed first, `a4545fe`: announced 2, diagnosed 1; fix `b7a3602`).
- **One convention for `insufficientSpace(required:available:)` (L4-R4's §7.6 row).** The engine's ENOSPC path reported the raw volume, reserve included (PROBE-C1-10a: `required: 300, available: 268435656`), and the planner added the reserve to `required` instead (and, over budget, reported the volume, so `required: 1_500, available: 5_000`). *Options.* (a) The planner's convention (`required` + reserve against the raw volume): the ledger reads a parked job's `required` back as its size (`spaceBlock`), and the budget case still read `required` < `available`; (b) the bytes needed against the room the decision had above the reserve. *Decision:* (b), §7.10's `available`: the planner reports its known bytes against `room` (the volume above the reserve plus pending reclaim, within the budget); the engine reports the attempt's bytes against the job's ledger headroom (`headroom(for:in:)`: its lane's volume above the reserve, less the other jobs in flight and the bytes committed since the read), so `required` exceeds `available` whenever space decided. The docs level amends the §7.6 row and §7.10. Tests: `R-SYN-11: an ENOSPC failure reports the bytes it needed and the room above the reserve, which is smaller` (failed first, `bea8924`; fix `77ba454`); the expected verdict of `R-SYN-11: a catalog larger than the budget syncs its largest fitting prefix essential-first` changed from `(required: 1_500, available: 5_000)` to `(required: 1_400, available: 1_000)` in the same failing commit (this level's own test, L4-R15: equally specific, the requirement unchanged). *Cycle 3:* the third site (`failFast` for a job the ledger parks without an ENOSPC, `EngineJobs.swift`) is pinned by `R-SYN-11: a waiter of a job the ledger parks for lack of space gets the bytes it needs and the room above the reserve`, which fails under mutant M-JOBS (`available` read from the raw volume); the contract doc comment `Sources/ZTronAssetModel/Errors/ErrorMapping.swift:50-51` ("carries the store's measured free space") contradicts the convention and joins §7.6 and §7.10 in what the docs level amends (WP-F does not edit WP-A's file).
- **The pack-backend fake's wait (test robustness).** `FakePackBackend.waitFor` resumes with `false` when its task is cancelled (a waiter cancelled before it registers is resumed at registration), and the R-APL-3 test requires the release it waits for. Under mutant MA1 (the engine never reports adoption to the source) the test now records its time limit and the failed requirement and the process ends (67.5 s); before, it never returned. Commit `2f74c56`.
- **Not changed.** The resolve and prefetch-join windows (mutants M-RESOLVEREG, M-JOIN): no deterministic seam (F-14). The 10,000-asset test's loaded figures: recorded in F-17. `outside-wp` (L4-R11): P-13 and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`77ba454`; run in this session).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 221 tests). Release build with warnings as errors: complete. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 221 of 221 each (3.9 s; load average 3.2 to 5.5); under the thread sanitizer once: 221 of 221, 0 reports, 16.9 s (the 10,000-asset test 16.9 s; load 4.6 to 2.9). Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.29% (6481/6947) OK`; the gate's one failure is `ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542)`, `outside-wp`. iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): exit 0. iOS 27 Simulator (`897F9D8D-…`): 1,015 of 1,015 passed (result bundle `Test-ZTronAssetKit-2026.10.15_19-56-29-+0200.xcresult`; the 10,000-asset test 12.8 s; load average up to 79.8). Release benchmarks: P-4, P-7, P-11, P-14 pass; P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed with its longest deletion unit at 11.3 ms against 5 ms, load average 16.2 falling to 2.9 (the simulator run had just ended).
- **Dispositions of the cycle-1 findings.**

| Finding | Blocking | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| correct: the ledger stays stale after a store mutation that throws (PROBE-C1-N1) | yes | Fixed: failing test `40f408d`, fix `76b9698`; also the deadline re-read and the catch-up after a failed `apply` (`0fde299`). |
| correct: the 404 failure not persisted when shutdown begins during the reads | no | Fixed by construction (`76b9698`); not produced. |
| correct: diagnostics' generation lags what the engine announced | no | Fixed: failing test `a4545fe`, fix `b7a3602`. |
| correct: `insufficientSpace` figures use two conventions | no | Fixed: failing tests `bea8924`, fix `77ba454`; one convention recorded for §7.6/§7.10. |
| correct: the pack-backend fake's wait ignores cancellation | no | Fixed (`2f74c56`); mutant MA1 now fails cleanly. |
| correct and reqs: the store guard and the 404's residual window | no | Not changed: the orchestrator's ruling on F-21's question is outstanding; accepted residue, no stale binding left (F-22, F-14). |
| reqs: the resolve and prefetch-join ledger checks have no test | no | Not changed: no deterministic seam (F-14). |
| reqs: the 10,000-asset test slows sharply under extreme load | no | Figures recorded in F-17; no change. |
| reqs and correct: P-13 and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target | no | `outside-wp` (L4-R11). |

## F-23 — Close-out cycle 3: the cycle-2 verifiers' findings, each blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 2 left three blocking findings (correctness lens, each with a probe that failed 5 of 5 runs) and several non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`: L4-R1…R15 unchanged). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle3Tests.swift`, each gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051); all of them failed on the unfixed engine (`d1560c3`: 5 tests, 10 of 10 cases), and each fix was checked against the mutant that removes it (listed below).
- **A dropped ledger is unknown, not absent (blocking; L4-R5, ARCHITECTURE §7.1; PROBE-C2-2, -4, -6).** *Reproduced:* after the engine dropped its whole binding ledger (an unrelated `ensureAvailable` whose `apply` failed its index commit, or a successful `performMaintenance`) while a run still had `x` to materialise, `materializeRun` found no binding for `x`, which the store held present at its content throughout, created a job and downloaded it again (x starts 2; the default tuning with a 40-asset catalog the same). *Options.* (a) Mark the ledger unverified on every whole drop, hold `materializeRun`/`materializeEnsures` until a scheduled store read re-notes the run's and groups' remaining fetches and the jobs' assets: covers materialisation only, stalls a run for the length of a maintenance pass, and the re-read itself can land after a forget it predates. (b) Before an attempt asks the source for bytes, read each id's location (O(1), no planning view) and answer the ids the store serves at their current content. *Decision:* (b), for every attempt that downloads (`drive` → `answeredFromStore`, REENTRANCY f-11): the ids the engine's copy maps to the content and the store serves at a location that is not stale are answered from the store (`answerPresentMembers`: a waiter gets a store read, a group member is done, the run counts the asset fetched); an attempt left with no interest is marked terminal (it never had a stream, so nothing drains) and retired, which frees its slot and records the breaker's `abandoned` for a half-open probe. Transfer requests (`transferRequests`, `resolveTransfers`) are now counted where the request is sent, so an attempt answered from the store counts none. *Consequences.* L4-R5's "never creates a job for an id the store holds present" holds as "never downloads it": after a dropped ledger a job may exist for the length of one location read per id (the orchestrator acknowledges this at merge, see the paragraph above F-1). The ledger becomes an optimisation everywhere: the resolve registration's and the prefetch join's checks (M-RESOLVEREG, M-JOIN) no longer change what is downloaded (F-14). Each attempt costs one store hop more; measured below (debug 10,000-asset test 4.0 s, as before; sanitizer 17.4 s against 16.9 s). The residual window (a legacy or orphan adoption of the same content between the read and the transfer) is the 404 path's. *Tests:* `R-SYN-6: a run does not download an asset the store holds present after the engine dropped its binding ledger` (cases `failedApply`, `maintenance`), `R-SYN-6: with the default tuning, a run does not download an asset the store holds present after a failed apply dropped the ledger`. Fix `26e77f3`; mutant M-PRE (`answeredFromStore` never answers) fails all three cases.
- **A failed transfer never fails a present record (blocking; L4-R5 second bullet, R-INT-3; PROBE-C2-3).** *Reproduced:* the redundant job above, whose second download staged corrupt bytes of the same length, made `integrityFailed` record the failure against present `x`: with the default cap `x` kept serving but counted `integrity.attempts == 1` and the run ended `completeWithFailures`; with `maxIntegrityAttempts: 1` the store failed `x` and dropped its blob. *Decision:* the 404 path's per-id presence read is now one function (`sparePresent`, REENTRANCY f-10) used before `recordFailure` and before `recordIntegrityFailure` (transport `.integrity` and the store's publication hash check alike): present ids are answered from the store, left out of the bookkeeping and of the run's failures; only the absent ids are recorded and forgotten. *Tests:* `R-INT-3: a present asset is never counted, quarantined or failed by a redundant transfer of its content` (caps 1 and 3) and its deterministic twin `R-INT-3: an integrity failure never counts against an asset the store bound to that content while the transfer ran` (transport and publication sources, caps 1 and 3; the store binds `x` behind the engine's back while the transfer is held, as `R-NET-6: a 404 never marks failed an asset the store bound to that content while the transfer ran` does). Fix `82beef0`; mutant M-INT (`sparePresent` bypassed) fails all four twin cases; the first test also passes under M-PRE alone, because this fix by itself keeps `x` (defence in depth).
- **A quarantine reaches every holder of the blob (blocking; R-CLN-8; PROBE-C2-7).** *Reproduced:* `x` and `y` share one blob; `reportUnreadable("x")` made the store quarantine both, but the engine forgot, announced and healed only `x`; the run, reaching `y`, counted it fetched from the stale binding and ended `.complete` (stamp written) while `y` stayed quarantined and unserved; `y` got no `assetRemoved(.quarantined)` and no heal (pre-existing since `bf46fad`). *Options.* (a) Forget the ledger entries bound to `x`'s transfer: checksum-less siblings have other TransferIDs and so would stay bound, and two extensions of one checksum share a TransferID without sharing a blob. (b) A return value from the store's `reportUnreadable` naming the ids it quarantined: a contract change, not authorised. (c) Snapshot the blob's holders before the quarantine, drop the whole ledger, and after the quarantine announce and heal each holder the store's records show quarantined or failed. *Decision:* (c). `blobHolders` reads a planning view and scans its records for the reported id's blob off the actor (`holdersOffActor`, C2; REENTRANCY f-12); the ledger is dropped after every `reportUnreadable`, thrown or not, even when the snapshot could not be read (`25fd9f7`), so no holder can stay bound; deep verification still forgets exactly the holders the store returns. A holder gained between the snapshot and the quarantine is not announced or healed (it is re-fetched by the next run or resolve once its quarantine ends; no binding survives). *Test:* `R-CLN-8: reportUnreadable announces, forgets and heals every asset the store quarantined with the blob, and a run downloads them again` (events read after the run's `syncFinished`, so nothing waits on an event that may never come). Fix `44dfaa8`; mutant M-SIB (only the reported id handled) fails it at the binding, the heal, the announcement and the unserved `y`.
- **Store views older than the copy, and bindings older than a forget (non-blocking, by reading; L4-R7, L4-R10).** The verifier's suspicion: a catch-up's view read before an `apply` whose `applied` turn runs first is adopted, so the copy goes back, the ids the apply added are announced pruned and their jobs follow stale entries. Not produced (no seam orders the two continuations). The same class covered three silent adoptions (a resolve's, a prefetch join's and a point admission's view, adopted without announcements, so a later catch-up found nothing to announce) and every binding learned from a read or a mutation (a forget landing during it, then the stale binding recorded). *Decision, made correct by construction (`5a53605`, `73d20b6`):* every store view is followed through `followStoreView`, which refuses a view read while an `apply` was in flight (`appliesInFlight`) or before the copy changed (`copySerial`, bumped by `adoptApplied`) and otherwise does what a catch-up did (forget the bindings the view does not show present, announce prunes, take the generation, publish `snapshotChanged`, `followApplied`); a refused view owes a re-read of the store wherever it was read (a resolve's, a prefetch join's, an admission's or a catch-up's), started at once when no catch-up or `apply` is in flight and otherwise by the last one to end, so a refused view never leaves the copy behind the store (found while writing this cycle's tests: `5a53605` owed it only for a catch-up; failing test `cd816af`, fix `73d20b6`). A binding from a read, a commit's locations or an apply's reuses is recorded only when no binding was forgotten while that call was in flight (`ledgerForgets`, bumped by every forget and drop): a binding not recorded costs one read before a transfer, a stale one would skip an absent asset. *Tests (function level):* `R-SYN-12: a store view read before an apply the engine followed never takes the engine's copy back` (mutant M-VIEW, the guard removed, fails it) and `R-SYN-12: a store view refused as possibly older than the copy is read again, so the engine still follows the store` (the store holds a manifest the engine never applied; mutant M-OWED, no re-read owed, fails it at the copy and both announcements). A point admission now publishes `snapshotChanged` and takes the store's generation when its view differs from the copy, as any other followed view does.
- **The insufficientSpace convention's third site (non-blocking; F-22).** Pinned by `R-SYN-11: a waiter of a job the ledger parks for lack of space gets the bytes it needs and the room above the reserve` (`9111c5d`, written against correct code; mutant M-JOBS fails it at both figure expectations). The contract doc comment `ErrorMapping.swift:50-51` is listed for the docs level (paragraph above F-1, F-22).
- **Traceability (non-blocking).** The cycle-2 test of a run's failed apply checks events, not availability: re-tagged `R-SYN-12:` (`acb0db7`, L4-R15; F-22 updated).
- **Not changed.** The store guard (L4-R5 second bullet; F-21's question): still no ruling, so no edit to WP-C's store and its window stays recorded residue (F-14, F-22). The ledger checks at resolve registration and prefetch join: no seam, now covered by the pre-transfer read (F-14). The 10,000-asset test's limit and asset count (F-17). `outside-wp` (L4-R11): P-13 and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target. The verifier's PROBE-C2-5 timeout observation: not reproduced by the verifier in more than 100 runs; nothing to act on.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`73d20b6`; run in this session after it; an earlier full set at `4038f7f` gave the same verdicts).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 229 tests; load 2.1 to 4.3). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 229 of 229 each (3.96, 3.98, 3.97 s; the 10,000-asset test 3.96 to 3.97 s; load 3.3 to 4.7); under the thread sanitizer once: 229 of 229, 0 reports, 16.97 s (load 2.8 to 4.3), and again beside the simulator run: 229 of 229, 0 reports, 67.4 s (1-minute load 41.8 to 95.1, median 81.5, 21 samples), 28.1 % of the 240 s limit. Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.35% (6624/7096) OK` (`Engine/` files: EngineRuns 90.73, EngineLifecycle 90.93, EngineFailures 91.01, EngineCommands 91.79, EngineJobs 91.81, EngineDispatch 92.22, EnginePresence 93.30, EngineTimers 93.75, the rest above 94); the gate's one failure is `ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542)`, `outside-wp`. iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): exit 0. iOS 27 Simulator (`897F9D8D-…`): `** TEST SUCCEEDED **`, 1,023 of 1,023 passed (result bundle `Test-ZTronAssetKit-2026.10.15_21-40-38-+0200.xcresult`; the 10,000-asset test 10.5 s; load up to 95). Release benchmarks (1-minute load 2.6 to 7.5, median 3.4): P-4 11.44 ms (budget 20.0), ratio 2.005 (max 2.5); P-7 2.45 MiB for 256 MiB and 0.13 MiB for 1,024 MiB (max 48.0); P-11 speedup 3.67 (min 3.0); P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled-prune benchmark ratio 1.42 (max 2). P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed: `unit max 12.660541 ms (p50 0.966458 ms, p95 0.9935 ms); budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`.
- **Dispositions of the cycle-2 findings.**

| Finding | Blocking | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| correct: a dropped ledger makes runs download present content (PROBE-C2-2, -4, -6) | yes | Fixed: failing tests `d1560c3`, fix `26e77f3` (pre-transfer presence read); mutant M-PRE shown. |
| correct: an integrity failure is recorded against a present record (PROBE-C2-3) | yes | Fixed: failing tests `d1560c3`, fix `82beef0` (`sparePresent` before `recordIntegrityFailure`); mutant M-INT shown. |
| correct: `reportUnreadable` leaves sibling bindings stale (PROBE-C2-7) | yes | Fixed: failing test `d1560c3`, fixes `44dfaa8`, `25fd9f7` (holders snapshot, ledger drop, announcements, heals); mutant M-SIB shown. |
| correct: catch-up can adopt a store view older than an apply (unproven) | no | Made correct by construction (`followStoreView`, `ledgerForgets`; `5a53605`; the owed re-read: failing test `cd816af`, fix `73d20b6`), pinned at the function level; mutants M-VIEW and M-OWED shown. |
| reqs: the `insufficientSpace` convention's ledger site not pinned (M-JOBS) | no | Test added (`9111c5d`); mutant M-JOBS shown. |
| reqs: `ErrorMapping.swift:50-51` contradicts the convention | no | Listed for the docs level (paragraph above F-1, F-22); WP-A's file not edited. |
| reqs: the 10,000-asset test at 59.6 % of its limit under concurrent contention | no | Figures recorded in F-17; no assertion, limit or count changed (load-only). |
| reqs and correct: resolve-registration and prefetch-join ledger checks untested (M-RESOLVEREG, M-JOIN) | no | Covered by the pre-transfer read since this cycle; recorded in F-14. |
| reqs: dropping the ledger accepts a redundant download (L4-R5 "never") | no | Resolved by the pre-transfer read: no redundant download; the transient job is listed for the orchestrator's acknowledgement (paragraph above F-1). |
