## F-24 — Close-out cycle 4: the cycle-3 verifiers' findings, the blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 3 left one blocking finding (correctness lens, PROBE-C3-7: 16 of 16 serialized and 13 of 16 in parallel at `c5a1487`, 8 of 8 at `676948c`, so pre-existing since the cycle-2 catch-up design) and non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`: L4-R1…R15 unchanged). The regression test lives in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle4Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051).
- **A prune written by an `apply` that threw is announced whoever commits after it (blocking; L4-R7, L4-R10, R-SYN-12; PROBE-C3-7).** *Reproduced, deterministically* (the verifier's probe polled with sleeps; the test orders the two applies with seams): two `ensureAvailable` calls each apply a manifest that drops `z`. The first `apply` is held in the store's planning unit (`NextUnitGate`; the store's apply gate is then held) until the second has begun its own (`appliesInFlight == 2`). Released, the first prunes `z` in the store's memory and fails its index commit (ADR-050); the second re-applies against that memory, so its report prunes nothing, and commits. The engine adopted the second report without announcing `z`, and the catch-up the failure owed then read a view equal to the copy: `assetRemoved(z, .pruned)` never arrived. Both arrangements of `R-SYN-12: a prune an apply wrote before it threw is announced when a concurrent apply of the same manifest commits` (the second applies the same manifest, or a later one that also drops `z`) failed 3 of 3 runs at `1e4cab4`, each only at the announcement. *Options.* (a) The verifier's: for a committed `apply`, announce the store's prunes and every id the engine's copy listed that the applied manifest does not (one pass over the copy before it moves). (b) Remember, on each failed `apply`, the ids it may have pruned and announce them at the next commit or catch-up: O(n) only on the failure path, but correct only if the engine processes failures and commits in store order. (c) Let a catch-up diff against the copy before the last `apply`: needs a history of copies. *Decision:* (a), `applied` → `prunesUnreported` (`42b7c1c`). An id is announced when it leaves the engine's copy, there or in `followStoreView`, so exactly once; when the store diffed against the copy, its prunes are exactly those ids and nothing extra is announced (the ordinary path's events are unchanged). Cost: one pass over the copy with O(1) lookups, nothing when the copy already is the manifest; the same pass over 100,000 ids took 6.0 to 7.8 ms in an optimised micro-benchmark (C2's ~10 ms), the pass a catch-up already makes. The seam the test needs (`b95b06b`): the engine's test waits are woken when an `apply` begins and when a catch-up ends (`notifyObservers`; nothing else changes). Mutant M-UNREP (`prunesUnreported` answers nothing) fails both cases.
- **The binding ledger's job avoidance is pinned (non-blocking; L4-R5; mutant M-NOLEDGER).** Option (a) of the finding: the engine counts the jobs it creates (`EngineState.jobsCreated`, incremented where `addInterest` creates one, T1), and `R-SYN-6: a run does not download an asset another interest committed after its plan, and counts it as fetched` and `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable does not download a member another interest committed after its plan` assert that reaching the asset another interest committed creates no job (`a1bbca2`; assertions added to this level's own tests, L4-R15). M-NOLEDGER (`isBound` always `false`) fails both at the new assertion. F-14 records what stays unpinned (M-RESOLVEREG, M-JOIN).
- **The older-view test checks events (non-blocking; R-SYN-12).** `R-SYN-12: a store view read before an apply the engine followed never takes the engine's copy back` now subscribes before the refused follow and asserts that subscribers get no `assetRemoved(b, .pruned)` and no `snapshotChanged` older than the apply's generation (`aa1ec13`; L4-R15). Mutant M-VIEW (the guard always lets the view through) fails it at the two copy expectations and at the new event expectation.
- **`insufficientSpace` wording (non-blocking; F-22).** `spaceFailed`'s doc comment now says the bytes the attempt needs and the job's room above the reserve; the test is renamed `R-SYN-11: a fetch failing with ENOSPC and nothing to reclaim fails its waiter with the bytes it needs and the room above the reserve`, and its `available` expectation now also bounds the figure by the 4,096 bytes the volume has over the reserve (a stronger assertion; `c255760`, L4-R15). The contract comment `ErrorMapping.swift:50-51` stays listed for the docs level (paragraph above F-1).
- **No transfer request once the engine stopped running (non-blocking, by reading; not produced).** `drive` returns after the pre-transfer reads (`answeredFromStore`) when the engine no longer runs (`8b496e4`). The request it sent before was cancelled at once and not drained (the path after `remote.transfer` already returns when the engine is not running), so the only change is that no request is sent or counted. No seam sits between the reads and a shutdown.
- **Not changed.** The store guard (L4-R5 second bullet; F-21's question) and the residual window the 404, integrity and pre-transfer reads share: still no ruling, so WP-C's store is not edited and the window stays recorded residue (F-14, F-22, paragraph above F-1). L4-R5's "never creates a job" read as "never downloads" after a dropped ledger (F-23): unchanged, listed for ADR-053 (paragraph above F-1). A holder gained between `blobHolders` and the quarantine (F-23, f-12): unchanged residue. The 10,000-asset test: figures in F-17, no limit, count or assertion changed. `outside-wp` (L4-R11): P-13, the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target, the P-1/P-8/P-9/P-12 benchmarks.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`8b496e4`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 230 tests; load 2.6 to 3.9). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 230 of 230 each (4.02, 4.01, 4.02 s; the 10,000-asset test 4.02, 4.00 and 4.02 s; load 3.0 to 5.9); under the thread sanitizer once: 230 of 230, no report, 17.68 s, the 10,000-asset test 17.68 s (7.4 % of its 240 s limit; load 4.8 to 3.1). Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.44% (6664/7132) min=90.0 OK`; the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,024 of 1,024 passed, 0 failed (Sync 230 tests in 12.23 s, the 10,000-asset test 12.18 s; load 3.2 at the start, 23.2 at the end with other simulators running). Release benchmarks WP-F owns: `P-4 plan 10,000: 11.42 ms (budget 20.0)`, ratio 2.00 (max 2.5); `P-7` 1.42 and 0.14 MiB (max 48); `P-11` speedup 3.67 (min 3.0); `P-14` 0 timers, 0 tasks; R-SYN-12's stalled-subscriber prune of 10,000 ids 8.0 ms against 11.4 ms, ratio 1.42 (max 2, plus 5 ms), which includes `prunesUnreported`; 1-minute load during the run 2.6 to 16.7, median 4.4. `outside-wp`: P-13 failed (`unit max 10.965416 ms … budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`, L4-R11).
- **Dispositions of the cycle-3 findings.**

| reqs: the binding ledger is untested as a whole (M-NOLEDGER) | no | Option (a): `jobsCreated` and two assertions (`a1bbca2`); M-NOLEDGER now fails both; F-14 updated. |

## F-25 — Close-out cycle 5: the cycle-4 verifiers' findings, each blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 4 left three blocking findings (correctness lens) and non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`: L4-R1…R15 unchanged). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle5Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051).
- **Two committed applies followed out of the store's order (blocking; R-SYN-12, L4-R7, L4-R10; PROBE-C4-4, PROBE-C4-5).** *Reproduced, deterministically.* A run's `apply` (v2, drops `z`) is held in the store's planning unit while an `ensureAvailable` begins its own (v2 again, or v3 that adds the on-demand `b`); the store commits the run's first. The verifier made the engine actor run the ensure's continuation first (a busy actor and a higher priority); the test does it with a seam (`8d040a0`: `holdNextApplyFollow()` holds the engine's side of the next `apply` to return, until no other `apply` is at the store or waiting to be followed; no production path arms it, REENTRANCY f-8). Following the run's older report last took the copy back to v2 while the store held v3, announced `z` pruned twice and announced `b` pruned although the store lists and serves it: `R-SYN-12: two committed applies are followed in the store's order, so a prune is announced once and a later apply's additions never` failed 6 of 6 runs at `41f2c7c` (5 issues each). The copy roll-back was older than cycle 4; the double and false announcements came with `prunesUnreported` (F-24). *Options.* (a) The verifier's: keep the store generation the copy reflects, skip a report older than it (forgetting only its unbinds) and announce exactly the ids that leave the copy. (b) Serialise the engine's applies: one at the store at a time, the next only after the engine followed the previous one. *Decision:* (b) (`cb0fb44`; `takeApplyTurn`, `passApplyTurn`, REENTRANCY f-13). The store already runs one `apply` at a time, so the only added wait is the engine's turn for the previous one; the continuations can then never run out of commit order, whatever the priorities, and the announcement rules of F-24 stay as they are and stay exact (an id leaves the copy once). (a) needs a rule for reports of equal generation (an unchanged `apply` commits no new generation, so a late report of the same generation cannot be told from a timely one by its number) and a memory of what a newer report already announced, to neither repeat `z` nor lose a prune of an id the copy never listed. Queued applies count in `manifestCatchUps` from the moment they begin (held jobs stay held), not in `appliesInFlight` (only an `apply` at the store can make a store view older than the copy); shutdown resumes every queued `apply`, which then sends nothing (`.failure(.closed)`, which callers map to `shutDown`). Mutant M-FIFO (the turn never waits) fails the new test; M-UNREP still fails both cases of the cycle-4 test. The cycle-4 test waited for `appliesInFlight == 2`, which the turn makes impossible: it now waits for both applies to have begun (`manifestCatchUps == 2`, `fd79a9a`; checked to pass before and after the fix; no assertion changed, L4-R15).
- **A job waiting for space that nothing will reclaim (blocking; R-SYN-11, R-SYN-10, L4-R8).** *Reproduced, deterministically* (`0838b3a`): the store counted doomed bytes as pending reclaim while `nextDeadline()` scheduled nothing for them, so a job short of space parked `awaitingSpace`, which only the alarm's `runDueWork()` wakes, and nothing ever did: `R-SYN-11: ensureAvailable short of bytes the store has not scheduled to reclaim fails with insufficientSpace instead of waiting for ever` hit its 1-minute limit 3 of 3 runs, the ensure answering `cancelled` as in the verifier's trace. The test reaches that store state with a snapshot sink that keeps its snapshot (no install is acknowledged, so the pruned blob waits for an acknowledgement and has no deadline; its setup expectations check `pendingReclaim == 300` and `nextDeadline() == nil`); the verifier reached it through the store's timing after an `apply` that threw (F-14, and the WP-B report above F-1). *Decision* (`e141721`, `52324a0`): a job is parked `awaitingSpace` only while something will run the store's due work: a store deadline armed, the alarm's store work under way (`storeWorkInFlight`, bookkeeping added first), or a re-read of the store's deadline in flight. Parking one with none of these re-reads the store's next deadline (an owned task; the engine's copy may predate work the store took on); when there is still none, and whenever the store deadline is cleared or the alarm's store work ends with jobs still waiting, those jobs are parked `insufficientSpace` (`settleUnwakeableSpaceWaits`): waiters and ensures get the typed figures at once, a run settles blocked and stays due, and the job is re-admitted at any later free-space read that holds it (a read after reaped bytes included). *Not chosen:* treating every pending reclaim without a deadline as a shortage at the plan (the planner counts pending reclaim as room on purpose, ADR-022), and a timer that re-reads the store (a poll; `runDueWork()` would not reclaim those bytes anyway).
- **An ensure whose member lacked room answered success (non-blocking, by reading; done first in this cycle because the blocking test's typed expectation needs it; R-SYN-11).** *Reproduced* (`0838b3a`): the plan fits, the member's job lacks room beside a transfer in flight and is failed `insufficientSpace`, and the ensure answered `.success` with the member missing (3 of 3). *Decision* (`20fefe1`): the group keeps the first `insufficientSpace` a member was failed with, and the ensure answers it while its scope is still incomplete (`R-SYN-11: ensureAvailable whose member lacks room beside a transfer in flight fails with insufficientSpace`); a scope another interest completed meanwhile still answers `.success`. Other member failures keep answering `.success` with the report's counts, as before.
- **Two tests that depended on the store's background work (blocking; L4-R8).** `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable downloads a member that an apply evicted before it threw` failed once in-suite and hung twice in 208 stressed runs: with `h` in flight, `z`'s job needed the evicted bytes reclaimed, which depended on when the alarm ran. The ballast is now removed before the second ensure, so the outcome depends on the binding ledger alone (its subject, PROBE-C1-N1); every assertion is unchanged, and mutant M-N1 (the ledger kept after a failed `apply`) fails four of them. `R-SYN-12: after a run's apply throws past its in-memory writes, the engine follows the store and announces its prunes` compared with a store generation the store's due work may move on before or after the catch-up's read (one failure in 208); it now requires the generation the engine followed (`state.generation`) to be announced at or after `z`'s removal, and mutant M-SNAP (no `snapshotChanged` from the catch-up) fails it (`63bc875`). A first attempt that required the followed generation to exceed the one before the failed run was wrong (the catch-up can read the view before the store's commit retry, at the same generation) and was not committed.
- **Display name (non-blocking).** The cycle-4 test is now `R-SYN-12: a prune an apply wrote before it threw is announced when a concurrent apply of that manifest or a later one that also drops it commits` (`7d76861`, L4-R15).
- **Not changed.** The store guard and the shared one-await window (L4-R5, F-21's question; no ruling), L4-R5's "never creates a job" read as "never downloads" after a dropped ledger (for ADR-053), a holder gained between `blobHolders` and the quarantine (F-23): all recorded residue (F-14, paragraph above F-1). `outside-wp` (L4-R11): P-13, the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target, the P-1/P-8/P-9/P-12 benchmarks.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`52324a0`, tests to `7d76861`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 233 tests, the 10,000-asset test 4.04 s; 1-minute load 1.8 to 7.9, a sanitizer run beside it). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 233 of 233 each (4.04, 4.03, 4.06 s; the 10,000-asset test 4.04, 4.03 and 4.06 s; load 3.8 to 6.7). Under the thread sanitizer: 233 of 233 and no report twice: alone 17.85 s, the 10,000-asset test 17.84 s (7.4 % of its 240 s limit; load 4.1 to 5.8, median 4.8, 11 samples), the same as cycle 4's 17.68 s, so the apply turn and the space-wake checks cost nothing measurable; beside the `--quick` run 60.21 s (load 4.1 to 10.7, median 8.4; 25.1 %). Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.38% (6799/7281) min=90.0 OK`; the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,027 of 1,027 passed, 0 failed (Sync 233 tests in 16.70 s, the 10,000-asset test 16.68 s; 1-minute load 3.6 to 91.3, median 44.6, while a stress loop of the regression suites ran beside it). Stress: the four regression suites of the close-out (`EngineRegressionCycle3Tests` to `EngineRegressionCycle5Tests`, `EngineRegressionCloseOutTests`) 25 runs in a row, 25 passed, with the load rising to 92. Release benchmarks WP-F owns: `P-4 plan 10,000: 11.48 ms (budget 20.0)`, ratio 2.02 (max 2.5); `P-7` 0.94 and 0.63 MiB (max 48); `P-11` speedup 3.72 (min 3.0); `P-14` 0 timers, 0 tasks; R-SYN-12's stalled-subscriber prune of 10,000 ids 8.1 ms against 11.4 ms, ratio 1.41 (max 2, plus 5 ms); 1-minute load during the run 2.5 to 19.3, median 5.0. `outside-wp`: P-13 failed (`unit max 8.761666 ms … budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`, L4-R11).
- **Dispositions of the cycle-4 findings.**

| correct: a job parked `awaitingSpace` that nothing wakes; `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable downloads a member that an apply evicted before it threw` fails in-suite and hangs | yes | Fixed: bookkeeping `e141721`, failing tests `0838b3a`, fix `52324a0` (park `insufficientSpace` when nothing will run the store's due work); the R-SYN-10 test no longer depends on the reclaim's timing (`63bc875`; M-N1 shown); WP-B gap reported (paragraph above F-1). |

## F-26 — Close-out cycle 6: the cycle-5 verifiers' findings, the blocking one with failing tests first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 5 left one blocking finding (correctness lens) and non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`: L4-R1…R15 unchanged). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle6Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051).
- **Jobs waiting for space settled `insufficientSpace` on out-of-date free-space figures (blocking; R-SYN-11, R-SYN-10, ARCHITECTURE §7.6; PROBE-C5-4b).** *Reproduced, deterministically* (`d1e764c`, 5 of 5 runs failed). Cycle 5's `settleUnwakeableSpaceWaits` parked every `awaitingSpace` job `insufficientSpace` on the engine's last read once nothing would run the store's due work; only the alarm's own reaped bytes refreshed that read. A purge's reaper reclaims every deletable blob, so when it ran before the alarm (the verifier's busy engine actor), the alarm then reaped nothing, the store scheduled nothing, and `y` was failed on a read that predated both reclaims. Three tests, each failing before the fix:
  - `R-SYN-11: ensureAvailable whose member waits for bytes a purge reclaimed before the engine's alarm ran fetches it instead of failing on older free-space figures` (PROBE-C5-4b): answered `insufficientSpace(required: 300, available: 80)` with 703 bytes above the reserve, the verifier's figures. The verifier forced the order with a 150 ms sleep inside the engine actor (a timing assumption); the test gives the store its own clock (`EngineHarness.make(storeTime:)`, a test-only option) and passes the purge's grace on it while the engine's clock, and so its alarm, stays behind; the engine's clock then catches up.
  - `R-SYN-11: a job waiting for space that nothing will reclaim is re-checked on a fresh free-space read before it fails with insufficientSpace`: the same defect without a purge (the volume gains room the engine has not read; at the end of `x`'s grace the store installs a fresh snapshot nobody acknowledges, so it reaps nothing): answered `insufficientSpace(300, 91)` with 99,083 bytes above the reserve.
  - `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable whose member waits for space fetches it as soon as a purge reclaimed the bytes, not at the store's next deadline`: after the purge reclaimed `q`'s bytes (enough for `y`), `y` still waited for `x`'s deadline 10 s later.
  *Options.* (a) Settle on a fresh read (the verifier's); (b) refresh after a store call that may reap; (c) treat every clear of the store deadline as a reason to read (a read per alarm run). *Decision:* (a) and (b) (`4036701`). (a) `settleUnwakeableSpaceWaits` no longer parks anything: when a job waits `awaitingSpace` and nothing will run the store's due work, it starts one read (`spaceSettleRead`, which counts as a wake meanwhile, so neither a second read nor a deadline re-read starts) and `settleSpaceWaitsOnFreshRead` decides each job still waiting: re-queued when that read holds it; left waiting when a wake was armed meanwhile; else parked `insufficientSpace` with that read's figures (an earlier ENOSPC's `required` kept, `available` re-computed, F-22). A failed read leaves the last figures, which then decide (the store cannot measure the volume). (b) `spaceMayHaveBeenReclaimed` after every purge, returned or thrown (the reaper reclaims more than the purge's own `bytesFreed`, and a purge that threw may have reaped a round first): a fresh read (which re-admits the `insufficientSpace` jobs it holds), then the `awaitingSpace` jobs are re-queued and gated on it. Neither reads anything while no job is parked for space. Mutants: M-STALE (the settlement without its read) fails only the second test; M-NOREAP (no read after a purge) fails only the third; the first passes with either half alone (each ends in a fresh read before any shortage) and fails without both. *Not chosen:* (c), and a read after maintenance (F-14: never a false shortage since (a), only a later re-admission).
- **Two alarm runs whose store work overlaps (non-blocking, by reading; PROBE-C5-5 inconclusive).** `storeWorkInFlight` is now derived from a count (`storeWorkRuns`, incremented and decremented in `fireDue`), so the first run to end no longer marks the other's work over (`4036701`). Not produced, so no test pins it (F-14).
- **The settlement's cost (non-blocking, efficiency).** The sort of every job key at each alarm run without a store deadline is gone: the settlement returns after one scan that stops at the first `awaitingSpace` job, and sorts only when it decides jobs. No count of `awaitingSpace` jobs is kept: it would need upkeep at every transition and removal for a scan that measured nothing (the verifier's 10,000-asset figures; this cycle's below).
- **Test seam (non-blocking).** An `apply` that joins the turn queue notifies the waiting test seams (`838536a`), so a seam can wait on `applyQueue.count`.
- **Display and setup (L4-R15).** The third test dropped a setup check of the room after the purge, which `y`'s own download may already have taken (`76af93d`, before the fix; it still failed on its assertion).

## F-27 — Close-out cycle 7: the cycle-6 verifiers' findings, the blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 6 left one blocking finding (requirements lens, L4-R4) and non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md`: L4-R1…R15 unchanged, each recorded above as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle7Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051).
- **An ENOSPC that waits for the store's reclaim joins the retry budget (blocking; L4-R4, R-SYN-11, R-SYN-5; PROBE-C6R-1).** *Reproduced, deterministically* (`8bb89f1`): `R-SYN-11: a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while the store holds bytes it has not scheduled to reclaim ends in insufficientSpace once the retry budget is spent`, for a resolve, an ensure and a run, scripted with the budget plus 20 ENOSPC failures and then a success, made 23, 23 and 25 transfer starts against budgets of 2, 2 and 4, and each ended in success. `spaceFailed` parked an ENOSPC `awaitingSpace` whenever the store counted pending reclaim, without counting an attempt; with nothing scheduled to reclaim those bytes (cycle 5's unacknowledged install), the settlement's fresh read (cycle 6) held the job and re-queued it at once, and the next start failed the same way. *Options.* (a) Count the attempt when the ENOSPC parks `awaitingSpace`, as `retryLater` does (the verifier's sketch); (b) drop `!reclaiming`, so a volume that holds the job always goes through `retryLater`: it fails `R-SYN-11: a fetch blocked on ENOSPC resumes after the store's reaper frees doomed blobs` (the verifier checked), which pins F-20's approved order (pending reclaim → `awaitingSpace`); (c) also route the settlement's re-queue of such a job through `retryLater`'s backoff: the transition table has no `blocked → failed` (`JobState`: `blocked → queued | blocked`), so it needs a new transition or deadline kind for a path whose attempts (a) already bounds. *Decision:* (a) (`eb792f3`). `countFailedAttempt` is taken out of `retryLater` unchanged (the attempt, `lastError`, the retry metric, the interests at their lane's cap get the figures, and the job ends `failed(.nextRun)` with its terminal event when none is left); `spaceFailed` calls it before it parks `awaitingSpace`. *Consequences.* A resolve or an ensure makes at most 2 attempts, a run 4, then a typed `insufficientSpace` with F-22's figures (the bytes the ENOSPC asked for against the job's room above the reserve); the wait for the reclaim, or for the settlement's read, stands where the backoff would. A fetch that waits for the reaper and then succeeds is unchanged (its wait counts as one attempt). `metrics().retries[.insufficientSpace]` now counts these parks too, as it counts `retryLater`'s. *Not chosen:* (b), (c).
- **An exclusivity conflict in the space settlement (found this cycle; a crash).** Writing the correctness verifier's optional test (b) produced it: `R-SYN-11: an ensure whose member waits for space after an ENOSPC asking for more than the entry fails with the bytes the ENOSPC asked for and the room above the reserve` stopped the test process with "Simultaneous accesses to …, but modification requires exclusive access" in `SyncEngine.headroom(for:in:)` called from `settleSpaceWaitsOnFreshRead` ("Fatal access conflict detected", 5 of 5 runs; test `89b6da4`). The settlement refreshed an earlier ENOSPC's figures with `self.state.jobs[transfer]?.lastError = .insufficientSpace(required: required, available: self.headroom(for: job, in: space))`: the right side of an optional-chained assignment runs inside the write's access to `state`, and `headroom` reads `state`. Swift checks exclusivity at run time in release builds too, so an app whose job reached this branch (count 0 in cycle 6's coverage run) would have stopped. Fix (`95d027a`): the room is computed before the write. A search of `Sources/` for an optional-chained write whose right side reads `self` finds no other site. The test also pins the figures: the ENOSPC's `required` (1,000,000, not the entry's 300) against the room above the reserve that the store reports afterwards, and one start. It does not pin the re-computation of `available` (both reads see the same room).
- **Test strength of `purgeReclaimsBeforeTheAlarm` (non-blocking, optional).** Tried: an assertion of `y`'s state right after the purge returns. It does not fail under mutant M-NOREAP (no read after a purge that returned): there the purge's `refreshStoreDeadline` clears `x`'s deadline, so the settlement's fresh read re-admits `y` within the same command. M-NOREAP fails `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable whose member waits for space fetches it as soon as a purge reclaimed the bytes, not at the store's next deadline` (run this cycle under the mutant: failed at line 238). The busy-actor order (the alarm between the store's reaper and the purge's return to the engine) needs a seam the harness lacks. Not changed; M-STALE stays pinned by `spaceWaitSettledOnAFreshRead`.
- **The settlement's uncovered branches (non-blocking; L4-R12).** Test (b) is written (above). Test (a), a settlement read that races a newly armed store deadline, needs a seam inside the one read; recorded in F-14.
- **Not changed.** The L4-R5 store guard and the one-await window shared by the 404, integrity and pre-transfer reads (no ruling on F-21's question), L4-R5's "never creates a job" read as "never downloads" after a dropped ledger (for ADR-053), a holder gained between `blobHolders` and the quarantine (F-23, f-12): recorded residue (F-14, paragraph above F-1). `outside-wp` (L4-R11): P-13, the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target, the P-1/P-8/P-9/P-12 benchmarks. WP-D's two loopback cancellation tests that failed once on the iOS Simulator at a 1-minute load up to 468.9 (`ForegroundBackendTests.swift:162,164` and `:200,202`) are WP-D's; WP-F changed no Transport file. Earlier verifiers' idle test helpers (PIDs 52290, 53148, 27626) are the orchestrator's to stop.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`95d027a`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 238 tests, the 10,000-asset test 4.136 s; 1-minute load 2.18 to 7.70, median 5.04). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 238 of 238 each (4.170, 4.125 and 4.103 s; the 10,000-asset test 4.167, 4.124 and 4.101 s; load 1.70 to 2.76). Under the thread sanitizer: 238 of 238, no report; the 10,000-asset test 18.136 s (7.6 % of its 240 s limit; load 2.51 to 3.20, median 2.79). Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.43% (6898/7383) min=90.0 OK`; the settlement's figures refresh and the counted `awaitingSpace` park now run (1 and 10 executions); the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded (exit 0, no diagnostic). iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,032 of 1,032 passed, 0 failed (the cycle-7 suite 2.944 s; the 10,000-asset test 10.517 s; 1-minute load 2.80 to 84.41, median 54.65); the simulator is shut down. The cycle-7 suite five times in a row after the fix: 5 passed. Release benchmarks WP-F owns: `P-4 plan 10,000: 11.747167 ms (budget 20.0)`, ratio 2.12 (max 2.5); `P-7` 0.95 and 0.16 MiB (max 48); `P-11` speedup 3.50 (min 3.0); `P-14` 0 timers, 0 tasks; R-SYN-12's stalled-subscriber prune of 10,000 ids 8.6 ms against 12.4 ms, ratio 1.45 (max 2, plus 5 ms); 1-minute load during the run 3.86 to 21.72, median 7.30. `outside-wp`: P-13 failed (`unit max 6.674833 ms … budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`, L4-R11), the only failure of the 20 benchmark tests.

## F-28 — Close-out cycle 8: the cycle-7 verifiers' findings, the blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 7 left one blocking finding (correctness lens: a quadratic actor stall when many jobs park for lack of space, PROBE-C7C-2) and non-blocking ones. No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` read again at the start of cycle 8: L4-R1…R15 unchanged, each recorded above as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle8Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051), and in two release benchmarks of `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchSync.swift` (WP-F's file; thresholds from `QualityBudgets`, no literal).
- **The headroom ledger was quadratic in the jobs (blocking; C2, R-SYN-11; PROBE-C7C-2).** *Reproduced* by a failing release benchmark (`2944091`): `R-SYN-11: parking a 10,000-member prefetch group for lack of space, and an ENOSPC's free-space read over it, never hold the engine's actor longer than P-9's heartbeat_gap_ms_p99_max`. A prefetch group of 10,000 on-demand 400-byte members on a volume with 1,000 bytes above the reserve (two start and hold, the others park `insufficientSpace`), then an ENOSPC of the first started member; a high-priority heartbeat calls into the engine's actor back to back, so its longest wait bounds the longest actor turn. At `02fb6c2` (medians of three, quiet host, 1-minute load 2.8 to 3.6): 9,754 ms while the members parked and 14,616 ms during the ENOSPC's free-space read, against P-9's 50 ms. Cause, as the verifier found: `headroom(for:in:)` summed the bytes in flight over every job for each job it gated; the parking pump gated every member twice in one turn, and every later read re-queued each member that fitted on its own, after which the pump parked all but one again. *Options.* (a) A running in-flight total kept by the transition funnel (the finding's first sketch); (b) a total computed once per pump round, sweep and settlement (its alternative): still a pass over every job per pass, and it leaves the re-queue and re-park of every parked job per read; (c) (a), plus bounds on the other work one turn does at that scale. *Decision:* (c), in four commits:
  1. `39f71b2`: `InFlightBytes`, a two-word running total (a removal undoes its addition exactly; the ledger reads `min(total, Int64.max)`, the figure the saturating sum gave), and the set `inFlight`, both kept by `transition` (the only mutator of `Job.state`; a job is created queued), `removeJob` and shutdown; `Job.entry` is now `let`, so a job leaves flight with the bytes it entered with. `headroom` is O(1) (the total less the job's own bytes when it is in flight itself). The slot counts, the preemption victims and the policy-pause pass of `apply(_:)` read `inFlight` only: a pass over every job cost about 0.35 µs per job in release, about 4 ms per pump at 10,000. `invariantViolations()` checks the set and the total against the job table, so the model-based test checks them after every step.
  2. `0f05022`: `joinGroup` joins `materializationLimit` members per actor turn and dispatches them, then yields (`Task.yield()`, f-14 (proposed)) and re-validates the lifecycle, the source, the group and the manifest copy (a changed copy sends the members left through a fresh `planningView()`, because the view's records may be stale for the new entries). `pump()` parks at most `parksPerPump` (512) jobs per turn and leaves the rest to an owned follow-up turn (`pumpFollowUp`, flag `pumpFollowUpScheduled`): a backlog in which every job parks, such as the first conditions element after a large join, took 15 to 28 ms in one turn at 10,000 (heartbeat probe, this cycle).
  3. `11de49e` (failing test) and `0274006`: `sweepSpaceParked`, one sweep over the jobs parked for space (an index `spaceParked`, kept and checked like `inFlight`) in the order the pump serves them (user singles, user batches, prefetch, bulk; by queue ticket within a lane), each admitted against the room the jobs admitted ahead of it in the sweep leave (`spaceBlock(_:ahead:)`; the gate counts those in flight once they start). An admitted job is re-queued; an `insufficientSpace` one that is not stays parked untouched until a later read; an `awaitingSpace` one is treated as the sweep's mode says: `.read` (every free-space read) does not sweep it (it waits for the reaper, T17); `.reclaimed` (after the alarm's reaped bytes, and after a purge, `spaceMayHaveBeenReclaimed`) leaves it waiting while the store counts pending reclaim and parks it `insufficientSpace` otherwise; `.settle` (`settleSpaceWaitsOnFreshRead`) leaves it waiting only while a wake is expected and parks it `insufficientSpace` otherwise. A job parked behind admitted ones reports the bytes it needs against the room they leave (F-22's convention: `required` exceeds `available`). A sweep parks at most `parksPerPump` jobs per turn and owes a follow-up sweep the rest (`spaceSweepFollowUp`, counted in `spaceWakeExpected`). The three reads after a possible reclaim now read through `readFreeSpace` and sweep once (they re-checked the `insufficientSpace` jobs and then re-queued every `awaitingSpace` one separately).
  4. `76000b8`: the doc comment of `spaceFailed` re-wrapped (the cycle-7 style finding).
  *Consequences.* The figures after the fix are below (benchmark run of this cycle). Re-admission after a read follows the queue order, not the order of transfer identifiers (content hashes). A job that fits on its own but not after the jobs admitted ahead of it stays parked until a later read; before, it was re-queued and, with a slot free, parked again at once, or, with every slot taken, waited queued and was gated again when a slot freed. The difference shows only when a job in flight then ends without committing (a failure or a cancellation): its room is used at the next free-space read instead of at the next dispatch; a commit keeps its bytes in the ledger until the next read in both designs. *Not chosen:* (a) or (b) alone. *Corrected in close-out cycle 18:* this consequence did not cover runs. A run whose working claim finished after such a job ended settled `.blocked(.insufficientSpace)` although the ledger admitted its claim parked for space (the cycle-17 correctness verifier's PROBE-C17C-INFLIGHT). `checkRunSettled` now re-checks the jobs parked `insufficientSpace` (a `.read` sweep) before it settles a run one of whose claims the ledger admits (F-38). Other parked jobs still wait for the next read.
- **Tests (behaviour, each failing under its mutant).** `R-SYN-11: a free-space read re-admits the jobs parked for space that the room holds together and leaves the others parked untouched` (three parked 400-byte members, room for two: the two ahead in the queue start and the third keeps its queue ticket; it failed before the sweep, `11de49e`, on all three expectations: the old code started members by transfer-id order and re-queued the third). `R-SYN-11: when the network returns under more queued members than one pump turn parks, every member the volume cannot hold ends parked for lack of space` (576 members parked `offline`, the volume then full: without the pump's follow-up, 64 stay queued and the test exceeds its time limit, mutant M-PUMPFOLLOW). `R-SYN-11: once the store reclaimed what jobs waited for, every one the room does not hold is parked insufficientSpace, more than one turn's worth` (576 members waiting `awaitingSpace` for a doomed blob; only the store's clock passes the grace, the store reaps, and the purge path sweeps: the first member starts and the other 575 are parked; without the sweep's follow-up, 63 stay `awaitingSpace` and the test exceeds its time limit, mutant M-SWEEPFOLLOW). Mutant M-AHEAD (the sweep ignores `ahead`) fails the first. `R-SYN-11: a job waiting for space keeps waiting after a reclaim that leaves it short while more is pending, and starts once the rest is reclaimed` (`0ffc058`) covers the sweep's waiting branch: `y` (500 bytes) waits for `x` and `z` (300 each, doomed 30 s apart); after `x` is reaped it stays `awaitingSpace` and does not start, and after `z` it is served, downloaded once; mutant M-RECLAIMPARK (a job the reclaim leaves short is parked `insufficientSpace` although more is pending) fails it on all three expectations. The old code reached the same end through a re-queue and a re-park, so this test pins behaviour the sweep must keep rather than a defect. A test of the join's copy-change branch needs a seam between two batches; recorded in F-14.
- **Measured residue at 10,000 jobs (not changed; for the orchestrator and ADR-053).** The second benchmark, `R-SYN-13: losing and regaining the network under a 10,000-member prefetch group never holds the engine's actor longer than P-9's heartbeat_gap_ms_p99_max` (`9f0d4af`), passes P-9's 50 ms, but its turns exceed C2's ~10 ms: going offline about 14.7 ms (one `parkLane` pass parks every queued member: a sort of every job and a park each) and coming back about 21.9 ms (`apply(_:)` re-queues every member parked `offline` and every one failed `whenOnline`, two sorted passes) (medians of three, quiet host). Both are linear and predate this cycle. Bounding `parkLane` per turn would change when a run settles blocked (a run claims queued jobs that are parked only when a slot is free); bounding the re-admission needs a follow-up pass. Not measured, and linear too: releasing a prefetch handle of 10,000 members (`performReleaseGroup` removes every interest in one turn). These go to VERIFICATION "Not verified" under C2 at 10,000 jobs.
- **The non-blocking findings.** *L4-R4 (both lenses):* no code change; the counted `awaitingSpace` retry without a timed backoff is for the orchestrator (L4-R4 above). *L4-R8 figures (both lenses):* recorded in F-17. *Style:* `spaceFailed`'s doc comment re-wrapped (`76000b8`). *Residue (both lenses):* unchanged, still recorded (F-14, F-22, F-23, F-26, F-27, paragraph above F-1). *`outside-wp` (both lenses):* P-13 and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target (L4-R11). *Host hygiene:* the idle test helpers (PIDs 27626, 52290, 53148) are not WP-F's; not touched.
- **A test that overran its time limit once (observed this cycle; not changed).** In this cycle's first `--quick` (at the code of `76000b8`, 1-minute load 4.6 to 3.9, coverage on, every target in parallel) `R-SYN-5: a run whose transfers open the 5xx breaker ends blocked and the pending run's probe closes it` (`EngineRunPathTests.swift:279`) exceeded its 60 s limit; the suite's other 240 tests passed. It did not recur in 8 isolated runs, 10 Sync-suite runs, 6 full coverage runs (load 3.9 to 5.5) and the later `--quick`. Under 8 CPU burners on this 8-core host (load about 12), `--maximum-repetitions 300 --repeat-until fail` fails it at once with another symptom, the launch run ending `.complete` instead of blocked by the breaker, at `02fb6c2` (the base of this cycle, built in a temporary worktree under `.build-f`, since removed) exactly as at `76000b8`: the test depends on `EngineHarness.drive` not moving the manual clock past the breaker's cooldown before the engine settles the run, which load can reorder. This cycle's changes do not reach its path (no job parks for space, no prefetch group, fewer than `parksPerPump` parks). Under L4-R8 a time-limit overrun is a flake: reported to the orchestrator with this evidence; a deterministic rewrite (drive only to the retry deadlines until the run settles, then past the cooldown) is the proposed fix, not made here because the overrun could not be reproduced.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`0ffc058`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 242 tests; the 10,000-asset test 4.019 s; 1-minute load 2.4 to 4.3; an earlier `--quick` at `76000b8` also passed, 241 tests, 3.963 s). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 242 of 242 each (3.959, 3.967 and 3.959 s; load 4.5 to 5.1). Under the thread sanitizer: 242 of 242, no report (the cycle-8 suite 2.145 s; the 10,000-asset test 15.781 s). Coverage (that `--quick`'s profile): `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.12% (7076/7599) min=90.0 OK`; the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded (exit 0, no diagnostic). iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,036 of 1,036 passed, 0 failed (the cycle-8 suite 3.219 s; the 10,000-asset test 12.739 s); the simulator is shut down. Release benchmarks WP-F owns (1-minute load 44.0 at the start, just after the simulator run, 3.7 at the end): `P-4 plan 10,000: 11.87 ms (budget 20.0)`, ratio 2.07 (max 2.5); `P-7` 1.91 and 0.47 MiB (max 48); `P-11` speedup 3.68 (min 3.0); `P-14` 0 timers, 0 tasks; R-SYN-12's stalled-subscriber prune 12.3 ms against 8.9 ms, ratio 1.38 (max 2, plus 5 ms); the new space-parking benchmark 5.77 ms while parking and 6.29 ms during the ENOSPC's read (runs 5.79, 5.77, 5.76 and 6.29, 6.16, 6.56; budget 50 ms; 9,754 and 14,616 ms before the fix); the new network-edge benchmark 14.93 ms offline and 22.00 ms online (budget 50 ms). `outside-wp`: P-13 failed (`unit max 9.714542 ms … budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`, L4-R11), the only failure of the 22 benchmark tests.
- **Dispositions of the cycle-7 findings.**

| Finding | Blocking | Disposition |
|---|---|---|

## F-29 — Close-out cycle 9: the cycle-8 verifiers' findings, each blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 8 left four blocking findings: actor turns over C2's ~10 ms at SPEC §6's 10,000 queued prefetch members with no ruling (requirements lens: going offline, coming back, releasing a prefetch handle; correctness lens: the same three and the space sweep's re-admissions when the volume gains room), the `R-SYN-5` breaker test's flake (both lenses; L4-R8), and the cycle-6 test helper `driving`, whose cancelled clock driver advanced the clocks after it returned (correctness lens; a sanitizer suite failure and a time-limit overrun). No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` read again at the start of cycle 9: L4-R1…R15 unchanged, each recorded above as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle9Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051), and in `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchSync.swift` (WP-F's file).
- **Actor turns over C2 at 10,000 members (blocking, both lenses; C2, R-SYN-4, R-SYN-11, R-SYN-13).** *Reproduced* by four failing release benchmarks (`f50cdfb`), which now assert C2 besides P-9: the two of F-28 (`R-SYN-11: parking a 10,000-member prefetch group for lack of space, and an ENOSPC's free-space read over it, …` and `R-SYN-13: losing and regaining the network under a 10,000-member prefetch group …`) and two new ones, `R-SYN-11: re-admitting 10,000 prefetch members parked for lack of space when the volume gains room holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn` (the correctness verifier's PROBE-C8C-2: the ballast removed and one free-space read) and `R-SYN-4: releasing a 10,000-member prefetch handle holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn and leaves no job` (PROBE-C8R-1, PROBE-C8C-3). The C2 budget is the rule's own 10 ms (`SyncBenchmarks.c2Budget`), as in `BenchCleanup` under the level-3 ruling R4 (ADR-052): `Scripts/quality-gates.json` has no C2 key and is frozen, and P-13's `batch_ms_max` is WP-B's per-batch reclaim budget, not a figure for an engine turn. It is asserted on the best of three runs (the heartbeat's longest wait is the turn plus scheduling noise, and noise only adds), the P-9 assertion on the median stays. At `ed6f98e` (release, this host, 1-minute load 2 to 3), the heartbeat's longest waits, runs of three: re-admission 15.54, 14.76 and 14.32 ms; going offline 14.08, 13.70 and 13.86 ms; coming back 20.52, 20.04 and 20.06 ms; release 13.24, 13.25 and 12.90 ms; all four failed C2 (the space-parking benchmark passed: 3.0 to 6.7 ms). *Options.* (a) An orchestrator exception assigned to Stage 3, as L3 R8 did (the verifiers' alternative); (b) bound each turn, as cycle 8 bounded the pump and the sweep's parks. *Decision:* (b), in `eca3e99`, `23342bd` and `0c7484b`:
  1. Indexes kept by the transition funnel (`reindex`), `removeJob` and shutdown, checked against the job table by `invariantViolations()` like `spaceParked`: `laneParked` (`blocked(offline)`, `blocked(policy)`), `circuitParked` and `throttleParked`, and `failedJobs`.
  2. `readmit(_:)` (new file `EngineReadmission.swift`): the re-queues of a conditions element, of a satisfied network edge (T19, R-SYN-13), of a closed breaker or a cooldown's end, and of an ended throttle, at most `readmissionsPerTurn` (512) per turn, in the order the pump serves the jobs (lane rank, then queue ticket; F-28 made the sweep's order the same), the rest owed to a follow-up turn (`readmissionFollowUp`, an owned task whose await cites RE-S21). A conditions element collects its candidates from `laneParked` under the conditions then current (one gate verdict per lane, and no pass at all while no lane is admitted); an edge and an origin event owe their re-queues to the jobs parked or failed at the event (`edgeOwed`, `originOwed`; a job leaves them when it leaves that state), so the follow-ups never re-queue a job the gate parked again: at a cooldown's end every job parked by the breaker is re-queued and all but the probe are parked again, and collecting those anew would re-queue them in every turn while the probe runs. `unblock(where:)` is gone; `apply(_:)` no longer sorts every job twice.
  3. `parkLane` parks at most what is left of the pump turn's `parksPerPump`, in queue order from the lane's queue (`LaneQueues.pending`, `dropInvalidHead`), not a sorted pass over every job; a lane it stops in is owed the rest (`laneParksOwed`), which every later pump continues first, whether or not a slot frees. A run settles blocked only once none of its claims is queued (`checkRunSettled` unchanged), so it settles at the last of those turns.
  4. `performReleaseGroup`: a group with at most `releasesPerTurn` (512) pending members is released in its turn as before; a larger one leaves `groups` at once (nothing joins it again), loses the interests of its members in flight in that turn (their slots free), and the others at most 512 per turn in the order they joined (`Group.joined`, `Release`; no sort of 10,000 transfer identifiers, which cost about 3 ms, and no split of them by a lookup each, about 4 ms), the rest owed to a follow-up turn. A job only released groups still want waits (`gate` answers `.wait`, `isReleasedOnly`), so none of the members starts between the turns (R-CAR-4). The release's follow-up turns pump only after the last member left: the members they reach wait in a queue or are parked, so they free no slot and no room.
  5. `sweepSpaceParked` re-queues at most `readmissionsPerTurn` jobs per turn and owes a follow-up sweep the rest, in the sweep's mode. The jobs sweeps re-queued count against the room until they start (`spaceAdmitted`, keyed by the ticket the sweep queued them with): the follow-up sweep, and the next read's, admit only what the room holds together, where a fresh start from zero would admit again the room the waiting jobs were admitted for (and the gate would park the surplus once a slot frees). A `.read` sweep stops once the room left cannot hold the smallest job parked `insufficientSpace`, and returns at once when none fits: the non-blocking C2 margin finding (every read sorted every parked job); the ENOSPC read at 10,000 parked members went from 6.2 to 2.9 ms.
  *Consequences.* The re-queues after an event follow the queue order, not the order of transfer identifiers. A burst of re-queues, a lane park or a release at scale spans several actor turns: the jobs reach their state at the last of them, and the engine answers every other call in between. A later free-space read admits fewer jobs than before when re-admitted jobs still wait for a slot (the behaviour change `b4fea32` pins). *Not chosen:* (a), since the turns could be bounded locally.
- **A defect in this cycle's own change, found before hand-off (R-CAR-4).** A prefetch handle registered at user-initiated priority left its released members in the user queue between the release's turns; the pump's check for waiting user work counted them, found nothing to preempt and stopped before the other queues, so other work started only after the release's last turn. Failing test `c75a3b9` (`R-CAR-4: releasing a user-initiated handle of more members than one turn releases lets queued prefetch work start in that turn`; 0 of the 2 waiting prefetch members started in the release's turn), fix `23342bd` (`hasQueuedUser` skips a job only released groups want).
- **Tests (behaviour; each fails before its fix or under its mutant).** `R-SYN-11: a later free-space read counts the jobs an earlier one re-admitted that still wait for a slot, so together they never exceed the room` (`b4fea32`; failed at `e3b36f5`: the second read re-queued three more, mutant M-ADMITTED). `R-SYN-13: when the network returns under more members parked offline than one turn re-queues, every member is re-queued` (mutant M-READMITFOLLOW: 64 members stay parked; it also fails the cycle-8 test `R-SYN-11: when the network returns under more queued members than one pump turn parks, …`). `R-SYN-11: when the volume gains room for more members parked for space than one turn re-admits, every one is re-queued` (M-SWEEPREADMIT). `R-CAR-4: releasing a prefetch handle of more members than one turn releases leaves no job and starts none of its members meanwhile` (M-RELEASEFOLLOW: interests are left; M-RELEASEGATE: three members start between the turns). The three pins (`59e2a63`) passed before the bounds too (everything happened in one turn then); each waits until no follow-up is owed, so a missing follow-up fails at once instead of overrunning. Mutant M-LANEOWED (`parkLane` stops at the bound without owing the rest) is not killed: when the network drops, nothing else can start, so a slot freed by a failed attempt stays free and the pump's own follow-up continues the lane park; the continuation decides only when other lanes take every freed slot between the turns (a policy block with user work waiting), which no test constructs (F-14).
- **The cycle-6 clock driver (blocking; L4-R8; PROBE-C8C-5).** *Reproduced* by `R-SYN-11: the clock driver of the space tests has stopped when driving returns, so it never advances a clock to a sleeper registered afterwards (2,000 rounds)` (`a087b48`; the clock moved after `driving` returned in 1,297, 1,234 and 1,393 of 2,000 rounds at `ed6f98e`; the verifier's version counted 7 and 6, its operation did not let the driver reach its wait first). Cause as the verifier found: the driver's wait for a sleeper returns on cancellation and the loop then advanced to the earliest deadline, and `driving` cancelled the driver without awaiting it. Fix `e3b36f5`: the driver returns when cancelled after its wait, and `driving` awaits it before returning. `EngineHarness.withClock` (33 uses) had the same pattern and is fixed the same way, and the live-transport test's clock loop returns on cancellation too. The test the verifier saw fail, `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable whose member waits for space fetches it as soon as a purge reclaimed the bytes, not at the store's next deadline`, now requires its setup (pending reclaim, then `y` waiting for space) instead of expecting it and waiting on, so a broken setup fails at once.
- **The breaker test (blocking; L4-R8).** *Reproduced* under 8 CPU burners on this 8-core host (`--repeat-until fail`: the old test failed at its first repetition, `syncFinished(run-1, complete(…))` 90 s of manual time after the start). The state at the failure, captured by a probe that logged each step of the drive: two attempts were still `downloading` (their 500 not yet received by the engine) while the drive advanced through the retry deadlines to the cooldown's end at t+30 s, the breaker half-opened, reopened (trips 2, until t+91 s), half-opened again, a probe closed it, and the run completed. No job was left blocked without a wake: an ordering problem of the test, not an engine liveness defect. Fix `4ec2d06`: once the breaker is open, the test waits until no transfer runs (every 500 reaches the engine without the clock, and the open breaker starts nothing), reads the cooldown's end from the engine and drives only to earlier deadlines (`EngineHarness.drive(before:)`) until the run has settled blocked; then past the cooldown as before. Same expectations. 300 repetitions passed under 16 burners (1-minute load 11.3 to 15.5). A drive cancelled by a time limit now throws `neverQuiesced` with the engine's introspection, so an overrun reports where the engine stopped; the 60 s overrun of cycle 8 itself was not reproduced.
- **The non-blocking findings.** *The 10,000-asset test's figures (both lenses):* recorded in F-17. *L4-R4 (both lenses):* no code change; for the orchestrator (L4-R4 above). *`outside-wp` (both lenses):* P-13 and P-5 (WP-B's `BenchStorage`) and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target (WP-G), L4-R11; not touched. *Residue (both lenses):* unchanged, still recorded (F-14, F-22, F-23, F-26, F-27, paragraph above F-1); the join's copy-change branch stays uncovered (F-14). *C2 margin of every free-space read (correctness):* fixed with the bounds, a read stops once the room left cannot hold the smallest job parked for space (above). *Room left by an admitted-ahead job that ends without committing (correctness):* no change; the trade-off of F-28 stands for the orchestrator (ADR-053), and the jobs earlier sweeps re-admitted now count against the room until they start (above). *f.md accuracy (requirements):* L4-R12's cycle-8 note now counts four tests (three failing under a mutant or before a fix, `0ffc058` a pin); L4-R5 and L4-R8 already carried their cycle-8 notes ("*Cycles 7 and 8:*", which the verifier's search for "Cycle 8" did not match), so they are unchanged. *Host hygiene (both lenses):* the idle `swiftpm-testing-helper` processes 27626, 52290 and 53148 belong to earlier verifiers' scratch paths, not to WP-F; not touched. Every process this cycle started (its CPU burners included, stopped by their exact PIDs) has ended.
- **Coverage of the new code (L4-R12).** Paths of the new code no test reaches, none of them a behaviour the tests above leave unpinned: an owed job that left its state before its follow-up (the stale-entry branches of `readmit`), a breaker or throttle event while re-queues are still owed (`formUnion`), the Apple tier's own lane check refusing a lane in `laneAdmitsAfterConditions`, a follow-up that cannot be spawned (the engine is shutting down) and one that finds the engine stopped. No coverage-only test was added.
- **Figures after the fix (release benchmarks of this cycle, the heartbeat's longest wait, runs of three; 1-minute load 5.38 at the start, 2.35 at the end of the benchmark run).** Re-admission when the volume gains room 4.19, 4.21 and 4.36 ms (15.54, 14.76 and 14.32 before); going offline 1.04, 1.06 and 1.07 ms (14.08, 13.70, 13.86); coming back 4.27, 4.55 and 4.55 ms (20.52, 20.04, 20.06); releasing a 10,000-member handle 4.45, 4.63 and 4.67 ms (13.24, 13.25, 12.90); parking for space 3.42, 3.11 and 3.16 ms and the ENOSPC's free-space read 3.08, 3.41 and 4.00 ms (3.0 to 6.7 before; the read stops once nothing more fits). Every WP-F turn of these benchmarks stays under half of C2's 10 ms on this host.
- **Figures at the cycle's last code commit (`0c7484b`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (Sync 248 tests in 4.098 s; the 10,000-asset test 4.095 s; 1-minute load 2.45 to 4.67). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 248 of 248 each (4.006, 4.030 and 3.995 s; the 10,000-asset test 4.004, 4.029 and 3.992 s; load 2.87 to 6.10). Under the thread sanitizer: 248 of 248, no report (the cycle-9 suite 2.376 s; `R-SYN-5: a run whose transfers open the 5xx breaker …` 2.620 s; the 10,000-asset test 16.301 s). Coverage (that `--quick`'s profile): `ZTronAssetSync lines= 92.88% (7411/7979) min=90.0 OK`; the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded (exit 0, no diagnostic). iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,042 of 1,042 passed, 0 failed (the cycle-9 suite 3.204 s; the 10,000-asset test 12.999 s); the simulator is shut down. Release benchmarks: 23 of 24 passed; WP-F's: `P-4 plan 10,000: 12.16 ms (budget 20.0)`, ratio 2.10 (max 2.5); `P-7` 0.95 and 0.39 MiB (max 48); `P-11` speedup 3.69 (min 3.0); `P-14` 0 timers, 0 tasks; R-SYN-12's stalled-subscriber prune 12.5 ms against 9.0 ms, ratio 1.40 (max 2, plus 5 ms); the four C2 benchmarks above. `outside-wp`: P-13 failed (`unit max 10.205959 ms … budgets 2.0 s, 5.0 ms per batch`, L4-R11), the only failure.
- **Dispositions of the cycle-8 findings.**

## F-30 — Close-out cycle 10: the cycle-9 verifiers' findings, each blocking one with a failing test first
- **Context.** The two verifiers of close-out cycle 9 left three blocking findings, all from the correctness lens: an actor turn over C2 when a prefetch handle registered at user-initiated priority is released at 10,000 members (PROBE-C9C-2; a regression of cycle 9's batched release), and two liveness defects present since `61b6eb9` (both fail identically at `ed6f98e`): a member paused for a policy that clears before the transport's `.paused` reaches the engine stays parked (PROBE-C9C-1a, 1b; T17), and a member whose `.offline` arrives after the network returned waits for an edge that already passed (PROBE-C9C-3; R-SYN-13 [MUST]). No new orchestrator ruling arrived (`.build-orchestrator/l4-rulings.md` read again at the start of cycle 10: L4-R1…R15 unchanged, each recorded above as "Orchestrator ruling (close-out)"). The regression tests live in `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineRegressionCycle10Tests.swift`, gated per `@Test` for both platforms (ADR-051), and in three new release benchmarks of `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchSync.swift` (WP-F's file; C2's 10 ms as in F-29, P-9's threshold from `QualityBudgets`).
- **A user-initiated handle's release (blocking; C2, R-CAR-4).** *Reproduced* by the release benchmark `R-SYN-4: releasing a 10,000-member prefetch handle of user-initiated priority holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn and leaves no job` (`65baf8a`; at `b9acb51`, release build, 1-minute load 1.7 to 2.4: 22.13, 22.16 and 22.00 ms, where the prefetch-lane release measured 4.80, 4.45 and 4.39 ms in the same run). Cause as the verifier found: the members a release has not reached yet stay queued, the gate answers `.wait` for each (`isReleasedOnly`), and every pump of the release (its own turn and one per cancelled attempt's drain, back to back) scanned all of them twice, in the user queue's pop and in the check for waiting user work. *Options.* (a) Take each such entry out of its queue the first time a scan meets it; (b) invalidate the queued members' entries in bounded batches ahead of the interest removal; (c) coalesce the drains' pumps. *Decision:* (a), in `dca73b9` (simplified in `51ef016`): `LaneQueues` keeps aside the entries a scan's caller claims (`pop` and the new `peek` take a `setAside` predicate; one rebuild of the scanned part, whatever the number removed), and the pump's pop and its check for waiting user work (`hasQueuedUser()`, which now peeks) claim the entries of jobs only released groups want (`setsAside`), so each entry is stepped over once. Such a job waits in no queue: an interest that joins it at its own lane queues it again at the front (`addInterest`; a user-initiated join or a lane change already re-queues it in `relane`), and when no release is left `continueReleases` puts back any entry set aside that is still valid (none, unless an interest of a released group outlived its release). The entries set aside count toward the queues' 2 × live bound and compaction drops the stale ones; the invariant check reports an entry set aside whose job another interest wants. The gate still answers `.wait` for a job only released groups want. A first version also set entries aside in the lane-wide park; `51ef016` dropped it: the pump's next pop sets the same entries aside, and no test could tell the two apart. (b) not chosen: it is still a pass over the members per batch; (c) not needed once each drain's pump scans nothing. *Figures after the fix* (release, this host; the benchmark run at the cycle's last code commit, below): the user-initiated release 4.12, 4.18 and 4.21 ms; the prefetch-lane release 4.52, 4.09 and 4.19 ms (4.39 to 4.80 at `b9acb51`; its first scan now sets the members aside). *Tests:* `R-CAR-4: a member of a released handle that another interest wants while the release continues starts at once` (one actor turn: the release's pump sets the member aside, a system interest at its own lane joins it, the pump must start it; mutants M-ASIDEJOIN, no re-queue on the join, and M-ASIDE, no set-aside, fail it), `R-CAR-4: a lane-wide park during a handle's release parks every other queued member and neither parks nor starts the released ones`, `R-SYN-3: lane queues set aside the entries a scan claims, keep the others in order and restore the valid ones first`, and `R-SYN-12: the invariant check reports a waiter interest outside the waiter's job and a set-aside entry another interest wants`.
- **A policy pause whose block cleared before the pause arrived (blocking; T17, R-SYN-7, R-CAR-3).** *Reproduced* by `R-SYN-7: a member paused for a policy that cleared before its pause arrived is re-queued and starts again` (PROBE-C9C-1a: a constrained path and an unrestricted one applied in one actor turn, as the watcher applies two buffered elements; at `b9acb51` the member stayed `blocked(policy(constrainedNetwork))` with one start) and `R-SYN-7: a Low Data Mode flap reported as two conditions elements never leaves a paused prefetch member parked (20 rounds)` (PROBE-C9C-1b through the conditions source; at `b9acb51` the member was left parked in the first round), committed in `5286ca8`. Fix `4e1de3f`: `paused()` decides after its sidecar read on the lane's block under the conditions then current (`laneWideBlock`): it parks the job with the block the lane gives now while the lane is still refused, and re-queues it at the front otherwise. The f-2 row now names this re-validation (L4-R13). Not chosen: clearing `policyPause` in `apply(_:)` for jobs in flight whose lane is admitted again (the verifier's optional addition): the pause has already gone to the transport, so the attempt still ends `.paused`, and the decision in `paused()` covers both orders.
- **An offline failure that arrives after the network returned (blocking; R-SYN-13 [MUST], T19).** *Reproduced* by `R-SYN-13: a member whose offline failure arrives after the network returned is retried at once, not left waiting for an edge that already passed` (PROBE-C9C-3; at `b9acb51` the member stayed `failed(whenOnline)` with one start, the path satisfied and no deadline armed), committed in `5286ca8`. Fix `5613fe6`: `EngineState.networkEdgeSerial` counts the satisfied edges `apply(_:)` applied, each attempt records it when it starts (`ActiveAttempt.edgeSerial`), and an `.offline` failure re-queues its job when an edge was applied since its attempt started and the path reads satisfied now; otherwise the job is failed `whenOnline` as before and waits for the next edge. The retry happens at most once per edge (the new attempt records the current count), so a path whose connections keep failing while it reads satisfied (F-16) still waits for an edge: no polling. `R-SYN-13: a member whose offline failure arrives after the network dropped again waits for the next edge and is retried there` pins the other order (it passed before the fix too: the fix must not start an attempt on a path that reads unsatisfied). Not chosen: the verifier's alternative, an orchestrator ruling extending L4-R3's single debounced retry to jobs outside runs; the engine-side fix needs none.
- **The non-blocking findings.** *f.md accuracy (requirements):* L4-R12's cycle-9 note now counts six tests and names `c75a3b9`, which failed before its fix `23342bd`. *`withdraw`'s pass over every job (requirements, PROBE-C9R-1):* fixed in `a1e0bc1`: a waiter's one interest is in the job it names (`Waiter.transfer`, which every reroute moves with it), so `withdraw` touches that job only; the invariant check now also reports a waiter interest held in another job, so the model test checks both directions after every operation. Measured by the new release benchmark `R-SYN-4: cancelling a resolve while 10,000 prefetch members wait holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn`: 5.28, 6.25 and 5.67 ms with the old body (measured in this session), 0.020, 0.021 and 0.019 ms after; F-14 updated. *The lane-park continuation's test gap (requirements):* `R-SYN-7: a lane-wide policy park that stops at its turn's bound reaches every queued member even when user work takes the freed slot` (`b89126c`) builds the interleaving in one actor turn and fails under M-LANEOWED (64 members stay queued); F-14 updated. *The cooldown's end at 10,000 (correctness, PROBE-C9C-4, whose window was unreliable):* measured deterministically by the new release benchmark `R-SYN-5: a breaker cooldown's end over 10,000 prefetch members parked by it holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn` (`886a2e4`): 4.91, 4.74 and 5.13 ms; F-14 updated. *Space-admitted accounting (correctness, by reading):* no change. The sweep's `ahead` stands for what the gate counts in flight for the jobs re-admitted ahead once they start, their `ledgerBytes` (the entry's size, `InFlightBytes`), which is what `headroom(for:in:)` subtracts; the larger ENOSPC figure is each job's own requirement, which the gate checks for that job when it is dispatched (`spaceBlock`). Counting it ahead would refuse jobs the gate would start; "never exceed the room" holds in the ledger's terms. *The 10,000-asset test's figures (both lenses):* recorded in F-17; no overrun. *L4-R4 (both lenses):* no code change; for the orchestrator (L4-R4 above). *Carried residue (both lenses):* unchanged, still recorded (F-14, F-22, F-23, F-26, F-27, F-28, paragraph above F-1). *`outside-wp` (both lenses):* P-13 (both), P-2 and P-5 (correctness; WP-B's `BenchStorage`) and the `ZTronAssetKit` coverage target (WP-G), L4-R11; not touched. *Confirmations (correctness):* the cycle-8 bounded turns, the cycle-6 clock driver and the breaker test; no action. *Host hygiene (both lenses):* the idle `swiftpm-testing-helper` processes 27626, 52290 and 53148 belong to earlier verifiers' scratch paths, not to WP-F; the stray `default.profraw` was moved by the correctness verifier, and the worktree was clean at the start of this cycle; not touched.
- **Coverage of the new code (L4-R12).** Of the lines cycle 10 changed in `ZTronAssetSync`, three are not reached, none a behaviour the tests above leave unpinned: the invariant check's message for a waiter interest without a waiter (the earlier check, moved into an `else`) and its filter's branch for a stale entry set aside. No coverage-only test was added; the queues' set-aside is covered by its own behaviour test (above).

Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 257 of 257 each (4.012, 4.010 and 4.034 s). Coverage (a run with `--enable-code-coverage`, all 1,051 tests passed): `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.01% (7522/8087) min=90.0 OK`; the gate's only failure is `outside-wp` (`ZTronAssetKit lines= 37.82% (205/542) min=70.0 FAIL`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded (exit 0, no diagnostic). iOS Simulator tests (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0, the assigned simulator): 1,051 of 1,051 passed, 0 failed (the cycle-10 suite 3.855 s; the 10,000-asset test 12.858 s); the simulator is shut down. ## F-31 — Close-out cycle 11: scheduling-dependent tests as a class (L4-R17), the store guard (L4-R16) and the cycle-10 items (L4-R18)
- **Context.** The verifiers of close-out cycle 10 left two blocking findings, both test flakes: `drivingStopsBeforeReturning` timed out its 10,000-yield wait for a sleeper's registration in the required iOS Simulator run (L4-C10-07), and `progressIsRateLimited` failed its lower bound in a macOS stress run because the clock moved before the engine had applied the round's update (L4-C10-08). Flakes recurred in cycles 4, 8 and 10, so ruling L4-R17 asks for the class: every WP-F test or helper that waits a bounded number of yields or rounds, asserts a lower bound on what a consumer happened to pull, or lets the clock move before the engine applied the previous step, converted to an explicit condition, census, gate or handle that ends on cancellation, without weakening an assertion. L4-R16 approves the store guard L4-R5 authorised; L4-R18 lists three clearly right non-blocking items.
- **The class fix in the helpers (L4-R17).** `EngineHarness.drive` (with `settle` and `syncDriven`), `EngineHarness.withClock` and `EngineRegressionCycle6Tests.driving` are the free-running clock drivers. Three scheduling dependencies were in them: (1) `drive` counted *rounds*, each one a wait for any change, so how many it used depended on how many intermediate states it happened to observe; it now counts clock moves (`advances:`) and waits without bound on a condition (`awaitCondition` over the introspection, the predicate and the fence) or one more sleeper, each ending on cancellation; (2) every driver advanced to the earliest sleeper as soon as one existed, so an in-flight manifest fetch's deadline (`FakeRemoteSource` answers at once) or a probe's watchdog could fire because the work had not been scheduled yet: a host-load outcome F-13's `maxStep` only covered for `syncDriven`. The drivers now never wake a deadline at or past the engine's clock fence (`SyncEngine.clockFence()`, a test seam: the in-flight fetch's deadline, an in-flight probe's watchdog), and decide the predicate, the fence and the advance in one engine turn (`readForTests`), so the fence cannot appear between the check and the move; a test that wants such a timeout holds the work and moves the clock itself (`R-SYN-6: a manifest fetch past its deadline falls back …`); (3) found by this cycle's own third Sync run (`R-SYN-6: an offline run ends blocked and a new run starts when the path is satisfied` overran its minute in-suite): `drive` read the sleeper count after an engine hop, so an alarm that registered its sleep in between was counted as already there and never woken. The count is now read in the same engine turn, before the deadline (`4e5beb8`; the same in `withClock` and `driving`). No production `await` changed; one production line: a progress update the rate limit holds back now calls `notifyObservers()` (`26557e6`), because `awaitCondition` promises a re-evaluation after every mutation and that one published nothing (the progress test's per-round wait depended on it).
- **The two blocking flakes.** *L4-C10-07:* `driving` gained test hooks (`DrivingHooks`: the driver's wait registered, the driver ended); each round's operation returns once the driver waits, then the test waits for the sleeper's registration or its wake (whichever first), then for the driver's end, and asserts the driver had ended when `driving` returned (`running`), that the clock did not move and that the sleeper registered. No yield is left in the test. The cycle-8 driver (no cancellation guard, not awaited; mutant M-C8DRIVER) fails it in every run: `running` 2,000 of 2,000 and `stale` 1,736, 1,749 and 1,776 of 2,000 (three runs; the yield version caught 6 or 7 of 2,000). *L4-C10-08:* each round now waits until the engine applied its update (`active.bytesReceived`) before the clock moves, and until a trailing emission the move made due was flushed (no `.progress` deadline at or before now) before the next update; the collector-drained wait stays. The count is then exact: one emission at the start and one per elapsed 0.1 s interval, 11 for 50 rounds of 0.02 s; the test asserts `== 11` besides `>= 2` and the 10 Hz ceiling.
- **The enumeration (L4-R17).** Every WP-F test file and helper was read for the three patterns (`Task.yield`, bounded loops and rounds, `advance`/`sleepDevice`, the free-running drivers, `EventProbe` and stream collectors, `received`, `>=` bounds; 262 Sync tests). Converted:

| `EngineHarness.withClock` (31 calls) | advance racing an in-flight fetch or probe | clock fence in one engine turn, waits for the fenced work or one more sleeper (count read in the same turn) |
