Reviewed and kept, each with the reason no scheduling decides its outcome: `EngineModelTests.randomOperations` (a `Task.yield()` every 64 operations is ADR-051's cooperative yield of a CPU-bound loop, not a wait; its clock moves race the engine by design and every assertion is an invariant of every interleaving; *corrected in close-out cycle 13:* the stranded-pending-run check was not until `a83ecdc`, row above; *and in cycle 14:* nor during the alarm turn's store work until `027c79d`, row above); `EngineConditionsTests.retryAfterHoldsTheOrigin` and `EngineRecoveryTests.breakerBlockedPrefetchResumesAtCooldown`'s advance (the census is followed by an assertion of the exact deadline, or the advance targets a deadline read from the engine; `rearm` keeps an unchanged target); `EngineRegressionRound2Tests.openBreaker` and `EngineOutcomeTests.transferProbePublishesHalfOpen` (the open breaker is awaited before any clock move; admission half-opens lazily from the clock, F-4); `EngineRegressionTests.offlineRunWaitsForAnEdge` (advances after the pending run's deadline; the 3,600 s move follows the assertion that nothing is armed); the store-clock advances of cycles 6 and 8 (the engine's clock never moves; the test runs the store's due work itself); `EngineStoreTests.urlsOutliveTheirGrace` (one-second advances with job waits: a later commit only delays the grace, so "still readable" holds in every order), `maintenanceStopsAtDeadline` and `EngineOutcomeTests.maintenanceKeepsPublicationInFlight` (advance after a held-unit census), and the advances after a `shutdown` (`pinsSurviveEviction`, `retentionScopeBoundsRunsAndEviction`, `noMutationAfterClose`) or before a run whose outcome no deadline order changes (`evictionNotPermitted`, `ensureAvailableEvicts`); the probe assertions read after `clientShutDown` whose event is the last of its slot before shutdown (`snapshotChanged` is published only by an apply's follow-up, a catch-up and a purge): `purgeDuringApply`, the prune counts of cycles 4 and 5, `ensureAvailableApplyFollowsPrunes`, `failedApplyIsFollowed`, `diagnosticsGenerationNeverLags`, `catchUpAnnouncesPrunes`, `olderViewIsNotFollowed`, `refusedViewIsReadAgain`; the `probe.next` waits for events no later event of their slot can follow before the test's next step (launch `syncStarted`, the breaker's open and close, the last run's `syncFinished`, `conditionsChanged`; until cycle 12 this list also held "the live transport's monotone progress", wrongly: see the table's cycle-12 rows); the bounds that are not pulled by a consumer (`diagnosticsAndMetrics`' `manifestRequests >= 1` holds whether `sync` joined the launch run; `ensureMaterialisesInBatches`' peak is sampled on the actor after every mutation; `boundedParallelism`' peak is reached in the turn the pump fills every slot; the stalled subscribers of `stalledSubscriberIsBounded` and `clientShutDownIsLast` never pull); and the live-transport test's own clock loop (no fetch in flight, and one asset's 503s cannot open the 5xx breaker, which counts distinct assets). *Added in cycle 12 (F-32), from the cycle-11 verifiers and a fresh scan:* `EngineRunPathTests.negativeCacheLimitsFetches` (the clock moves after the five resolves were answered, with nothing in flight and no sleeper involved: it only ages the negative cache); `EngineRegressionCycle10Tests.lowDataModeFlapLeavesNoMemberParked` (20 rounds, setup bound `paused > 0`: the engine's watcher is an actor-isolated `for await` (`EngineConditions.swift:52`) that registers its next `next()` in the turn that applied the previous element, and the test hops onto the engine after each round's wait and before its next send, so every round after the first sends its constrained element to a waiting consumer and pauses the member); the lower bounds `throttledManifest`'s `after > 0` and `userAndThrottle`'s `after >= 119` (retry hints computed from a clock nothing moves), cycle 7's `available >=` (the fake volume's reading), `EngineSupportTests.openUntil`'s spread (a pure function over seeds) and `releasedQueuedMembersLeaveNoStaleEntries`' setup bound (read after the awaited join, every slot held by the blockers); the loops of `concurrentShutdown` (eight concurrent calls, idempotence holds in every order), `laneQueuesStayCompact` and `hundredCallers` (each step waits on engine state); the stream collectors of `shutdownMidFlight`, `clientShutDownIsLast`, `cancelledSubscriberIsRemoved` and `releasedStreamIsRemoved` (their assertions are on the last event or on the engine's census); the new seeded model of cycle 12 (its waits are conditions that end on cancellation, its assertions invariants of every interleaving); `SyncContractTests` is not WP-F's file.
- **Stress (L4-R17: "run the Sync suite repeatedly under load").** At `8a0e007`: `--repeat-until fail`, 15 repetitions while this cycle's first iOS Simulator run went on (1-minute load 5.7 to 64.5): passed. The third of three Sync runs afterwards overran the minute of `R-SYN-6: an offline run ends blocked and a new run starts when the path is satisfied` in-suite: the sleeper count of `drive` (helper fix 3 above, `4e5beb8`); 300 repetitions of that test alone in each of six parallel processes had passed. Then 15 repetitions (load about 6): passed; four copies serialised by SwiftPM's lock, 10 repetitions each: one failure of `diagnosticsAndMetrics` (`0f7b389`). Running the test bundle directly in parallel (`swiftpm-testing-helper`, which takes no lock): six copies, 15 repetitions each, on this 8-core host (load up to 67.7) slowed a repetition from about 4 s to about 280 s; `boundedParallelism`, `oneFailureNeverAbortsTheRun` and `runAfterLedgerDropDefaultTuning` then overran their minute in some repetitions, every failure being such an overrun and its consequences, and one overrun hung its process (`db4a785`). At `db4a785`: three copies, 15 repetitions each (load up to 25.8), and two copies, 15 repetitions each, beside the final iOS Simulator run (load 59.5 to 196.4): all 75 repetitions passed. The other joins on a task (`await task.value`, about 120) were not converted: in these runs only that one outlived a cancellation, and a join that outlives a recorded overrun delays the report of a failure already recorded; noted for the verifiers.

WP-C's `R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need` retargets its failure case, as the ruling says: o9's entry changes first (stale, still 100 bytes used) and the 404 is recorded for the new content, which dooms the old blob as before; its assertions (500 bytes and five files freed, `o0`…`o4` evicted, the blob doomed `unreferenced`) are unchanged. The engine's presence read before `recordFailure` (row f-10) stays. The residual window of F-14 and F-22 is closed for `recordFailure`; `recordIntegrityFailure` is unchanged (not part of the ruling). *Corrected in cycle 12 (F-32):* closed on the store's side only: the job's waiters for an id bound during the await are still answered `notFound` (F-14).

Failing first (`2f0482d`): `R-SYN-3: members of a released handle that regain an interest keep their queue order and never pass earlier queued work`, in place (dispatched `m0547, m0520, m0574` before `early`) and set aside (`m0547, m0520, m0574`); fix `80c672d`. A re-join that changes the lane or comes from user work still goes through `relane`'s re-queue at the back (unchanged since level 4). *L4-C10-02:* the merge paragraph above now asks for the §7.1 and §7.6 amendments.

## F-32 — Close-out cycle 12: the live-transport progress waits (L4-R17), a start-order flake this cycle's simulator run found, and the cycle-11 items
- **Context.** The cycle-11 verifiers left one blocking finding: `R-STO-1: a cancelled, paused or failed foreground transfer leaves no byte outside the store root` overran its minute in the required sanitizer run, and F-31's enumeration had kept the live transport's progress waits as "monotone progress" (L4-R8, L4-R17). The non-blocking ones: no test told `restoreAside`'s merge by place from prepending (mutant RESTOREPREPEND); two items missing from the enumeration; a comment made stale by L4-R16; no C2 benchmark for re-joining a released 10,000-member handle; the model test never reaches a release over several turns (mutant M2); figures for F-17; `outside-wp` items and host hygiene. No new ruling (the rulings file is unchanged since cycle 11); the entries of L4-R8, L4-R16, L4-R17 and L4-R18 above say how this cycle applied them.
- **The blocking flake (L4-R17; `7034e65`).** *Cause:* the foreground transport reports a data task's first chunk and then only a chunk that crosses a boundary of `max(256 KiB, expected / 64)` (`ProgressQuantum`, `Sources/ZTronAssetTransport/Session/SessionDelegate+DataTasks.swift:87-88`, `:382-389`). For a 16, 32 or 64 KiB stalled prefix, the only event of at least the stall's size is the first chunk's, and only when that chunk held the whole prefix; the server writes the head and the prefix at once, more than one loopback segment, so a client read between the segments (likelier under the sanitizer or load) leaves the wait nothing to receive. *Fix:* each stalled prefix is exactly one quantum (256 KiB) and each body longer than it and at most 16 MiB (384 KiB for R-STO-1's three bodies and R-NET-8's, 512 KiB for R-SYN-15's), so the chunk that completes the prefix crosses the boundary and is reported with `received == stall` however the reads split; the reason is documented on `EngineLiveTransportTests.progressQuantum`. No assertion changed (R-NET-8's `Range == "bytes=\(stall)-"` stays). *Reproduction:* a failing test committed first is not possible inside WP-F, because splitting the server's writes needs a change to WP-H's `LoopbackHTTPServer` (L4-R1 keeps `ZTronAssetTestSupport` untouched); the probe ran in a scratch copy under `.build-f/tmp`. PROBE SPLITSTALL3 sends the same bytes as three writes 150 ms apart (the head with the prefix's first byte, the prefix but its last byte, the last byte). At `db6b3d3` all three tests overran their minute (`EngineLiveTransportTests.swift:174`, `:229`, `:299`) with the waits at `:209`, `:261`, `:266` and `:315` `<not evaluated>` (64.03 s, 7 issues): the signature the verifier reported. With the fix they pass (0.90 s), and 10 repetitions under the thread sanitizer pass (19.67 s). PROBE SPLITSTALLN (the prefix in writes of 7,919 bytes, 1 ms apart) passes 20 repetitions (7.28 s).
- **Found by this cycle's own iOS Simulator run (a flake; `2c5ac8c`).** The first simulator run (beside a 15-repetition Sync stress run, 1-minute load up to 54.5) failed 1 of 1,059: `R-SYN-11: a free-space read re-admits the jobs parked for space that the room holds together and leaves the others parked untouched` at `EngineRegressionCycle8Tests.swift:123`, its two starts recorded in reverse order. `FakeRemoteSource` records a start when the attempt's driver task reaches it, and drivers dispatched in one engine turn race there: an order no scheduling-independent test may assert, which the enumeration had missed. `R-CAR-5: the retention scope bounds bulk runs and evicts excluded entries first without deleting anything by itself` compared a run's two starts the same way. PROBE LATEFIRST (scratch copy: the first driver to reach the fake arrives 100 ms later) reproduces both failures, the sweep test's with the simulator's reversed identifiers. *Fix:* the starts are compared as a set with their count; the sweep test asserts the queue order on the engine's own dispatch order (`ActiveAttempt.startSequence`, set in the turn that dispatched both). Both pass 10 repetitions under the probe. Every other assertion on the order of `starts`, `controlLog` or `transportLog` in WP-F's tests was read: each concerns one transfer's sequential attempts, a single start, a set, concurrency 1, or controls the engine records in its own turn.
- **The enumeration completed (L4-R17).** F-31's table gained the cycle-12 rows (the three live-transport tests, `SyncBench.drive`, `SyncBench.cooldownEndWait`, the 19 benchmark condition loops, the two held-unit release loops of `EngineOutcomeTests`), and its reviewed list the reasons for `negativeCacheLimitsFetches`, `lowDataModeFlapLeavesNoMemberParked` and the other bounds, loops and collectors a fresh scan found. The scan read every WP-F test file and `BenchSync.swift` for `Task.yield`, bounded `for` and `while` loops, clock advances, `>=` and `> 0` expectations, `received`, stream collectors, and the order of starts, controls and transport events. The benchmark helpers (`0910c3a`) now follow the Sync tests' clock-driver rule: `SyncBench.drive` decides the predicate, the clock fence (a copy of the Sync tests' `clockFence()` seam, which the benchmark target does not link), the sleeper count and the advance in one engine turn and bounds the clock's moves, not its waits; `cooldownEndWait` waits for a running member each round and is bounded by the failures injected; every condition loop ends on cancellation. Two loops that released held units (`resolveDuringMigration`, `maintenanceKeepsPublicationInFlight`) end on cancellation too (`6e00c57`).
- **The non-blocking items.** *`restoreAside` (`e9be085`):* `R-SYN-3: restoring set-aside lane-queue entries merges them by place, behind a front push made after the scan`: entries 1 and 2 set aside by a scan that finds 3, a preempted entry pushed to the front and one appended after the scan; mutant RESTOREPREPEND gives `[1, 2, 9, 3, 4]` instead of `[9, 1, 2, 3, 4]`. *The model over several release turns (`e9be085`, cost cut `76efaa6`):* `R-SYN-3: seeded random releases and re-joins of overlapping handles larger than one release turn keep every engine invariant and leave no job` (PROBE-C11C-2 as a test, four seeds, 100 operations): `2 × releasesPerTurn + 100` on-demand entries and three slots; handles of up to the whole catalogue registered (each join awaited, so the handle released later has the size the seed chose) and released, re-joined in the release's own turn (system interests at its lane for members the first release turn does not reach and no live handle holds), raced with a registration over the same members, and callers at every lane; invariants after every operation, and liveness at the end (every handle released, the jobs left and the callers' assets scripted to stage, no job, no release owed, every caller answered with its asset). Mutant M2 (a `reinstate` that bumps the ticket and queues no entry) fails every seed, at operations 11, 21, 5 and 8 ("4", "6", "4" and "7 queued jobs wait in no queue"), while `EngineModelTests.randomOperations` passes under it. *The stale comment (`205e5f7`):* the comment at `forgetBound` after `recordFailure` now says that the store's guard leaves such an id present and that the job's waiters are still answered `notFound`; that residual is recorded in F-14 and F-22, and F-31's L4-R16 paragraph is corrected. *The re-join benchmark (`0c3f0b3`):* `R-SYN-3: re-registering the members of a released 10,000-member handle holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn` (PROBE-C11C-3 as a benchmark, prefetch and bulk). The release build measures 7.42 and 7.36 ms medians (runs 7.33 to 7.68 ms), against 4.21 ms for the release alone. That is within C2 with about 2.3 ms of margin, which the benchmark now watches.
- **Mutants and probes run in this cycle (scratch copies only, restored and compared clean).** SPLITSTALL3, SPLITSTALLN and LATEFIRST as above; RESTOREPREPEND fails only the new pin (the cycle-11 suite and the new model pass under it, as the verifier found for the other suites; that model's run predates its cost cut); M2 fails the new model in all four seeds and the cycle-11 `rejoinedMembersKeepTheirPlaces` in both places.
- **Figures at the last code commit (`76efaa6`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (1,059 tests: Sync 264 in 5.687 s; 1-minute load 22.3 to 7.1). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 264 passed in 5.360, 5.225 and 5.252 s; under the thread sanitizer: 264 passed in 18.470 s, no ThreadSanitizer report. Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.00% (7613/8186) min=90.0 OK` (`ZTronAssetKit` 37.82 %, `outside-wp`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator 897F9D8D (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0), beside a 15-repetition Sync stress run: result Passed, 1,059 of 1,059; the simulator is shut down. Stress: `--repeat-until fail`, 15 repetitions, passed beside each of two simulator runs (at `0910c3a`: 210.0 s, load 4.1 to 54.5, while the first simulator run hit the start-order flake; at `76efaa6`: 202.5 s, load 3.1 to 53.9). Release benchmarks (`.build-f-perf`; 1-minute load 3.3 at the start, 3.4 at the end): 27 of 28 passed. Every WP-F benchmark passed: P-4 11.81 ms (ratio 2.17); P-7 0.91 and 0.14 MiB; P-11 speedup 3.18; P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled prune ratio 1.33; the space parking 3.59 and 3.70 ms; the re-admission 4.70 ms; the network edges 1.05 and 4.66 ms; the 10,000-member releases 4.21 and 4.23 ms; the re-joins 7.42 and 7.36 ms; the cancelled resolve 0.02 ms; the cooldown end 5.54 ms. P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed with a unit of 6.44 ms against 5 ms.
- **No production `await` added** (no REENTRANCY row); the one production change is a comment. No edit outside WP-F's files.

## F-33 — Close-out cycle 13: the 10,000-asset sync's overrun under default-QoS load, an invariant that raced the alarm, and the cycle-12 items
- **Context.** One blocking finding from the cycle-12 requirements verifier: with the 1-minute load held near 25 by 16 `yes` processes on this 8-core host, `R-SYN-12: a stalled subscriber's backlog never exceeds one slot per progress key and subject during a 10,000-asset sync` overran its four minutes in the required sanitizer run (L4-R8: a flake, blocking). Non-blocking: the re-join benchmark never reached `reinstate` or `absorb`; the live-transport tests copied the transport's progress quantum; time-budget figures; `outside-wp` items; host hygiene. This cycle's own stress and simulator runs found a second flake (below).
- **The overrun (blocking; `2640a2c`).** *Reproduced* at `500022a`: in-suite under the sanitizer beside 16 burners (load 24.9 to 29.4), "Time limit was exceeded: 240.000 seconds" at 258.139 s. *Measured first* (a scratch probe, never committed: a counting `swift_task_enqueueGlobal` hook and timestamps on the driver's steps): per transfer 3.91 global enqueues on a quiet host, each a hand-off that wakes a pool thread (1.92 rescheduling the engine, 1.93 the store, 0.06 the fake source; 3.0 executor units per publication), and 4.48 to 4.50 beside the burners. The 10,000 transfers after the plan took 235.7 s at `maxConcurrentTransfers` 8, 226.4 s at 16 and 300.7 s at 1 (load 21 to 27): latency-bound, and parallelism barely helps. Per transfer at concurrency 8: 27 ms from the spawn to the driver, 63 ms for the presence read, 42 ms for the source's start call (the fake already stages in that call), 18 ms for the stream's first element, 186 ms for the store's publication. The decisive comparison: with the 16 burners at utility QoS (`taskpolicy -c utility`) and the same load (21 to 23) the transfers took 54.3 s; at default QoS 245.0 s. The run's drivers, the run task and the alarm run at utility priority (`OwnedTaskKind.priority`: bulk lane, sync-1 S1-17); the sampled pool threads were `com.apple.root.utility-qos.cooperative`, and while default-QoS processes saturate the cores the scheduler gives that class a starvation share, so each of the about 45,000 hand-offs waited about 5 ms. An owned task whose operation is isolated to the engine (one hop fewer per spawn on paper) changed nothing (260.4 s, 4.50 enqueues per transfer: the compiler already elides that hop) and was not kept. *Options:* (a) `maxConcurrentTransfers` 16 (4 % at most, above); (b) fewer hops in the fake path (the fake already stages in the start call; the hops left are the store's units and the engine's calls through the contract, WP-B's design and the Model's); (c) a larger time limit (L4-R8 allows one only with a cost cut, and at 245 s its 50 % rule asks for more than 8 minutes); (d) run the test engines' owned tasks at the default priority, the class of the load. *Decision:* (d). `EngineState.ownedTaskPriorityFloor` (a test seam, `nil` in production) puts a floor under `OwnedTaskKind.priority` in `spawn`; `EngineHarness.start()` and `LiveEngineHarness.start()` set it to `.medium` (`EngineHarness.ownedTaskPriorityFloor`) before the engine starts. The lanes' priorities above the floor are kept (user-initiated drivers stay higher); no assertion depends on a task's priority and no test reads one. Raising the 10,000-asset test alone was tried and rejected: in-suite beside the burners it took 45.9 s, but the utility-priority tests it then outran slowed down (the multi-turn release model 53.1 s, `R-SYN-6: one failing asset among 100 never aborts the sync and is listed in the report` 55.4 s, 92 % of its minute) against 28.8 and 26.4 s at `500022a`; with every harness engine raised they took 24.8 to 26.0 s and 9.8 s. *Figures:* F-17, cycle 13: 43.1 to 45.0 s in-suite beside the burners (18 to 19 % of the limit), alone 32.6 s. *Consequences:* the harness engines no longer reproduce the production gap between utility and default priority, which only a scheduling-dependent test could rely on (L4-R17 rules those out). The benchmarks keep the production priorities: they build their engines without the harness.
- **A pending run the clock opened (found by this cycle's runs; failing test `4dfaaa6`, fix `a83ecdc`).** The first 15-repetition Sync stress run, beside the first iOS Simulator run (load up to 167), and that simulator run (1,058 of 1,059) both failed `R-SYN-2: 10,000 seeded random operations never leave the transition table and keep every engine invariant` at seed 155373 operation 131: "the pending run's throttle gate is open but no run started". The model checks the invariants after every operation, and a clock move is one: it opens a throttle gate (or ends a breaker's cooldown), and the run starts in the alarm's next turn, when the alarm fires the deadline the move made due (`.pendingRun` at the throttle's end, `.breaker` at the cooldown's end). A check between the move and that turn reported a run the engine was about to start: the invariant, not the engine, was wrong for that interleaving, so F-31's reviewed list was wrong to call every assertion of the model an invariant of every interleaving. The raised priorities made the interleaving likelier; nothing excluded it before. *Test:* `R-SYN-2: the invariant check accepts a pending run whose gate the clock opened before the alarm's turn starts it` (throttle and breaker cases) moves the clock to the gate's deadline and checks the invariants in the same engine turn, then lets the alarm start the run; both cases failed with the model's message. *Fix:* `strandedPendingRun` leaves the run to the alarm while that deadline is due and still in the heap with the alarm armed (`startsInAlarmTurn`); a run that the alarm's turn does not start is reported at the next check, the deadline gone by then. The invariant oracle (`R-SYN-12: the invariant check reports every broken engine invariant it guards`) still reports its injected open gate. Afterwards the 15-repetition stress run passed (265 tests, 169.0 s, load up to 155) beside a simulator run that passed 1,060 of 1,060. *Incomplete, completed in cycle 14 (F-34):* the alarm clears `state.alarm` before it fires what is due, so a gate the clock opened while its turn ran the store's due work was still reported.
- **The L4-R17 enumeration.** F-31's table gains this cycle's two rows (the model's invariant check after a clock move; the harness engines' duration under load), and its reviewed list is corrected for `EngineModelTests.randomOperations`. A scan of the tests that call `invariantViolations()` found no other time-dependent invariant: only `strandedPendingRun` reads the clock (through `pendingRunGate`).
- **The re-join benchmark (non-blocking; `364f031`).** The cycle-12 verifier counted no `reinstate` in 8 runs: the new handle's join task reads the store before its first batch, and by then the release's 512-member turns had passed every member, so F-32's "merged back at its place (`reinstate`, `LaneQueues.absorb`)" was not true of that benchmark. Now the release's own turn and each of its later turns are met by one join batch (`materializationLimit`, 24 members, what a handle's join task adds per turn) from the end of the release order, as system interests followed by a pump; the benchmark counts the members reinstated (432 in every run), requires some in each run, and then registers the handle over all identifiers as before. Release build: medians 4.62 and 4.61 ms (runs 4.43 to 4.90 ms), within C2. Mutant REJOINPERENTRY (an `absorb` per reinstated entry): medians 4.56 and 4.39 ms, also within C2: at a join batch of 24, 24 merge passes over the about 10,000 queued entries cost less than the scenario's longest turn, so a per-entry merge is no C2 hazard at this scale. R-SYN-3's order stays pinned by `rejoinedMembersKeepTheirPlaces` (cycle 11).
- **The progress quantum (non-blocking; `f5826fe`).** The live-transport tests read `ProgressQuantum.minimum` through `@testable import ZTronAssetTransport` (already a test-only dependency, ARCHITECTURE §13.5 #1) and size each body from it (one quantum plus 128 or 256 KiB: 384 and 512 KiB today). With the quantum set to 512 KiB in a scratch build (restored), the three tests pass.
- **Not changed.** The 10,000-asset test keeps `maxConcurrentTransfers` 8 (16 gained nothing measurable), its `.timeLimit(.minutes(4))`, its asset count and its assertions.
- **Production changes.** Two, in WP-F's files: the priority floor (`EngineState.ownedTaskPriorityFloor`, read by `spawn`) and the invariant check (a test seam). No `await` added, so no REENTRANCY row; no contract signature changed; no edit outside WP-F's files.
- **Figures at the last code commit (`a83ecdc`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (1,060 tests: Sync 265 in 5.096 s; load 38.0 falling to 6.5). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 265 passed in 5.022, 4.951 and 5.057 s; under the thread sanitizer: 265 passed in 18.897 s, no ThreadSanitizer report; beside 16 burners 44.953 s, no report. Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.06% (7641/8211) min=90.0 OK` (`ZTronAssetKit` 37.82 %, `outside-wp`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator 897F9D8D (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0), beside a 15-repetition Sync stress run: result Passed, 1,060 of 1,060; the simulator is shut down. Release benchmarks (`.build-f-perf`; load 4.8 at the start, 3.9 at the end): 27 of 28 passed. Every WP-F benchmark passed: P-4 11.91 ms (ratio 2.09); P-7 0.94 and 0.17 MiB; P-11 speedup 3.44; P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled prune ratio 1.37; the space parking 3.56 and 3.20 ms; the re-admission 4.86 ms; the network edges 1.10 and 4.52 ms; the releases 4.62 and 4.40 ms; the re-joins 4.62 and 4.61 ms; the cancelled resolve 0.02 ms; the cooldown end 5.56 ms. P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed with a unit of 7.69 ms against 5 ms.

## F-34 — Close-out cycle 14: the invariant check during the alarm's store work, an alarm invariant, and the owned-task priority table
- **Context.** One blocking finding from the cycle-13 correctness verifier: F-33's exemption (`startsInAlarmTurn`) excused an open throttle or breaker gate only while `state.alarm != nil`, but `alarmLoop` clears the alarm before `fireDue`, and a due store deadline suspends that turn in `store.runDueWork()` (and in the free-space read after reaped bytes). A clock move in that window opens the gate, and the next check reported "the pending run's throttle gate is open but no run started" for an engine that starts the run once the turn resumes (PROBE-ALARMSTOREWORK, deterministic; seeds 155605 and 155545 of the model test with a store whose due work takes 50 ms, every time). Non-blocking: the exemption trusted any armed alarm (optional fix); the model test reaches none of the mutants PENDING-NOFIRE, REARM-NO-EARLIER, ALARM-STUCK (optional liveness phase); no test pins the S1-17 priority table (optional); confirmations of cycles 12 and 13's fixes; time-budget figures; `outside-wp`; host hygiene.
- **The window during the alarm's store work (blocking; failing test `94fcd17`, fix `027c79d`).** *Test:* `R-SYN-2: the invariant check accepts a pending run whose gate the clock opened while the alarm's turn runs the store's due work` (throttle and breaker cases) is PROBE-ALARMSTOREWORK without its bounded yield loops (L4-R17): a publication outside any run opens the store's commit window (`commitInterval` 2), a blocked run leaves the pending run, and the test waits on the engine for both deadlines; the metadata executor (`SteppedBlockingExecutor`) then holds the commit unit, the clock reaches the store deadline, and the test waits for the held unit (`requireHeldUnits`, which throws on cancellation), requires the window (`alarm == nil`, `storeWorkRuns == 1`, no run, the pending run), moves the clock to the gate's deadline and checks the invariants in that one turn; then it releases the store and waits for the run. Before the fix both cases failed at `:82` with the model's message, 40 of 40 in 20 repetitions, with no other issue. *Fix:* `startsInAlarmTurn` also excuses the gate while an alarm turn's store work is under way (`storeWorkInFlight`). That turn re-arms at the heap's earliest deadline before it returns (`setStoreDeadline`, then `pump()` and `rearm()`, with no await after its store block), and that alarm starts the run; a run it still leaves pending is reported at the next check. `storeWorkRuns` is raised only in `fireDue`'s store block (`alarmLoop` is `fireDue`'s only caller), and its `defer` covers both awaits.
- **Only a live, timely alarm excuses an open gate (non-blocking, the verifier's optional fix; `027c79d`, test `38f7b12`).** The armed-alarm case now also requires the alarm's owned task to be registered (`state.tasks[alarm.task] != nil`) and its deadline at or before the gate's. Both hold for every alarm the engine arms: each of the twelve `heap.schedule` sites calls `rearm()` in the same turn, or runs inside `fireDue`, which re-arms before it returns or is in its store work; and an alarm's task ends only after it cleared `state.alarm`, found its token stale, or was cancelled by `rearm` after the clear (or by shutdown, when nothing is checked). *Test:* `R-SYN-2: an open pending-run gate is excused only by a live alarm armed at or before its deadline` replaces the engine's alarm, in the turn that moves the clock to the throttle's end, first by an alarm whose task is gone and then by one later than the gate's deadline, checks each, and puts the engine's alarm back. Mutant ANY-ALARM (the cycle-13 check, `state.alarm != nil`, with the store-work case kept) failed it at `:130` and `:131` at `38f7b12`, and at `:137` and `:139` at `8f66c47`; the other tests of cycles 13 and 14 and the oracle pass.
- **The alarm as an invariant (non-blocking, the model test's reach; `e39931d`, oracle `8f66c47`).** Instead of the optional liveness phase, a new invariant (`unfiredDeadline`): while running and outside an alarm turn's store work, a non-empty heap has a live alarm armed at or before its earliest deadline. The model checks it after every operation, whatever the deadline (commit windows every 0.25 s, installs every 0.1 s, retries, progress), so it no longer depends on reaching a throttle-gated pending run. *Mutants:* REARM-NO-EARLIER (an armed alarm never moves earlier) is now named by the model test, "seed 155373 operation 114: [\"the alarm is armed after the earliest deadline (store)\"]"; before, it passed the model. ALARM-STUCK (the verifier's: the alarm returns without firing or clearing while a *throttle* pending run waits) still passes the model at its seed, which reaches a throttle-gated pending run rarely. The dedicated tests' final waits (the cycle-13 test and the two alarm tests above) now also end when the alarm's task ends with the alarm still armed, and then assert that the run started and the invariants hold: at `8f66c47` ALARM-STUCK fails the cycle-13 throttle case at `:75` and `:77` and the cycle-14 alarm test at `:147` and `:149` at once, instead of at their time limits, naming "no live alarm will fire the earliest deadline (throttle(…))" beside the stranded gate; the cycle-14 store-work throttle case still meets it only at its time limit (its setup waits for the commit unit, which the stuck alarm never submits). The invariant oracle (`R-SYN-12: the invariant check reports every broken engine invariant it guards`) gains a due deadline with no alarm and one with a later alarm; mutant NO-UNFIRED (the check removed) fails the oracle at `:466` and `:473` and the cycle-14 alarm test. False positives: a sweep of 60 model seeds (200001 to 200060, debug) found no violation, and every Sync run of this cycle (below) passed. *Not done:* the liveness phase itself. The model ends with transfers that only its script or `release` finishes, interests outliving the operations, and conditions that may refuse bulk work; a liveness end would need every held transfer resolved and the conditions reset, a second model. PENDING-NOFIRE (the gate's deadline fires and does nothing) stays pinned by the cycle-13 throttle case.
- **The owned-task priority table (non-blocking, the verifier's optional fix; `f4e7c5b`).** `spawn`'s floor rule moved into `OwnedTaskKind.priority(floor:)` (pure, same behaviour). `R-SYN-3: owned tasks are spawned at their lane's task priority, and the test harnesses' floor only raises it` pins the table for every kind, and the rule with no floor (production), under the harnesses' `.medium` floor and under `.background`. Mutants: `min` for `max` (17 issues), the bulk driver at `.medium` (3 issues). That `spawn` reads the floor stays observable only through the loaded sanitizer duration (FLOOROFF, cycle-13 requirements verifier): no test reads a task priority, and the benchmarks keep production priorities.
- **The L4-R17 enumeration.** F-31's table gains two rows (the model's store-work window; the alarm tests' final waits), and its reviewed list's correction for `EngineModelTests.randomOperations` names this cycle's. The new tests' waits are engine conditions, the executor's held-unit census and the harness's `syncDriven`, each ending on cancellation; no bounded yield, no consumer-pulled bound, and every clock move is made in the engine turn that checks or after the engine reached the state it moves from. *Corrected in cycle 15 (F-35):* not so. `syncDriven`'s first wait could be met by the launch run and its join did not end on cancellation; the store-work test published, read the store's deadline and moved the clock while a store deadline the drive had made due was still to fire, and checked its window while the blocked run's watcher could still apply its first element. Rows in F-31's table.
- **Stress (L4-R17).** At `e39931d`, `--repeat-until fail` with 15 repetitions beside the first iOS Simulator run (load 10.6 to 317.1): passed in 185.311 s; at `8f66c47` beside the second (5.6 to 272.5): passed in 176.454 s.
- **Not changed.** No `.timeLimit`, asset count or assertion was lowered; the three tests whose final wait changed gained assertions. `ZTronAssetSync`'s production behaviour is unchanged: the two invariant checks are test seams, and `priority(floor:)` computes what `spawn` computed.
- **Production changes.** In WP-F's files only: `startsInAlarmTurn` and the new `unfiredDeadline` (`EngineCommands.swift`, test seams), `OwnedTaskKind.priority(floor:)` (`EngineLifecycle.swift`). No `await` added, so no REENTRANCY row; no contract signature changed; no edit outside WP-F's files.
- **Figures at the last commit (`8f66c47`; run in this session after it).** `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (1,063 tests: Sync 268 in 5.079 s). Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete!`. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`, `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281`. `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times: 268 passed in 4.944, 4.906 and 4.936 s; under the thread sanitizer: 268 passed in 19.248 s, no ThreadSanitizer report. Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.04% (7662/8235) min=90.0 OK` (`ZTronAssetKit` 37.82 %, `outside-wp`, L4-R11). iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator 897F9D8D (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0): result Passed, 1,063 of 1,063; the simulator is shut down. Release benchmarks (`.build-f-perf`; 1-minute load 17.2 at the start, 4.0 at the end): 27 of 28 passed. Every WP-F benchmark passed: P-4 11.92 ms (ratio 2.10); P-7 0.89 and 0.11 MiB; P-11 speedup 3.66; P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; the stalled prune ratio 1.41; the space parking 3.65 and 3.22 ms; the re-admission 4.46 ms; the network edges 1.08 and 4.39 ms; the releases 4.14 and 4.15 ms; the re-joins 4.44 and 4.46 ms, with [432, 432, 432] members re-joined in each lane; the cancelled resolve 0.02 ms; the cooldown end 5.55 ms. P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed with a unit of 8.31 ms against 5 ms. Loaded figures in F-17.

## F-35 — Close-out cycle 15: a manifest 429 that moved the throttle's deadline earlier, three test races, and the launch-run join as a class
- **Context.** Four blocking findings from the cycle-14 verifiers. Correctness: a manifest 429 shorter than a transfer's moved the origin's `.throttle` deadline earlier; the alarm dropped it with the throttle still in force, so the job the throttle parked was never re-admitted and the pending run looped without the clock moving (PROBE-THROTTLEEARLY: 302 manifest requests in 0.3 s; R-SYN-5, R-SYN-13, R-SYN-14). Requirements (L4-R8, L4-R17): the cycle-14 store-work test overran its minute in 5 of 69 stress iterations (two causes), its window check raced the blocked run's conditions watcher, and `syncDriven` drove the wrong run and joined it uncancellably (a hung test process). Non-blocking: confirmations of cycle 14's fixes and of its mutants, time-budget figures, `outside-wp` items, host hygiene. No new ruling.
- **The throttle's deadline (blocking; failing tests `fa3d472`, fixes `98db32e`, `294672a`).** *Cause:* `performManifestFetch` kept the later end in `throttles[origin]` (the max, as the transfer path does) but scheduled `.throttle(origin)`, and reported the run's `.throttled(until)`, at its own end; `heap.schedule` replaces the key, so the deadline moved earlier. At that end `fireDue` found the throttle still in force and dropped the deadline (`continue`). Only that deadline re-admits the jobs a throttle parked (`unblockOrigin(.throttled)`), so the job stayed parked, and a run that planned it settled blocked by the throttle and re-armed its pending run at the throttle's end, already past. *Tests:* `R-SYN-5: a manifest 429 shorter than the origin's throttle leaves the throttle's deadline at its end, where its parked job resumes and no run repeats` (PROBE-THROTTLEEARLY without its prints, over `HeldManifestSource`, a held-manifest source whose waits end on cancellation; the clock moves only from a quiet engine) and `R-SYN-5: a throttle deadline that fires before the origin's throttle ends is kept at the throttle's end, where its parked job resumes` (an earlier deadline put into the heap, the state the manifest path left). At `fa3d472` both failed, with 11 issues: the deadline at 2 s for a throttle ending at 61 s, the run reporting 2 s, 20 runs and 20 manifest requests after the throttle's end without the clock moving (22 in all), the job still `blocked(throttled(until: 61 s))`, both callers answered `.shutDown`. *Fix:* the manifest path schedules the deadline and reports the block at the throttle's end (`98db32e`); `fireDue` keeps a `.throttle` deadline that fires before the throttle's end at that end instead of dropping it (`294672a`, defence in depth for any path that extends a throttle without moving its deadline). *Not done:* the optional unblock in `setPendingRun(.throttle)` for a throttle recorded as ended. The case it covers, jobs parked by a throttle that has ended with nothing left to re-admit them, no longer arises: the alarm turn that fires the deadline at the throttle's end clears the throttle and owes every parked job a re-queue in that turn (the invariant below). *(Corrected in close-out cycle 16, F-36: a run did settle blocked by a throttle that had ended, through claims a re-admission turn still owed a re-queue; after `c3f7fd0` it no longer can, so the unblock stays not done.)* *(Corrected again in close-out cycle 19, F-39: a run also settled blocked by a throttle that had ended when its last working claim ended after the throttle's end and before the alarm's turn that clears it; after `69aa023` such a claim keeps the run unsettled until that turn, so the unblock still stays not done.)*
- **A throttle liveness invariant (`6b8952f`).** The model test checked no throttle liveness. A new invariant (`unwakeableThrottleParks`): while running, a job parked by the throttle and not owed a re-queue (`originOwed`) has the origin's `.throttle` deadline in the heap. Every path that parks a job by the throttle schedules that deadline in the same turn (`park`), and the alarm removes it only in a turn that either keeps it at the throttle's end or clears the throttle and owes every parked job a re-queue. The cycle-15 tests show the check naming the dropped deadline ("nothing will re-admit the 1 jobs the throttle parked", with both fixes reverted, and on an injected removal); mutant MUT_NOINVARIANT fails the second test. False positives: none in any run of this cycle (the model test runs 10,000 operations with throttled transfers in every Sync run, stress repetition and simulator run below).
- **The store-work test (blocking; `1dc5476`).** *(B, C):* after `syncDriven()` the test waits until no store work runs and no store deadline is due (the launch run's last store deadline, made due by the drive's last move), and its setup wait requires a store deadline after the clock with no store work under way. *(The watcher):* the setup wait also requires the conditions watcher's first element applied (`watcher` and `conditions` set) and no owned task but the alarm, the watcher and a memory-pressure observer, so no late turn re-arms the alarm inside the held store work. No assertion changed. *Probes (scratch copy, env-gated, never committed):* PROBE-ALARMDELAY (the alarm takes its turn 50 ms after its sleep returns) makes the old waits overrun both cases (64.0 s, 2 issues), while the new ones pass (0.175 s); PROBE-WATCHERINSTOREWORK (the explicit run's watcher applies its first element when the alarm's store work starts) fails the old window requirement in both cases, the verifier's signature. The new wait excludes that order by construction (under that probe it waits for an element the probe withholds until the clock moves), and with the watcher delayed 100 ms (PROBE-WATCHERDELAY) it passes (0.217 s).
- **`syncDriven` (blocking; `061ec57`).** The drive starts once this call is a joiner of the run (`state.syncJoiners`) or has been answered (`untilFinishedOrCondition`: the task's end or an isolated engine condition, whichever comes first, ending on cancellation); a drive that throws cancels the call; the join is `joinCancelling(run)`, and a cancelled `sync` caller is answered `.cancelled`. *Probe:* PROBE-LATESYNC (the `sync` task starts 300 ms late) with the old `syncDriven` hung `R-SYN-14: after a sync quiesces the engine owns zero timers and zero tasks and observes nothing` with no time-limit issue (killed after 90 s: the verifier's hang); with the new one the Sync suite passes (270 tests).
- **The launch-run join, as a class (found in this cycle; L4-R17; `9f72bd3`).** PROBE-LATESYNC also failed `R-SYN-6: a run reports each asset it could not fetch with its failure class` (one case's report, three overruns): its assertions were on the launch run's report, which `syncDriven` returns only when its `sync` joins the launch run. The same holds for any `start(); sync(.force)`: `start()` resumes its caller in the turn that starts the launch run, and a caller the host delays past a launch run that needs no clock starts a second run. PROBE-LATEJOIN (`EngineHarness.start()` and `LiveEngineHarness.start()` return only once the launch run has ended, or after 3 s for a run that waits for the clock) failed nine tests: `EngineSyncTests.completesAgainstFakeSource` and `oneFailureNeverAbortsTheRun` (reports with `fetched` 0), `runFailureClasses`, `ambiguousForbidden`, `overBudgetCatalog`, `offlineRunResumes`, `retryAfterHoldsTheOrigin`, `offlineRunWaitsForAnEdge` and R-APL-3. They now read the launch run's own outcome from its `syncFinished` (`EngineHarness.startAndAwaitLaunchRun`, subscribed before the start and released before it returns); their assertions are unchanged. With the change the whole Sync suite passes under PROBE-LATEJOIN twice (6.916 and 6.809 s) and under PROBE-LATESYNC. Every other `start(); sync(.force)` site passed under the probe: a second forced run gives it the same outcome (a `.complete` report over present or on-demand assets). The benchmarks' starts were read: a `sync(.force)` after their start needs only `isComplete`, and P-4 states that its measured run may be the launch run. ## F-36 — Close-out cycle 16: a run that settled blocked by a throttle that had ended, and the old end a parked job kept
- **Context.** Three blocking findings from the cycle-15 correctness verifier, each older than cycle 15. (1) At a throttle's end the alarm re-queues at most `readmissionsPerTurn` (512) of the jobs the throttle parked, in the pump's order, and owes the rest to follow-up turns (`originOwed`). A run's bulk claim ranks last. When the run's last working claim finished before the follow-up, `checkRunSettled` counted the owed claim as parked and settled the run `.blocked(.throttled)` by the throttle that had just ended: the claim's job was retired, the pending run found its gate open, and a second run and a second manifest request followed without the clock moving (PROBE-OWEDSETTLE; R-SYN-5, R-SYN-6). (2) A user-initiated joiner of a job a shorter 429 parked was checked against the job's own end, not the origin's later one, so it waited 60 s against a 2 s bound, while a fresh request was told `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: 60)` (PROBE-STALEJOIN, the transfer and manifest paths; R-SYN-5). (3) A run that settled on its claims reported the first claim's parked end, 59 s before the origin's (PROBE-STALEREPORT). One observation to decide: a user waiter admitted under a short throttle kept waiting when a later 429 extended it (PROBE-EXTEND). Non-blocking: confirmations of cycle 15's fixes with probes and mutants, time-budget figures, `outside-wp` items, host hygiene. No new ruling.
- **Claims owed a re-queue (blocking; seam `befee7e`, failing test `ad581fa`, fix `c3f7fd0`, predicate test `7524df3`).** *Fix:* in `checkRunSettled`'s first pass, which every claim holding the run's interest goes through before the reasons pass, a parked claim that a follow-up turn still owes a re-queue counts as working (`return`). A claim is owed when it is in `originOwed` or `edgeOwed`, or when a conditions pass is owed (`readmissionsOwed` contains `.conditions`) and the claim is lane-parked in a lane the conditions admit. `OwedRequeues` mirrors what `readmit` takes in its next turn. The follow-up's `pump` calls `checkRunSettled` again. Cost: two set lookups per parked claim, plus three lane verdicts per call while a conditions pass is owed (C2). A closed breaker (`unblockOrigin(.circuitOpen)`), a network edge and a conditions element owe re-queues the same way. The predicate covers them, and its unit test pins each branch; only the throttle path is shown end to end, as the verifier did. *Corrected in close-out cycle 17:* that unit test pins `OwedRequeues.contains`, not the predicate's construction in `checkRunSettled`; two mutants of the construction (an empty `edge`, an empty `lanes`) survived every committed test, and the network-edge and conditions paths are now shown end to end as well (F-37). *Seam:* `holdReadmissionFollowUps()` and `releaseReadmissionFollowUps()` keep a follow-up's passes owed, with the turn still scheduled, until the test releases it. The verifier's probe raced that turn: its 2,100-member case failed 8 times in 8, and its 520-member case needed a 300 ms delay. The seam fixes the order instead. No production path sets it, and it adds no `await`. *Tests:* `R-SYN-6: a run whose parked claim a re-admission turn still owes a re-queue waits for it instead of settling blocked by the ended throttle` (PROBE-OWEDSETTLE with `readmissionsPerTurn` + 8 prefetch members, without its prints; every member's transfer is scripted, so the re-queued members finish). At `ad581fa` it failed in 0.341 s with 7 issues: the run had settled (`RunSettlement.finished`, after `syncFinished(run-1, blocked(throttled(until: 31000000000ns)))`); a second run had started (`2 after 1`); the claim's job was gone; one manifest request had been made since the throttle's end. At `c3f7fd0` it passed in 0.32 s. `R-SYN-6: a parked claim counts as owed a re-queue exactly when a follow-up re-admission turn will take it` is a unit test of `OwedRequeues`.
- **The parked job's old end (blocking; failing tests `ad581fa`, fixes `318b178`, `964268f`).** A parked job keeps the end it was parked with. A later 429 extends only `throttles[origin]` and moves only the `.throttle` deadline. `throttleEnd(after:)` returns the later of the origin's end and the job's. Every reader of a parked job's end now reads it there:
  - `failFast`: a user joiner gets the answer a fresh request gets.
  - `checkRunSettled`'s reasons pass, through `currentReason`: the outcome agrees with the throttle, its deadline and the pending run.
  - `blockCleared`: a lane raise no longer re-queues a job that the gate would park again at once. This changes nothing a caller can observe; the gate used to re-park the job with the origin's end in the same turn.

  *Corrected in close-out cycle 18:* the readers of a manifest fetch's `.throttled(until)` outcome were missed: a throttled sync, a fetch-first `ensureAvailable` and the unknown-id waiters answered after an earlier waiter's admission all reported the end the fetch's turn read, although a transfer's 429 may extend the throttle before they answer. They now read the end through `throttleEnd(after:)` in the answering turn (F-38). The cycle-16 correctness verifier's statement that the run's manifest outcome already used the origin's end was inaccurate. *Corrected in close-out cycle 19:* one more reader was missed: a run that settles on claims the throttle parked answers in its own task's later turn, and reported the end its settling turn read; it now reads the origin's end in that turn, and a throttle-gated pending run moves with every extension of the throttle (F-39).

  *Tests:* `R-SYN-5: a user-initiated request that joins a job a shorter 429 parked fails fast against the origin's throttle a later transfer 429 extended`, its twin for `a later manifest 429 extended`, and `R-SYN-6: a run blocked by claims a shorter and a longer 429 parked reports the origin's throttle end` (PROBE-STALEJOIN, PROBE-STALEJOIN-M and PROBE-STALEREPORT, without their prints). At `ad581fa`, on both paths, the joiner was left waiting (`!waits`) and was then answered `failure(shutDown)` by the test's shutdown, not the fresh request's `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: 60)`. The run reported 2000000000ns, against the origin's 61000000000ns.
- **PROBE-EXTEND, decided (failing tests `ad581fa` and `48a1f05`; fix `bd8c6c6`, made bounded in `7f8f3b0`; benchmark `81aac19`).**
  - *Options:* the bound applies only when the request is admitted (record that and leave the code); or re-run the fail-fast policy for user interests whenever the throttle's end moves later.
  - *Decision:* `interactiveMaxWait` bounds the whole wait, not only the request's admission. sync.md §7.6 says a user-initiated request "waits if `until − now ≤ interactiveMaxWait`", where `until` is the origin's throttle. `RetryPolicy.interactiveMaxWait` is documented as how long a user-initiated request waits for an origin throttle before it fails fast. The joiner fix above answers a request made at 1.5 s with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: 60)`; leaving a request made at 1 s waiting 60 s would contradict both texts.
  - *Fix:* when a transfer 429 or a manifest 429 moves the origin's end later, `throttleExtended(from:)` schedules a turn of its own, `answerThrottledUsers()`, so the 429's turn stays O(1). While the throttle ends beyond `interactiveMaxWait`, that turn gives the user-initiated `resolve` and `ensureAvailable` interests of the jobs the throttle parked the fail-fast answer for the origin's end. Every other interest keeps waiting.
  - *Bound:* each turn handles at most `parksPerPump` jobs, in queue order (by ticket), and owes the rest to a later turn. Each answer removes its interest, so every turn makes progress. A pump follows each turn, so an `ensureAvailable` group refills, and the gate parks and answers its next members the same way. Each turn makes one pass over `throttleParked`, never over every job.
  - *Why bounded:* the first form (`bd8c6c6`) answered every waiter in the 429's turn, about the 20 ms a pump took at 10,000 parks before `parksPerPump` existed. The first measurement of the bounded form sorted transfer identifiers, which are 66-character strings, and took 8.3 ms. The ticket order cut that to about 6 ms.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-5: a user-initiated request waiting for a short throttle fails fast once a later 429 extends the throttle beyond interactiveMaxWait` (PROBE-EXTEND); its manifest twin (`48a1f05`, 2 issues at that commit, before `bd8c6c6`); and `R-SYN-5: when a 429 extends the throttle under more user-initiated waiters than one turn answers, every waiter fails fast` (`parksPerPump` + 8 waiters).
  - *Benchmark:* `R-SYN-5: a 429 that extends the throttle under 10,000 user-initiated waiters holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn` (figures below).
- **Mutants.** Each was applied in the worktree and reverted with `git checkout -- Sources`; none was committed.
  - EDGE, which drops `edgeOwed` from `OwedRequeues`: the predicate test fails at its `edgeOwed` expectation.
  - LANES, which makes the conditions branch always false: the predicate test fails at its admitted-lane expectation.

  *Corrected in close-out cycle 17:* EDGE and LANES change `OwedRequeues.contains`, so they pin the predicate, not its construction in `checkRunSettled`; the construction's mutants are pinned by F-37's edge and conditions tests.
  - NOEXT-TRANSFER, with no `throttleExtended` call in the transfer path: both transfer-path extension tests fail, 4 issues in all: the transfer extension test (2) and the `parksPerPump` + 8 test (2), which also extends the throttle through a transfer 429; every other cycle-16 test passes. *Corrected in close-out cycle 17:* this line said only the first test failed, a note written before `7f8f3b0` added the second (the cycle-16 requirements verifier's run at HEAD).
  - NOEXT-MANIFEST: the manifest extension test fails (2 issues).
  - NOFOLLOWUP, with no later turn for the remaining answers: the `parksPerPump` + 8 test fails ("8 user requests were left waiting", "8 did not"), and the other cycle-16 tests pass.

  The state before each blocking fix is `ad581fa`, above.
- **F-35 corrected.** F-35's "Not done" said that the ended-throttle case of `setPendingRun(.throttle)` no longer arises. It did arise, through claims owed a re-queue. After `c3f7fd0`, a run settles blocked by a throttle only while that throttle is in force:
  - at the throttle's end, `unblockOrigin(.throttled)` owes every job it parked a re-queue;
  - a job leaves `originOwed` only by leaving the throttle's park (the transition funnel) or by being re-queued;
  - a claim that is owed a re-queue keeps the run unsettled.

  The optional unblock therefore stays not done. *Corrected in close-out cycle 17:* the same class reached the space gate, which this paragraph did not cover: until `6a7c395` a run settled blocked for lack of space while a follow-up free-space sweep still owed its claim a re-check (F-37). *Corrected in close-out cycle 19:* "only while that throttle is in force" did not hold. Between the throttle's end and the alarm's turn that clears it (the alarm's wake-up and its place in the actor's queue), the gate is open, nothing owes the claim a re-queue yet, and a run whose last working claim ended then settled blocked by the ended throttle; the breaker's cooldown end has the same window. Since `69aa023` such a claim counts as working until that turn (F-39).
- **L4-R17.** The new tests contain none of the three patterns:
  - Only the owed-claim test moves the clock, once, from an applied state: nothing due, no store work, and the alarm armed at or before the throttle's deadline.
  - The seam fixes the order of the follow-up turn; nothing races it.
  - Every wait is a condition, a held seam or a join that ends on cancellation.

  A row is added to F-31's table. Stress: at `81aac19`, the Sync suite with `--repeat-until fail`, 15 repetitions, beside this cycle's iOS Simulator run, passed in 193.316 s (1-minute load 5.4 rising to 174.5); the cycle-14, cycle-15 and cycle-16 suites, 200 repetitions, passed in 83.876 s (load 174.5 falling to 71.6). The same two runs at `7524df3`, before the bounded answers: 193.829 s (load 3.1 to 136.3) and 77.682 s. None recorded "Time limit was exceeded".
- **Production changes.** All are in WP-F's files:
  - `EngineRuns.swift`: `checkRunSettled`.
  - `EngineReadmission.swift`: `OwedRequeues` and the seam.
  - `EngineState.swift`: the seam's two flags and `throttleAnswersScheduled`.
  - `EngineJobs.swift`: `throttleEnd(after:)`, `currentReason`, `failFast` and `blockCleared`.
  - `EngineFailures.swift`: `throttleExtended`, `answerThrottledUsers` and its follow-up.
  - `EngineManifest.swift`: the extension call.

  The answers' follow-up awaits only the owned task's hop onto the actor (RE-S21, as the other follow-ups do; the turn re-reads the throttle, the clock and the index), so no new REENTRANCY row is needed. No other `await` was added, no contract signature changed, and no public declaration was added. The benchmark is in `BenchSync.swift`.

## F-37 — Close-out cycle 17: a run that settled blocked for lack of space while a follow-up sweep owed its claim a re-check, and the cycle-16 test-strength items
- **Context.** One blocking finding from the cycle-16 correctness verifier, older than cycle 16. A free-space read that finds room re-queues at most `readmissionsPerTurn` (512) of the jobs parked `insufficientSpace`, in the pump's order, and owes the rest to a follow-up sweep (`spaceSweepFollowUp`), an owned task of its own. When the run's last working claim finished before that sweep, `checkRunSettled` counted the run's bulk claim as parked and settled the run `.blocked(.insufficientSpace)`, although the latest read admits the claim: the settlement removed the run's interest, so the claim's job was retired; it set no pending run; and the sync caller was told the volume lacks space (PROBE-SPACEOWED; R-SYN-6, R-SYN-11, sync.md §12.5). F-36's `OwedRequeues` covered re-admission turns only. Non-blocking, from the requirements verifier: two mutants of the owed-claim check's construction in `checkRunSettled` (an empty `edge`, an empty `lanes`) and one of the throttle-extension answers (no `.ensure`) survived the whole Sync suite; P-11 missed its minimum on a loaded host; F-36's NOEXT-TRANSFER note was inaccurate; an observation to decide on a throttled `ensureAvailable`. Both lenses also sent confirmations of cycle 16's fixes, figures, `outside-wp` items and host hygiene. No new ruling.
- **Claims a follow-up space sweep still owes a re-check (blocking; seam `81813ab`, failing test `e2e5b82`, fix `6a7c395`, second test `ad24839`).**
  - *Fix, part 1:* in `checkRunSettled`'s first pass, a claim parked `insufficientSpace` counts as working while a follow-up sweep is owed (`spaceSweepFollowUp != nil`). Every sweep kind re-checks such a job against the latest read (a `.read` sweep re-checks only those), so the claim's verdict waits for the owed sweep. The rule is not narrowed to the claims the latest read admits: the follow-up decides with the room left by the jobs admitted ahead of the claim (`spaceAdmittedAhead`), which a verdict taken in `checkRunSettled` would not know. The wider rule delays a settlement by at most the follow-up chain's turns, each bounded by `readmissionsPerTurn` re-queues or `parksPerPump` parks.
  - *Fix, part 2:* `spaceSweepFollowUp()` calls `checkRunSettled()` after its sweep. A sweep that neither parks nor re-queues (its room cannot hold the smallest parked job) checks nothing itself, so without this a run kept open by part 1 would wait for an unrelated event.
  - *Cost (C2):* one optional check per `checkRunSettled` call and one more settling check per follow-up turn. No `await` was added.
  - *Seam:* `holdSpaceSweepFollowUps()` and `releaseSpaceSweepFollowUps()` keep the follow-up's sweep owed until the test releases it, the same kind of hold as `holdReadmissionFollowUps()`. The verifier's seam-free attempts never hit the order (24 iterations racing the claim's completion against the read, and 5,000- and 10,000-member variants), but production leaves it free: the follow-up is a separate task whose hop onto the actor is unordered against continuations already queued there, such as a user-initiated claim's commit completion. No production path sets the hold.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim parked for space a follow-up free-space sweep still owes a re-check waits for it instead of settling blocked for lack of space` (PROBE-SPACEOWED's held case, without its prints). At `e2e5b82` it failed in 0.127 s with 3 issues: the run had ended (`waiting.0 → nil`), the claim's job was gone (`waiting.3 → nil`), and the caller got `.blocked(insufficientSpace, SyncReport(runID: run-2 …))`. It passes since `6a7c395`. `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim parked for space the owed follow-up sweep does not admit settles blocked for lack of space once that sweep ran` pins part 2: the volume loses its room again before the held sweep runs, the sweep admits nothing, and the run settles in the sweep's turn. Both share one setup helper (`spaceOwed()`).
- **The owed-claim check's construction, end to end (non-blocking; tests `ad24839`).** `R-SYN-6: a run whose failed-whenOnline claim a network-edge re-admission turn still owes a re-queue waits for it instead of settling offline` and `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim a lane-wide park left that a conditions re-admission turn still owes a re-queue waits for it instead of settling blocked by the policy` (PROBE-C16R, without its prints): `holdReadmissionFollowUps`, `readmissionsPerTurn` + 8 prefetch members ranked ahead of the run's bulk claim, `maxConcurrentTransfers` 2; in the conditions case a user-initiated waiter keeps the working claim running under `constrained: true`. Each setup requires the owed set that holds the claim (`edgeOwed`; or the conditions pass owed with the claim lane-parked and in no other owed set). Before the release each requires the run unsettled, its id unchanged and `lastRunID` unchanged; after it, `.complete` with `fetched == 2`, no second run, and no invariant violation.
- **The `ensureAvailable` branch of the throttle-extension answers (non-blocking; test `ad24839`).** `R-SYN-5: an ensureAvailable member waiting for a short throttle is answered once a later 429 extends the throttle beyond interactiveMaxWait` (PROBE-C16R-ENSURE). The verifier's `quiet` wait cannot hold while the ensure's entry task waits, so its mutant ended at the 60 s limit. The test instead joins the user-initiated waiter of the job the first 429 parked, which the same answers turn answers under the fix and under the mutant, and then reads the member's job: it must hold no `.ensure` interest. The ensure call then returns, without the clock moving, with the scope's report (`missing == 1`, `present == 0`), and `invariantViolations()` is empty.
- **Mutants of this cycle.** Each was gated by an environment variable in the worktree, built once, run against the whole Sync suite (283 tests, before the throttled-ensure test was added), and reverted with `git checkout -- Sources`; none was committed. Without a mutant, the same build passed all 283.
  - NOEDGE (`edge: []` in `checkRunSettled`): the edge test fails, 2 issues (`waiting.0 == launchRun`; the run settled `syncFinished(run-1, blocked(offline …))`).
  - NOLANES (`lanes` always empty): the conditions test fails, 2 issues (the run settled `blocked(policy(constrainedNetwork))`).
  - NOPART1 (part 1 off, the state before the fix): both space tests fail, 5 issues.
  - NOPART2 (no settling check after the follow-up sweep): the second space test fails, 2 issues (no settlement in the sweep's turn; the run was answered only by the test's shutdown).
  - NOENSURE (`.ensure` dropped from `answerThrottledUsers`): the ensure test fails at once, 1 issue ("the member's job no longer holds the ensure interest").
- **A throttled fetch-first `ensureAvailable` (observation, decided; failing test `367bee5`, fix `9f4882d`).**
  - *Observation:* when the origin's throttle refuses `ensureAvailable`'s manifest fetch and the store cannot answer the scope, the caller is answered at once with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)`.
  - *Options:* (a) keep failing at once, as a fetch-first call whose manifest is unreachable; (b) apply the interactive wait rule to the manifest fetch: wait while the end is within `interactiveMaxWait`, else fail with the time left.
  - *Decision:* (a), with the time left reported. The manifest fetch is single-flight and nothing parks it; an unknown-id `resolve`'s point fetch answers the same refusal at once with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: until − now)` (`EngineManifest`), so failing at once is the manifest path's existing rule. §7.6's wait applies where a job is parked, which the ensure's members are once the manifest is in hand, and F-36 answers them under an extension (tested above). What was wrong is the dropped end: `unreachableError` returned `retryAfter: nil`, against §7.6's `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` for a 429 and against the `resolve`'s answer. It now reports `until − now`. *Corrected in close-out cycle 18:* `until` was the end the fetch's turn read, which a transfer's 429 may extend before the ensure answers; the time left is now read through the origin's end in the answering turn (`throttleEnd(after:)`, F-38).
  - *Test:* `R-SYN-10: an ensureAvailable whose manifest fetch an origin throttle refuses reports the time left until the throttle ends`; at `367bee5` it failed with `wait → nil`, expected 30.0.
  - *For the orchestrator:* (b) remains open to a ruling; it would need a wait and a fresh fetch at the throttle's end that honours the caller's cancellation.
- **P-11 (non-blocking).** No code change: both verifiers reproduced the miss on the pre-cycle-16 commit under the same load (figures in F-17). The concurrency-5 leg stays near 1.0 s and the serial leg varies with the host, so the margin depends on the host; if P-11 keeps missing on idle runs, its measurement needs a ruling (never a lower budget, G1).
- **F-36 corrected.** Three notes, each marked in place: the predicate test pins `OwedRequeues.contains`, not its construction (the construction is now pinned above); the EDGE and LANES mutants likewise; NOEXT-TRANSFER fails both transfer-path extension tests (4 issues), not one; and the paragraph on runs settling blocked only while their gate is in force did not cover the space gate.
- **L4-R17.** No new test moves the clock. Every wait is a condition, a held seam or a join that ends on cancellation; the ensure test's join is of a caller answered in the turn it checks, under the fix and under the mutant alike. Rows are added to F-31's table.
- **Production changes.** All in WP-F's files:

  - `EngineJobs.swift`: the seam and the follow-up sweep's settling check (part 2).
  - `EngineState.swift`: the seam's two flags.
  - `EngineCommands.swift`: `unreachableError`.

  No `await` was added, no contract signature changed and no public declaration was added.
