## F-38 — Close-out cycle 18: the clock driver's overrun, stale throttle ends from a manifest outcome, and a run that settled blocked for lack of space the ledger had
- **Context.** Three blocking findings, none from cycle 17's production change. (1) Requirements: `R-SYN-11: the clock driver of the space tests has stopped when driving returns …` (`EngineRegressionCycle9Tests.drivingStopsBeforeReturning`) overran its one-minute limit in-suite at a load of 205 to 259 ("failed after 79.044 seconds"), with no other issue: pure queueing. Its 2,000 rounds take 0.04 s alone; in the suite each of its about 12,000 pool hops waited behind other work, and the cycle-17 suite's four 520-member tests doubled that (the verifier's A/B: 3.5 to 3.7 s at HEAD, 1.7 to 1.9 s without them). L4-R8 counts an overrun as a flake. (2) and (3) Correctness: a fetch-first `ensureAvailable` and a sync run whose manifest fetch the origin's throttle refuses reported the end the fetch's turn read (`ManifestOutcome.throttled(until)`), although a transfer's 429 may extend the throttle in the awaits before they answer (PROBE-C17C-ENSURESTALE, -ENSURENATURAL: 36 stale of 300 without a seam; PROBE-C17C-RUNEND: 13 of 300), against F-36's rule that every reader reports the end a fresh request gets. Non-blocking: a waiting `ensureAvailable` answered under a throttle extension reports success with the member missing (decide); a run settles `.blocked(.insufficientSpace)` while the ledger admits its claim after a job in flight ended without committing (decide); confirmations of cycle 17's fixes with mutants and probes; time-budget figures; `outside-wp` items; host hygiene. No new ruling.
- **The overrun (blocking; `4540419`, `33860e6`).** No assertion, count or time limit changed.
  - *The clock driver's rounds* run in a task at high priority (`joinCancelling(Task(priority: .high) { await Self.drivingRounds() })`); every task a round starts inherits it. The body, its hooks and signals and the three counts are unchanged; the join ends on the test's cancellation.
  - *The cycle-17 suite's cost.* Its four 520-member tests downloaded the 512 members each sweep or re-admission turn re-queued. Each member is now held in flight once it starts (`.hold`), so the free slot holds one and the rest wait in the queue. The waits that required every re-queued member to finish (`jobs.values.allSatisfy …` over about 530 jobs, re-evaluated after every mutation) now name only the run's claims: the members are no claims of the run, so its settling check sees the same claims as before. Where the run must complete, the members' group is released after the follow-up re-queued the run's claim (which ranks after them), and the tests now also require that re-queue. `.serialized` was not needed: the suite alone fell from 1.000 s (the verifier's figure) to 0.141 s.
  - *Mutants of cycle 17, re-run against the cheaper tests* (each applied to `Sources`, built, run, reverted with `git checkout -- Sources`): NOPART1 fails both space tests (5 issues; *corrected in close-out cycle 19:* measured at `33860e6`, before `87a2cd8`; at `a494dfe` and at `fd256a0` it gives 4 issues, 1 + 3: the run's own space re-check of `87a2cd8` re-queues the claim the ledger admits in the first test, so that run no longer settles and only `waiting.3 == .blocked(.insufficientSpace)` (:133) catches it there, while the second test still fails at :172, :182 and :190; re-measured in cycle 19); NOPART2 the second (2); NOEDGE the edge test (3 issues: the run had settled, `waiting.1 == nil`; the settlement had retired the claim's job, `requeued == .queued`; the launch run did not complete); NOLANES the conditions test (the same 3).
- **Stale throttle ends from a manifest outcome (blocking; seam `9716cef`, failing tests `f01374d`, fix `974d19e`).**
  - *Seam:* `holdManifestOutcomeDelivery()` and `releaseManifestOutcomeDelivery()`. While armed, a manifest fetch that ends keeps its outcome, with the runs and `ensureAvailable` calls waiting for it (`heldManifestDeliveries`), until the release delivers it in that turn. A held waiter's cancellation withdraws it as before (`withdrawManifestWaiter`), and shutdown releases the hold and tells the waiters `.shutDown`. It adds no `await`; no production path arms it.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-10: an ensureAvailable whose throttled manifest fetch is answered after a 429 extended the throttle reports the time left until the extended end` and `R-SYN-5: a sync whose manifest fetch the throttle refuses reports the origin's end when a 429 extends the throttle before the run answers`. A prefetch transfer's 429 throttles the origin for 30 s; the fetch's outcome is held; a second, held transfer's 429 extends the throttle to 60 s; then the outcome is delivered. Each also requires that no manifest request was made, and the run test that the pending run waits for the end the run reports. At `f01374d`: the ensure answered `retryAfter` 30.0 against 60.0; the run reported `throttled(until: 31000000000ns)` against the origin's 61000000000ns, and its pending run was scheduled at the later end (2 issues).
  - *Fix:* both read the end in the answering turn through `throttleEnd(after:)`. `EngineRuns.runSteps` returns `.blocked(.throttled(until: self.throttleEnd(after: until)), …)`. `unreachableError` is now an instance method and answers through `throttledError(after:)` (`EngineJobs`, beside `throttleEnd`): `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` with the time left until the origin's end, read now.
- **The unknown-id waiters of a throttled fetch (found in this cycle while checking F-36's readers; failing test `67c0cbe`, fix `a938e6e`).** `answerUnknownWaiters` computed the failure for a throttled fetch once, before its loop; a waiter whose id the newest manifest lists is admitted first (`store.admit`, then a planning view: awaits), so every later waiter got the end read before those awaits. *Test:* `R-SYN-5: an unknown-id resolve that a throttled manifest fetch answers after an earlier waiter's admission reports the time left until the origin's extended end`. A test-only source wrapper holds the manifest request until the second waiter joined the fetch; the request's 429 throttles the origin for 30 s; a test-only metadata executor holds the first waiter's admission while a held transfer's 429 extends the throttle to 60 s; both holds signal through streams whose iteration ends on cancellation. At `67c0cbe` the second waiter was told 30.0 against 60.0. *Fix:* each waiter's throttle answer is computed when it is answered (`throttledError(after:)`).
- **A run that settled blocked for lack of space the ledger had (the correctness verifier's observation, decided; failing test `5a7865c`, fix `87a2cd8`, pins `adba717`).**
  - *Observation:* a job parked `insufficientSpace` waits for the next free-space read (F-28). When a job in flight ends without committing (a 404, a cancellation), its bytes leave the ledger and nothing re-checks the parked jobs. A run whose working claim then finished settled `.blocked(.insufficientSpace)` although the ledger admitted its claim parked for space: its job was retired and the caller was told the volume lacks space (PROBE-C17C-INFLIGHT).
  - *Options:* (a) record it in F-28 as a consequence, and leave the code; (b) re-check before settling.
  - *Decision:* (b), the verifier's second option. It is F-37's reasoning ("settling now would report a gate that is open") on a path without an owed sweep. In `checkRunSettled`'s first pass, a claim parked `insufficientSpace` that `spaceBlock(job)` admits is noted. If no claim works, the jobs parked `insufficientSpace` are re-checked as a free-space read re-checks them (`sweepSpaceParked(.read)`: the pump's order, and the room left by the jobs admitted ahead), and the check runs once more without that step (`checkRunSettled(spaceSwept: true)`). A re-queued claim then works; one still parked settles the run as before. The sweep re-queues at most `readmissionsPerTurn` jobs, parks none, and runs only when a run would otherwise settle (C2). No `await` was added.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim parked for space the ledger admits once a job in flight ended without committing fetches it instead of settling blocked for lack of space` (PROBE-C17C-INFLIGHT without its prints). At `5a7865c` it failed: `.blocked(insufficientSpace, SyncReport(runID: run-2 …))`. Two pins of the fix's other branches: `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim parked for space loses the room the ledger gained to a job ranked ahead of it settles blocked for lack of space at once` (a prefetch job parked ahead takes the room) and `R-SYN-6: a run whose claim parked for space the ledger admits alone but not behind a re-queued job waiting for a slot settles blocked for lack of space at once` (the re-check re-queues nothing, so no later dispatch checks the run; every slot is held by transfers of unknown size). *Mutants:* NOSWEEP (the re-check dropped) fails the first test and the ranked-ahead pin; NORECHECK (no second check after the sweep) fails the nothing-re-queued pin (2 issues); dropping `!spaceSwept` would recurse without end.
  - F-28's consequence is corrected in place.
- **A waiting `ensureAvailable` answered under a throttle extension (the requirements verifier's observation, decided: by design).**
  - *Observation:* F-36's answers turn removes the `.ensure` interest of a member the extended throttle parked, and the call then reports the scope with the member missing (`success`, `missing == 1`, `failed == 0`, `manifestFresh == true`, no retry-after), while a fresh `ensureAvailable` gets `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)`.
  - *Options:* (a) by design; (b) keep the throttle's answer on the group and, while the scope is incomplete, return `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: until − now)`, as F-25 maps a member failed for lack of space (`spaceFailure`).
  - *Decision:* (a), until the orchestrator rules otherwise. The fresh call differs at its manifest step: its fetch is refused, which F-37 answers at once. A call whose manifest is in hand reports the scope; a member a throttle answered counts as missing, which is also what a member the gate fails fast at admission has reported since before cycle 17. No contract text decides between (a) and (b). (b) would change that older answer too, so it is left to a ruling. `R-SYN-5: an ensureAvailable member waiting for a short throttle is answered …` pins (a).
- **L4-R17.** Every new or changed test waits on a condition, a held seam, a held-signal stream or a join, each ending on cancellation; none moves the clock. Rows are added to F-31's table. Stress results are below.
- **Production changes.** All in WP-F's files:
  - `EngineManifest.swift`: the outcome-delivery seam, and the unknown-id answers.
  - `EngineState.swift`: the seam's state and `HeldManifestDelivery`.
  - `EngineLifecycle.swift`: shutdown releases the seam.
  - `EngineRuns.swift`: the run's throttled outcome and `checkRunSettled`'s space re-check.
  - `EngineCommands.swift`: `unreachableError`.
  - `EngineJobs.swift`: `throttledError(after:)`.

  No `await` was added, no contract signature changed and no public declaration was added.

## F-39 — Close-out cycle 19: a run that settled blocked by an origin gate the clock had opened before the alarm's turn, and the throttle end a run settled on its claims reports
- **Context.** Two blocking findings from the cycle-18 correctness verifier, both older than cycle 18 and both of the F-36 class (a run settles blocked by a gate that is open; a reader of the throttle's end F-38 missed). (1) PROBE-C18C-ALARMLAG: the alarm's turn clears an ended throttle and re-queues the jobs it parked only when it runs, after the sleep's wake-up and the `.utility` alarm task's place in the actor's queue; in between, `gate` already admits work (it blocks only while the end is in the future), and a run whose last working claim ended then settled `.blocked(.throttled(until:))` with an end already past. The settlement retired the parked claim's job and armed the pending run at that past end, and the alarm's turn started a second run and a second manifest request (deterministic with a busy engine; 198 of 200 without a seam at HEAD, 187 of 200 at `1d33aac`). It contradicted F-35's "Not done" and F-36's "only while that throttle is in force". (2) PROBE-C18C-CLAIMEND: a run that settles on claims the throttle parked answers in its run task's later turn (`awaitRunSettlement`, then `.blocked(reason, …)`), and a transfer's 429 handled in between extends the origin's throttle: the caller and `syncFinished` got the end the settling turn read, and the pending run stayed at that end, where it started a run the throttle refused at its manifest step (stale in 572 to 599 of 600 without a seam). Non-blocking: the same pending run firing at its old deadline when the 429 lands after the answer (observation); F-38's NOPART1 figure; time-budget figures (L4-R8); F-38's decided observation awaiting a ruling; confirmations of cycle 18's fixes; `outside-wp` items; host hygiene. No new ruling: `l4-rulings.md` has none on F-38's (a)/(b) yet, and L4-R1…L4-R19 are recorded above.
- **Seams (`3e06092`).** Both test-only; no production path arms either.
  - `holdAlarmTurns()` / `releaseAlarmTurns()`: while armed, an alarm whose sleep returned waits in `alarmLoop` before its validation and `fireDue` (`heldAlarmTurns`, by alarm token) until released. `rearm` resumes a replaced alarm's held turn (it then finds its token stale), and shutdown releases the hold. `alarmTurnHeld` is the census a test waits for. This adds the cycle's only new `await` in production code, reached only while the seam is armed; its `// REENTRANCY: RE-S16` comment names what is re-checked, and the existing guard right after it re-checks the lifecycle and the alarm token (due entries are recomputed from `monotonicNow()` as before).
  - `holdRunSettlementDelivery()` / `releaseRunSettlementDelivery()`: while armed, a run's settlement is recorded as before but kept from the run task's wait (`heldRunSettlements`), whether the task registered its wait before the settlement or after it; the release resumes each in that turn, and shutdown releases them with `.shutDown`, as it tells a waiting run task. `runSettlementHeld` is the census.
- **A run that settled blocked by a gate the clock had opened (blocking; failing tests `f7a5eb8`, fix `69aa023`).**
  - *Fix:* in `checkRunSettled`'s first pass, a claim parked `throttled` counts as working while the origin's throttle has ended but is still recorded and the claim's end (read through `throttleEnd(after:)`) has passed; a claim parked `circuitOpen` counts as working while the breaker still reads `.open` with its cooldown ended (`originGatesAwaitingAlarm(now:)`, `EngineJobs`, one read of the clock and two dictionary reads per check). The alarm's turn re-queues such a claim, or owes it a re-queue (`unblockOrigin`), and its pump checks the run again. This follows F-31's row-498 rule (a due gate is left to the alarm).
  - *Why it cannot strand a run:* only the `.throttle` deadline's turn clears a throttle (`fireDue`), and that deadline stays in the heap while the throttle is recorded (F-35; the `unwakeableThrottleParks` invariant), so an ended, still-recorded throttle always has its turn due. A breaker that opens schedules its `.breaker` deadline at the cooldown's end (`breakerChanged` → `scheduleBreakerDeadline`). While it reads `.open` with that end passed, either that turn is still due, or it ran and re-queued or owed every job the breaker parked. A job cannot be parked `circuitOpen` afresh in that state, because the gate admits it as the probe (`CircuitBreaker.admit` half-opens). A half-open breaker is not included: with a probe in flight its gate is closed, and without one it has no deadline to wait for (F-4: the pending run's own probe opens it). *Corrected in close-out cycle 20 (F-40):* with a probe in flight the gate is closed only until that probe's watchdog, after which it admits the next transfer (bug A of L4-C19); and without a probe nothing re-admitted the parked jobs (bug B). F-40 replaces this case and `originGatesAwaitingAlarm` with one gate reading at `now` and an in-turn re-admission.
  - *The breaker case (found in this cycle).* The verifier suggested checking `.circuitOpen` claims and did not probe them. The same seam reproduced it, so under the strict bar it is fixed with the throttle case: a 403 opens the auth breaker (`authDistinctAssets: 1`); the run's third claim parks `circuitOpen`; the cooldown ends with the alarm's turn held; the run's other claim is rejected. Before the fix the run settled `.blocked(.circuitOpen(.authentication))`, and the pending run, its gate open, started a second run whose manifest fetch was the probe.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-6: a run whose last working claim ends after the throttle's end but before the alarm's turn waits for the alarm instead of settling blocked by the ended throttle` (PROBE-C18C-ALARMLAG) and `R-SYN-6: a run whose last working claim ends after the breaker's cooldown but before the alarm's turn waits for the alarm instead of settling blocked by the open breaker`. Each holds the alarm's turns, moves the clock to the gate's end, waits for the held turn, ends the working claim, and then requires four things: the launch run still in place and unsettled, the parked claim still holding its interest, and no second run. After the release it requires `.completeWithFailures` from the launch run, with the parked claim fetched, then a quiet engine with no second run and no manifest request. A wrong outcome ends the test at once: the bug's further runs probe attempts the test does not script, and an earlier draft of the breaker test waited for them until its time limit. At `f7a5eb8` each failed with 4 issues in 0.011 s: the run in place was run 2, the claim had lost the run's interest, a second run had started, and the launch run answered `blocked(throttled(until: 31000000000ns))` or `blocked(circuitOpen(authentication))`.
- **The end a run settled on its claims reports, and its pending run (blocking, and the observation; failing tests `6fb6128`, fix `fd256a0`).**
  - *Fix:* the run task's answer reads the origin's end (`.blocked(self.currentReason(reason), …)` in `runSteps`), as the manifest path does since F-38. `throttleExtended(from:)`, which both 429 paths call whenever they move the origin's end later, moves a pending run that waits for the throttle (`pendingRun?.gate == .throttle`) to the new end and re-arms. So the pending run never fires before the throttle's end, whether the 429 lands before the run answers or after: that is the observation, fixed at its root.
  - *Not done:* the verifier's alternative, making `pendingRunDeadlineFired` re-check the throttle gate. The throttle's end moves only through those two 429 paths, and each now moves the pending run, so such a check could never act: it would be an unreachable branch (G4). Recorded as decided.
  - *Tests:* `R-SYN-5: a run that settled on claims a throttle parked reports the origin's end when a 429 extends the throttle before the run answers` (PROBE-C18C-CLAIMEND with the settlement held) and `R-SYN-5: a throttle-gated pending run waits for the origin's end when a 429 extends the throttle after the run answered` (the observation). Both share `settledOnThrottle(holdSettlement:)`: a launch run's two claims and a prefetch transfer wait in flight; one claim's 429 throttles the origin for 30 s; the other claim is rejected, so the run settles on the parked claim; the prefetch transfer's 429 then extends the throttle to 60 s. Each requires the pending run's deadline at the extended end, gated by the throttle. The first also requires the outcome `.blocked(.throttled(until: E))` with E the origin's end. Then the clock moves, from a quiet engine, to the first end, and each requires no run and no manifest request. At `6fb6128` the first failed with 3 issues: it reported `31000000000ns` against the origin's `61000000000ns`, its pending run was at the old end, and a run started at that end. The second failed with 2 issues: the pending run at the old end, and a run started there.

## F-40 — Close-out cycle 20: the stale-gate race family as a class (L4-R20), its two open members, and the cycle-19 items
- **Context.** Three blocking findings of the cycle-19 verifiers, two distinct bugs, both of the family L4-R20 names (a decision taken from a gate state the clock or an attempt's outcome had changed but the engine had not processed: F-35, F-36, F-38, F-39). (A) L4-C19-00 and L4-C19-07: a half-open breaker whose probe is silent past `probeWatchdog` admits the next transfer (`CircuitBreaker.admit`), while the jobs it parked wait for the `.breaker` deadline's alarm turn at the watchdog; F-39's first-pass case covered only a breaker still reading `.open`, so a run whose last working claim ended between the watchdog and that turn settled `.blocked(.circuitOpen)`, retired its claim's job, and the turn started a second run (192 to 198 of 200 without a seam; deterministic with `holdAlarmTurns()`). F-39's sentence "with a probe in flight its gate is closed" was wrong past the watchdog. (B) L4-C19-08: a probe that ends without a verdict (404, 400, integrity, offline, a different cause, abandoned) leaves `halfOpen(probe: nil)`; the public state is unchanged, so `breakerChanged` did nothing and nothing re-admitted the jobs the breaker parked before the old watchdog. The run settled blocked by a gate that admits, its pending run's gate read open, and runs repeated, one manifest request each, while the clock never moved (7 runs for 6 parked claims, 23 for 19; 28 runs with a prefetch probe). Non-blocking: confirmations of cycle 19, regression run, F-17 figures, benchmarks, ownership, host hygiene, `outside-wp` items, and the manifest-probe watchdog observation (L4-C19-10).
- **Ruling.** L4-R20 (the family as a class, blocking) and L4-R21 (stops), recorded below under "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)".
- **The gates and every transition they make without an engine turn (L4-R20 item 1).**

| Gate | Transition without an engine turn | Processed by (before this cycle) | Processed by (now) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Origin throttle (object 429s, manifest 429s; extensions and the F-35 shortening) | The clock reaches its end. Extensions and shortenings happen in the 429's own turn (`throttleExtended` moves a throttle-gated pending run, F-39) | The `.throttle` deadline's alarm turn (`fireDue`: clear, `unblockOrigin(.throttled)`) | The same turn, through `endThrottle`; and any decision that finds a job parked by an ended throttle performs it first (`readmitOpenedOriginGates`) |
| Breaker open | The clock reaches the cooldown's end (the gate admits the next transfer as the probe, `admit` half-opens lazily) | The `.breaker` deadline's alarm turn at the cooldown's end (`unblockOrigin(.circuitOpen)`, pending run) | The same turn; and any decision that finds a job it parked re-admits first |
| Breaker half-open, probe in flight | The clock passes the probe's watchdog (the gate admits the next transfer as the new probe) | The `.breaker` deadline's alarm turn at the watchdog; no decision site counted it (bug A) | The same turn; and any decision that finds a job it parked re-admits first; the pending run's gate reads open (it read `.armed` on any probe) |
| Breaker half-open, no probe (`halfOpen(probe: nil)`) | Made by an outcome in an engine turn (404, 400, integrity, offline, a different cause; cancelled, paused, superseded, shut down: abandoned), not by the clock | Nothing: the public state is unchanged, so `breakerChanged` returned; the old watchdog's deadline, up to 60 s later (bug B) | That turn: `probeEndedWithoutVerdict` drops the stale watchdog deadline and re-admits every job the breaker parked (`unblockOrigin(.circuitOpen)`, pump follow-up); the first dispatched probes, the others park behind it |
| Space: `awaitingSpace` | The store's reclaim falls due (its `.store` deadline) | The alarm's store work, then `sweepSpaceParked(.reclaimed)`; a run never settles on an `awaitingSpace` claim | Unchanged (not an origin gate; no decision settles on it) |
| Space: `insufficientSpace` | A job in flight ends without committing (the ledger admits a parked claim, F-38); a read's bound leaves a follow-up sweep owed (F-37) | `checkRunSettled` sweeps inline (F-38) and counts an owed sweep as working (F-37) | Unchanged |
| Policy pause / lane admitted again | A conditions element or a pause command (an engine turn) | `readmit(.conditions)`; an owed lane counts as working in `checkRunSettled` | Unchanged |
| Offline with a satisfied edge seen | A conditions element (an engine turn) | `edgeOwed`; owed counts as working | Unchanged |
| Pending-run debounce | The clock reaches the `.pendingRun` deadline | `pendingRunDeadlineFired` → `fulfilPendingRun` | `pendingRunDeadlineFired` → `reconsiderPendingRun`, which reads the gate at `now` |
| A user join's `interactiveMaxWait` | Time only shortens the wait; an extension happens in a 429's turn | `failFast` reads the origin's end at `now`; `answerThrottledUsers` after an extension (F-36) | Unchanged |

- **Every decision site that reads a gate (L4-R20 item 2), and how it reads it now (item 3).** The one shared, pure evaluation is `CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)` (the verdict `admit` gives, without its effect; `admit` now applies it) and the throttle's end against `now`, bundled as `OriginGate` by `originGate(at:)` (`EngineOriginGate.swift`). Every site below reads it, and a site that finds a job parked by a gate that admits re-admits it first (`readmitOpenedOriginGates`: the alarm's work done in this turn), so no decision depends on an alarm turn having run.

| Site | Gate read before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Dispatch (`gate`) | Throttle `until > now`; a copy of the breaker's `admit` | `originGate(at:).throttledUntil`; `admission(of:at:)` (the same function `admit` applies in `start`) |
| A run settling and its blocked reason (`checkRunSettled`, first pass) | F-39's `originGatesAwaitingAlarm`: an ended throttle, or a breaker reading `.open` past its cooldown, counted as working until the alarm's turn; half-open breakers were excluded (bugs A and B) | A claim parked by an origin gate that `originGate` shows admitting is re-admitted in this turn (unless a follow-up already owes it) and counts as working; the run settles blocked only on claims whose gate rejects at `now`. `originGatesAwaitingAlarm` is removed |
| The reported `retryAt` / throttle end (`currentReason`, `throttledError`, `failFast`) | The origin's end at the answering turn (F-36, F-38, F-39) | Unchanged: the run settles only on claims a throttle in force holds, so the end it reports is in the future |
| The run's manifest step (`EngineManifest`) | Throttle `until > now`; `admit(.manifest)` | `originGate(at:).throttledUntil`; `admit(.manifest)`; a manifest probe now schedules its watchdog (L4-C19-10) |
| A failed manifest fetch ending the run blocked (`openBreakerCause`) | The breaker reads `.open` | `.open` and its gate rejects at `now`: a cooldown already over by the answer admits the next run's probe, so the run fails instead |
| The pending run's gate state (`pendingRunGate`) | Breaker: `.open` past its cooldown read open; `halfOpen(probe: nil)` open; any other half-open `.armed`, also past the watchdog | Breaker: open whenever `originGate` admits a parked job (closed, cooldown over, no probe, a probe past its watchdog); otherwise armed by its deadline or a live probe. Throttle: `originGate(at:).throttledUntil`. *Cycle 21 (F-41):* a breaker that admits only a probe reads armed while a pump is owed |
| The pending run's start | `fulfilPendingRun` from four places: `reconsiderPendingRun`, the `.breaker` alarm turn, `pendingRunDeadlineFired`, `breakerChanged(.closed)` | Only `reconsiderPendingRun`, which reads the gate (`fulfilPendingRun` is private); the other three call it |
| The pending run's start against the same turn's re-admission (added in close-out cycle 21, F-41) | `reconsiderPendingRun` in the turn that re-admitted the jobs the breaker parked (a probe's exit without a verdict, the `.breaker` turn): the gate read open before the pump dispatched them, so the run lost the probe to the first and ended blocked, once per probe | A breaker that admits only a probe (`admission(of: nil, at:) == .probe`) reads `.armed` while a pump is owed (`dispatchOwed`); the `.breaker` turn owes one when it re-admits; every `pump()` reconsiders a pending run that waits for the breaker, after the follow-ups it scheduled |
| The pending run's start against a job dispatched before the run's manifest admission (added in close-out cycle 22, F-42) | The breaker's verdict at the run's start (`pendingRunGate`, `dispatchOwed`): the probe stayed free for two task turns, until the fetch task admitted the manifest, and a job dispatched in between (a queued job taking a probe exit's freed slot, a slot freed by a pre-trip transfer, a pause's re-dispatch, a join's batch, a retry) took it; the run settled blocked, once per such job, while the clock did not move | `startRun` admits the manifest fetch as the probe in the run's start turn whenever the breaker admits only a probe (`reserveManifestProbe`; not under a throttle in force, nor for a fetch already past the gates), so every later dispatch reads `.reject`; a fetch that finds the origin throttled lets it go; every probe exit without a verdict and every `.breaker` turn owe a pump follow-up |
| Parking and unparking; re-admitting parked claims | `park` (the dispatch gate's verdict); `unblockOrigin` from the alarm's turns and `breakerChanged(.closed)` | The same, plus `probeEndedWithoutVerdict` and `readmitOpenedOriginGates` (shared with `fireDue`'s `.throttle` turn through `endThrottle`) |
| A promotion (`relane` → `blockCleared`) | Throttle through `throttleEnd`; breaker: `.open` past its cooldown, or any half-open, read cleared | `originGate(at:).admits(reason)`: a half-open breaker with a live probe before its watchdog no longer reads cleared (the job used to be re-queued only to park again, with the same fail-fast answer) |
| A user request failing fast or waiting (`failFastJoiner`, `originAdmitsUser`) | The joiner of a parked job got the parked reason's fail-fast answer, even when the gate already admitted it; the backoff skip refused any `.open` breaker, also past its cooldown, and admitted a half-open one with a live probe | The joiner of a job parked by a gate that admits re-admits it (no stale `authRejected`/`serverUnavailable`); the backoff skip reads `originGate` (no throttle in force, a breaker that admits) |
| The alarm's re-arm (`rearm`, `scheduleBreakerDeadline`) | The heap's earliest deadline | Unchanged; `endThrottle` drops a throttle deadline it pre-empted, and a probe's exit without a verdict drops the stale watchdog |

- **Why the class is impossible now, and why nothing strands or loops.** A decision cannot read a gate otherwise than the next dispatch would, because both read the same pure function at the same `now`. A job parked by a gate that admits is never left to an alarm turn by a decision: the decision re-queues it (or finds it owed by a follow-up turn, which counts as working), and the pump follow-up dispatches it. A re-queued job parks again only through the dispatch gate, that is, only when the same reading rejects, so the re-admission cannot repeat without a change. Every exit of a half-open probe without a verdict re-admits the jobs the breaker parked, in that turn. A pending run starts only through `reconsiderPendingRun`, which reads the gate at `now`. Re-admission is bounded as before (`readmissionsPerTurn`, the rest owed), and the first pass's re-admission runs at most once per claim and transition (afterwards the claim is queued or owed). *Corrected in close-out cycle 21 (F-41):* "nothing loops" did not hold for the pending run. In the turn a probe's exit without a verdict (or the `.breaker` turn) re-admitted the parked jobs, the pending run read the gate open, since no probe was in flight yet, and started; the pump then made the first re-admitted job the probe before the run's manifest step, which the gate rejected, so the run ended blocked with a new pending run: one run per prefetch probe while the clock did not move (11 runs for 10 members, 51 for 50). The pending run now waits for the pump that dispatches the re-admitted jobs.
- **Rejected alternative (from L4-C19-08's sketch).** Re-queuing only the first parked job by rank at a probe's exit without a verdict is cheaper for 10,000 parked prefetch members, but if that job is then held by another gate (a lane policy, lack of space, a throttle), the breaker stays `halfOpen(probe: nil)` with every other parked job left behind while the gate admits (the model test's I3). The full re-admission is the work a cooldown's end already does, measured by the existing benchmark (`R-SYN-5` cooldown end over 10,000: 4.61 to 4.89 ms per the cycle-19 verifiers), and bounded per turn the same way. *Close-out cycle 21 (F-41):* per turn, yes; in total, no. Each exit re-queues every parked job and the pump parks all but the next probe again, so a chain of N parked jobs that each end without a verdict costs about N²/2 re-queues and re-parks, as fast as the origin answers (the cycle-20 verifiers: 1,000 in 2.22 s, 10,000 in 160.88 s in release). The same total existed before (each old watchdog's `.breaker` turn did a full re-admission), but about 60 s apart. Put to the orchestrator in F-41.
- **The randomized model test (L4-R20 item 4).** `R-SYN-6: seeded walks over clock moves, held alarm turns, probe outcomes, 429s and space events never settle, start or park against a gate that admits at now, and never repeat runs without the clock moving` (`EngineGateModelTests`): 160 seeded walks of 60 steps (SplitMix64; `GATE_MODEL_SEEDS=17,42` replays chosen walks, and a failure prints its seed and the walk). Five run claims and three on-demand prefetch members (two walks in three register the group at the start); 2 or 3 slots; `authDistinctAssets: 1`, 60 s auth cooldown, 503s trip after one sample. Steps: an attempt's outcome (success, a same-cause 403, a different-cause 503, 404 and 400 without a verdict, a 429 with a random retry-after; an attempt other than a probe three times in four, so probes go silent), a clock move (to a gate's end or the heap's next deadline four times in five, else by 1 to 70 s), toggling the alarm-turn hold, a boundary step (hold the alarm's turns, move the clock to a gate's end, and end an attempt other than the probe next), registering or releasing the prefetch group (a release abandons a member probe), and filling or freeing the volume (`refreshFreeSpace`). After each step it waits for a settled engine (no store work; nothing due unless the alarm's turn is held; no attempt between its outcome and the engine's handling of it; no owned task but the alarm, the watchers, the attempts' drivers and a run task waiting for its settlement) and checks: I1, no run settles blocked by an origin gate that admits at `now`, and I2, no pending run starts while the gate it waits on is closed at `now`, both inside the settling or starting turn (a condition observer the engine evaluates in every notifying turn); I3, once the alarm has had its turn, no job stays parked by an origin gate that admits at `now`; I4, a step makes at most one manifest request; and `invariantViolations()` is empty. The gates are read by `Oracle`, written from ARCHITECTURE §7.6/§7.7, not from the engine's helpers. Seam: a run's registration of its settlement wait now re-evaluates the test seams' conditions (`e8d5277`; a mutation the settled predicate reads, which notified nothing). *Extended in close-out cycle 21 (F-41):* I4 also bounds the runs that start while the clock does not move (at most two), I5 checks that no pending run waits on an origin gate that admits at `now` with no run in place, and every clock move to an instant lands on it exactly.
- **Failing tests first (`56f5b98`, then `6d8294b` after the seam `e8d5277`), the fix `1f00225` and its per-dispatch read `d9efa40`.**
  - `R-SYN-6: a run whose last working claim ends after a half-open probe's watchdog but before the alarm's turn neither settles blocked by the breaker nor starts a second run` (A, modelled on cycle 19's breaker test): 3 slots, `authDistinctAssets: 1`; a prefetch member probes after the cooldown and stays silent; the run's claim parks behind it; the alarm's turns are held, the clock moves to the watchdog, the run's last working claim ends. Requires the launch run in place and unsettled, its claim keeping the run's interest, no second run; after the release, `.completeWithFailures` with the claim fetched (1) and three failed, then a closed breaker, no second run and no manifest request. At `6d8294b`: 3 issues (the run gone, the claim's interest lost, the outcome `blocked(circuitOpen(authentication))`).
  - `R-SYN-5: a half-open probe that ends without a verdict lets the next parked job probe, so the run neither settles blocked nor starts further runs without the clock moving` (B, the NATURAL probe without prints): 4 slots, 10 claims; one held, one 403, every other answers 404; after the cooldown the clock never moves. Requires no claim left parked once the probes end, the launch run `.completeWithFailures` with 10 failed, then no further run and no manifest request. At `6d8294b`: 2 issues (6 claims still parked; the launch run `blocked(circuitOpen(authentication))`).
  - The model test at `6d8294b` (the unfixed engine): seed 2, I1, a run settled `blocked(circuitOpen)` under `halfOpen(probe: nil)` (B).
- **Mutants (each applied to `Sources` at `1f00225`, or to the unfixed engine where named, built, run against the cycle-19 and cycle-20 suites and the model test, reverted; none committed).**
  - MUT-BONLY (the unfixed engine plus only the probe-exit re-admission, so bug A alone remains): the model test fails at seed 17, I1, a run settled `blocked(circuitOpen)` under `halfOpen(probe: c-50ec…, watchdog: now)`, after a boundary step to the watchdog (A); test A fails with 3 issues; test B passes. Together with the unfixed engine's seed 2 (B), the model test fails on `d5191e2`'s engine for A and for B.
  - NOVERDICT (the fix without `probeEndedWithoutVerdict`): test B fails with 1 issue (claims left parked; the first pass's re-admission still completes the run); the model test fails at seed 12, I3, a job parked `circuitOpen(serverErrors)` under `halfOpen(probe: nil)` after the alarm's turn.
  - NOSETTLEREADMIT (the fix without the first pass's re-admission): both cycle-19 alarm tests fail (4 issues each), test A fails (4 issues), and the model test fails at seed 4, I1, a run settled `blocked(throttled(until: 108 s))` at 108 s (F-39's throttle member).
- **Cycle-19 tests adjusted (this level's own tests; L4-R15's reasoning, no `MIGRATION.md` entry).** `R-SYN-6: a run whose last working claim ends after the throttle's end …` and `… after the breaker's cooldown …` scripted the parked claim's success before their between-checks. The run now re-admits the claim in the turn its last working claim ends, so that success could finish the run before the checks read it, a scheduling dependence (L4-R17). Each now scripts the success after its checks and the alarm's release; every assertion is unchanged. Their display names ("waits for the alarm") predate F-40: the run no longer depends on the alarm's turn, it still neither settles nor loses its claim, which is what they assert.
- **F-3, F-4 and F-39 corrected in place.** F-3's consequence: a probe's exit without a verdict re-admits the parked jobs, and a pending run starts only once its gate reads open at `now`. F-39's "a half-open breaker is not included: with a probe in flight its gate is closed" holds only until the probe's watchdog; F-40 replaces F-39's first-pass case and `originGatesAwaitingAlarm`.
- **L4-C19-10 (observation, fixed).** A manifest probe admitted from `halfOpen(probe: nil)` scheduled no watchdog (the public state did not change); it now schedules one, as a transfer probe does.
- **L4-R17.** The new tests wait only on conditions, the held alarm seam and `EventProbe.next`, each ending on cancellation. In the model test every clock move is made from a settled engine; the attempts it holds have no clock dependence but the probe's watchdog, which is the point of the walk. Row added to F-31's table.
- **C2 (the cooldown-end benchmark, measured in this cycle).** The first measurement after `1f00225` gave `R-SYN-5 cooldown end over 10,000 prefetch members parked by the breaker` 8.08 and 8.12 ms against 5.62 ms for the unfixed engine on the same host minutes later: the dispatch gate built the whole `OriginGate` per job (a copy of the remote's state and two more dictionary reads for each of the 512 jobs a cooldown's turn re-queues and parks again). `d9efa40` reads each gate once per job through the same pure functions (`OriginGate.inForce`, `CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`): 5.60 ms (runs 5.60, 5.65, 5.60). The probe-exit re-admission costs the same per exit as that turn, within C2's 10 ms. *Close-out cycle 21:* that is one exit, not a chain; the chain's total is quadratic (F-41, the new benchmark).
- **REENTRANCY.** No new `await` in production code; f-n rows unchanged.
- **Production changes.** All in WP-F's files: `EngineOriginGate.swift` (new: `OriginGate` with `inForce(_:at:)`, `originGate(at:)`, `originGateNow()`, `readmitOpenedOriginGates(_:)`, `endThrottle(_:)`); `EngineSupport.swift` (`CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`, `probe`, `admit` applies `admission`; both new members internal, no contract change); `EngineFailures.swift` (`probeEndedWithoutVerdict`, `breakerChanged(.closed)` → `reconsiderPendingRun`); `EngineRuns.swift` (first pass, `pendingRunGate`, `openBreakerCause`, private `fulfilPendingRun`, the settlement-wait notification); `EngineTimers.swift` (`.throttle` turn through `endThrottle`, `.breaker` turn and `pendingRunDeadlineFired` through `reconsiderPendingRun`); `EngineJobs.swift` (`blockCleared`, `originAdmitsUser`, `failFastJoiner`; `originGatesAwaitingAlarm` removed); `EngineDispatch.swift` (`gate` reads `OriginGate.inForce` and `admission`; `schedulePumpFollowUp` internal); `EngineManifest.swift` (throttle through `originGate`, the manifest probe's watchdog). No contract signature changed and no public declaration was added.

The Sync and cleanup suites at `d9efa40` (load 3.5 rising to 8.9): 22 of 24; the two misses are P-11 (speedup 2.963 against 3.0; three runs of P-11 alone right after, load 5.8 to 6.3: 3.463, 3.487 and 3.422, so load) and WP-C's `BenchCleanup` C2 eviction benchmark (`outside-wp`). ## F-41 — Close-out cycle 21: a pending run that lost the half-open probe to the jobs its own turn re-admitted, the PEND pin, and the cycle-20 items
- **Context.** One blocking finding of the cycle-20 correctness verifier, a member of the stale-gate family (L4-R20) that cycle 20's re-admission introduced. When a half-open probe ended without a verdict, `recordBreaker` re-admitted every job the breaker parked (`probeEndedWithoutVerdict`) and, in the same turn, `reconsiderPendingRun` read the pending run's gate open (no probe in flight yet) and started the run; the pump then made the first re-admitted job the probe before the run's manifest step, the gate rejected the manifest, and the run ended `.blocked(.circuitOpen)` with a new pending run. The `.breaker` alarm turn did the same at a cooldown's end. One run per prefetch probe while the clock never moved (PROBE-C20C-PREFETCH-PROBES: 11 runs after the cooldown for 10 members, 51 for 50; each emitted `syncStarted(…, .breakerClosed)` and `syncFinished(.blocked)`). F-40's "nothing strands or loops" was wrong for the pending run. Non-blocking: the PEND reading no test caught (requirements), the total cost of a chain of probe exits (both verifiers), other F-40 site changes no test pins (requirements, optional), the model's outcome kinds (requirements, optional), `ManualTimeSource.advance(to:)` stopping 1 ns short (correctness), confirmations, regression runs, F-17 figures, ownership (F-38's (a)/(b) still unruled), `outside-wp` items and host hygiene.
- **Ruling.** No new ruling this cycle: `l4-rulings.md` holds L4-R1…L4-R21, each recorded above under "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)". L4-R20 (the class) and L4-R21 (stops) apply.
- **Why F-40's class argument missed it.** F-40 makes every decision read the gate as the next dispatch reads it, and the pending run did: the breaker admitted a probe. But a breaker that admits only a probe has one to give, and two contenders were in line for it: the jobs re-admitted in that turn, which a pump dispatches at the end of the turn or in a follow-up, and the pending run's manifest fetch, admitted in the run task's own later turn. The verdict at the run's start was right and stale by the time its manifest asked: a decision whose effect lands after an engine turn that changes the gate, the family's shape. A closed breaker admits every transfer, so only the probe-only states (open past its cooldown, half-open without a probe, half-open past a silent probe's watchdog) have the contention.
- **The fix (`6fcf4f8`), and the site-table row added to F-40.** A breaker-gated pending run starts only after the pump that dispatches the jobs re-admitted for the probe:
  - `pendingRunGate(.breaker)` reads `.armed` while the breaker admits only a probe (`admission(of: nil, at:) == .probe`) and a pump is owed (`dispatchOwed`: a pump follow-up, a re-admission follow-up, re-queues still owed to the origin's jobs, or an `ensureAvailable` refill; each ends in `pump()`).
  - The `.breaker` alarm turn owes a pump follow-up when it re-admits parked jobs (its own pump may follow an await on the store's work, and `strandedPendingRun` reads the gate meanwhile); `probeEndedWithoutVerdict` and `readmitOpenedOriginGates` already owed one.
  - Every `pump()` reconsiders a pending run that waits for the breaker, after the follow-ups it scheduled. After that pump either a re-admitted job is the probe (the gate rejects; its outcome reconsiders through `recordBreaker`), or none could start (another gate holds them, or no slot is free) and the run starts, its manifest fetch probing (F-4).
  - *Why nothing strands:* `.armed` here is backed by an owned task that ends in `pump()`, each flag of `dispatchOwed` being set only with its task spawned (and cleared when the spawn fails); every pump reconsiders; `strandedPendingRun` reads the same gate. Without the pump's reconsideration (mutant NOPUMPEND) the model fails at seed 17, I5, a pending run waiting on a half-open breaker that admits.
  - *Why it no longer loops:* a pending run starts only when no job it could lose the probe to is about to be dispatched. A run that starts and still loses the probe needs a job that was neither parked nor re-admitted at its start (one waiting for a slot, a prefetch handle's join batch in a later turn, new work a command queued), and that job's probe must end first, so the runs started while the clock does not move are bounded by such jobs, not by probe exits. The model's I4 bound of two runs holds over 2,160 walks (seeds 0 to 2,159); the engine of `58287dc` reaches three or four in 19 of 1,000. *(Amended in close-out cycle 22, F-42: wrong for jobs waiting in the queue for a slot, which are neither parked nor re-admitted; a probe exit owed no pump for them, so the run started in the exit's turn and the exit's own pump gave the freed slot, and the probe, to the next of them: PROBE-C21C-QUEUED-PROBES, 10 runs for 10 members. The loss itself is now impossible: the run holds the probe from its start turn.)*
  - *Residue (accepted):* a pending run can still lose the probe to a job a prefetch handle's join queues in a later turn of the same join; each such loss costs one blocked run and needs that job's probe to end before the next start, so it is bounded by the join's batches.
  - *Cost:* O(1) per pump; one extra pump turn per `.breaker` deadline that finds parked jobs (cooldown end over 10,000 parked members 5.497 and 5.716 ms this cycle, 5.60 ms in cycle 20). No new `await`; no f-n row changes (REENTRANCY).
- **Failing tests first (`ebbc5a0`), then the fix (`6fcf4f8`).**
  - `R-SYN-5: prefetch probes that each end without a verdict start no run per probe while the clock does not move` (`EngineRegressionCycle21Tests`, PROBE-C20C-PREFETCH-PROBES without its prints): 2 slots, `authDistinctAssets: 1`, 4 claims (held, 403, two queued) and 10 on-demand prefetch members; the 403 parks the queued claims and every member, the held claim's 400 ends the launch run blocked by the open breaker, every object answers 404 from then on, and the clock moves once, to the cooldown's end. Requires one run after the cooldown, no member left parked, at most one manifest request, and the last run `.completeWithFailures`. At `58287dc`: 11 runs. The commit with the fix tightened the verifier's suggested bound (at most two runs) to exactly one: the cooldown's own run no longer starts before its first member probes (mutant NOBREAKERFOLLOWUP gives 2). At `6d8294b` (the engine before cycle 20's fix, with its seam): 4 runs, 9 members left parked, the stranding of bug B.
  - The gate model's I4 now also bounds the runs that start while the clock does not move (`runsWithoutClockMove = 2`, read as `lastRunID` against its value at the last clock move): at `58287dc` seed 24, three runs.
- **The PEND pin (requirements finding, non-blocking; `224b5a5`).** `R-SYN-5: a pending run that waits for a half-open breaker starts once its silent probe's watchdog passes`: as cycle 20's test A, but the run's last working claim ends before the probe's watchdog, so the run settles blocked (correctly) and leaves a breaker-gated pending run; the clock moves to the watchdog, and the test waits for a condition that holds whether or not the run starts (a new run, or nothing due, no `.breaker` deadline, no store work, no pump owed). Requires the run started (`.breakerClosed`) and its one manifest request. And the model's I5 (in `staleParks`, read once the alarm has had its turn): with no run in place, a pending run that waits for the breaker while the oracle reads it admitting, or for the throttle while it is not in force, is a violation. Under PEND (a half-open breaker with a probe reads armed, also past the watchdog) the test fails ("0 runs, pending breaker") and the model fails at seed 47, I5, as the verifier found. At `6d8294b` the test passes (the `.breaker` turn then started the run without reading the gate), as expected for a pin of cycle 20's reading.
- **Exact clock moves (correctness finding, non-blocking).** `EngineRegressionCycle21Tests.advance(_:to:)` moves a `ManualTimeSource` to an instant and then by 1 ns steps until it is reached: `advance(to:)` goes through a `Double` and `MonotonicInstant.adding(seconds:)` truncates, so it stopped 1 ns short in 2.9 % of random gaps, where a boundary step tested nothing. The gate model and the two new tests use it, and the new benchmark does the same inline. `ManualTimeSource` is WP-H's file and is not edited (the merge paragraph asks the orchestrator).
- **The chain's total cost (both verifiers, non-blocking; put to the orchestrator).** Each probe exit without a verdict re-queues every job the breaker parked and the pump parks all but the next probe again, so N parked jobs whose objects each answer 404 cost about N²/2 re-queues and re-parks, back to back. Each turn stays within C2 (`readmissionsPerTurn`, `parksPerPump`). The verifiers' release figures: 1,000 in 2.22 s, 2,000 in 9.03 s, 4,000 in 36.48 s, 10,000 in 160.88 s (load 6.3 falling to 2.5); one exit over 10,000 parked 5.20 ms; a 10,000-member release whose member is the probe 4.75 ms. The new benchmark `R-SYN-5: a chain of half-open probes ending without a verdict over 1,000 prefetch members parked by the breaker holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn, and reports the chain's total time` (`8f23a2d`): longest actor wait 2.119 and 2.144 ms (median of three; budgets 50 ms median, 10 ms best), totals 2.82 to 3.01 s and 3.24 to 3.63 s in two full runs. Not changed this cycle, because neither verifier holds it blocking and the cheaper design is a trade-off F-40 rejected once: re-queuing in rank order only until one job is admitted as the probe (a job another gate holds moves to that gate's park; one left queued for lack of a slot falls back to the full re-admission) would make each exit cost one scan of the parked set instead of a re-queue and re-park of each, still quadratic in scans (an ordered index of the parked set would remove that, a larger change of the transition funnel). **For the orchestrator:** rule the measured burst acceptable (one full re-admission per exit without a verdict, as L4-C19-08 asked), or order the lazy re-admission.
- **Other F-40 site changes without a test (requirements, optional).** Recorded as defence in depth with no reachable difference, each by the verifier's reading and ours: FULFIL (the `.breaker` turn and `pendingRunDeadlineFired` start the pending run without reading the gate) needs a stale `.breaker` deadline, which `scheduleBreakerDeadline` prevents; JOIN (a joiner of a circuit-parked job gets the parked reason's fail-fast answer) needs a same-lane user join of such a job, which `relane` → `blockCleared` promotes in most cases; USER (the old backoff skip) and CLEAR (the old promotion reading) only re-queued jobs that parked again; OBC (`openBreakerCause` without the gate check) changes a run whose manifest failure arrives after the cooldown ended from blocked to failed, with the sync still due; MWD (the manifest probe's watchdog) is masked by any job that parks behind the manifest probe (`park(.circuitOpen)` schedules the breaker's deadline), and with nothing parked the watchdog has nothing to re-admit, so the verifier's suggested tuning (`manifestFetchDeadline > probeWatchdog`) does not expose it either.
- **The model's outcome kinds (requirements, optional).** Not extended: an offline exit, a policy pause, supersession, an integrity failure and a cancelled resolve all leave a probe through the same `recordBreaker` hook (`probing && probe == nil && halfOpen`) as the 404 and 400 the walks produce, and adding outcome kinds changes every walk's random stream, which would invalidate the seeds this ADR cites for cycle 20's unfixed engine.
- **Mutants (each applied to `Sources` at `6fcf4f8`, built in `.build-f`, run against the whole Sync suite at the final tests, reverted; none committed).** NODEFER (no `dispatchOwed` wait): the chain test 11 runs; the model seed 24, I4, three runs. NOBREAKERFOLLOWUP (the `.breaker` turn owes no pump): the chain test 2 runs. NOPUMPEND (no reconsideration at the pump's end): the model seed 17, I5. PEND: the watchdog test (0 runs) and the model seed 47, I5. The engine of `6d8294b` (before cycle 20's fix): the model seed 2, I1, B; the chain test 4 runs and 9 members left parked.
- **L4-R17.** The new tests wait only on conditions and `EventProbe.next`, each ending on cancellation; clock moves are made from an engine with nothing due. Row added to F-31's table.
- **Production changes.** WP-F's files only: `EngineRuns.swift` (`pendingRunGate(.breaker)`, `dispatchOwed`, the `.armed` doc), `EngineDispatch.swift` (the pump's reconsideration), `EngineTimers.swift` (the `.breaker` turn's pump follow-up). No contract signature changed and no public declaration was added. Tests: `EngineRegressionCycle21Tests.swift` (new), `EngineGateModelTests.swift`, `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchSync.swift` (WP-F's benchmark file, F-12).
- **Figures at the last code commit (`8f23a2d`; run in this session after it).**
  - `SWIFTPM_SCRATCH=.build-f bash Scripts/verify.sh --quick`: `VERIFY: PASS` (1,094 tests: Sync 299 in 5.635 s).
  - Release build with warnings as errors: `Build complete! (27,74 s)`, no warning. `UNSAFE escapes found=0 max=3`; `DOCS public declarations=1281 documented=1281 exempt=0 fraction=100.0%`.
  - `ZTronAssetSyncTests` three times (1-minute load 3.65 to 9.90): 299 passed in 5.553, 5.589 and 5.575 s; under the thread sanitizer (load 8.53 falling to 5.77) 299 passed in 21.181 s, with no ThreadSanitizer report. The gate model over seeds 160 to 2,159: passed (23.096 and 24.135 s).
  - Coverage: `ZTronAssetSync lines= 93.24% (8135/8725) min=90.0 OK` (`ZTronAssetKit` 37.82 %, `outside-wp`, L4-R11).
  - iOS build (`generic/platform=iOS`, warnings as errors): succeeded. iOS Simulator 897F9D8D (iPhone 18 Pro, iOS 27.0; load 2.67 at the start, 40.07 at the end): result Passed, 1,094 of 1,094; the simulator is shut down.
  - No run of this cycle recorded "Time limit was exceeded".
  - Release benchmarks (`.build-f-perf`; 1-minute load 29.22 at the start, 2.83 at the end): 29 of 30 passed; P-13 (WP-B, `outside-wp`) failed with a unit max of 10.49 ms against 5 ms. WP-F figures: P-4 12.05 ms (ratio 2.081); P-7 1.20 and 0.19 MiB; P-11 speedup 3.434; P-14 0 timers, 0 tasks; prune ratio 1.382; space parking 3.49 and 3.29 ms; space re-admission 4.52 ms; network edges 1.05 and 4.38 ms; releases 4.12 and 4.15 ms; re-joins 4.52 and 4.71 ms; cancelled resolve 0.022 ms; cooldown end 5.716 ms; the probe chain 2.144 ms (totals 3.36, 3.24 and 3.63 s); throttle extension 6.28 ms.

## F-42 — Close-out cycle 22: the half-open probe held for a starting run's manifest fetch (the contention closed as a class), a pump owed after every probe exit, and the cycle-21 items
- **Context.** One blocking finding of the cycle-21 correctness verifier, a member of the stale-gate family (L4-R20) that contradicted F-41's "Why it no longer loops" and the model's I4 bound. `probeEndedWithoutVerdict` owed a pump only when the breaker had parked jobs, and so did the `.breaker` alarm turn. Jobs that wait in the queue for a slot are not parked (the pump stops when no slot is free), so in a probe exit's turn `recordBreaker`'s reconsideration read no pump owed (`dispatchOwed`) and started the pending run; that turn's own pump (`failed()`'s, with no `await` in between for 400, 405, 409, 422, 451, refused redirects, offline and `backgroundSessionUnavailable`) gave the freed slot, and the probe, to the next queued job before the run's manifest admission; the run ended `.blocked(.circuitOpen)` with a new pending run. One run per queued member while the clock never moved (PROBE-C21C-QUEUED-PROBES: 10 runs for 10 members, 40 for 40; PROBE-C21C-DEFAULT-TUNING, the default tuning with two long downloads in flight: 10 runs; the committed model with one reserved slot, seed 966: I4, three runs). Non-blocking: the requirements verifier's benchmark miss under load and its re-runs, F-41's chain cost and F-38's (a)/(b) still unruled, the confirmations of cycle 21 (both verifiers), regression runs, F-17 figures, benchmarks and `outside-wp` items, host hygiene.
- **Ruling.** No new ruling this cycle: `l4-rulings.md` still holds L4-R1…L4-R21, each recorded above under "Orchestrator rulings (close-out)"; L4-R20 (the class) and L4-R21 (stops) apply. F-41's chain cost and F-38's (a)/(b) stay requests to the orchestrator (last bullet).
- **Why fixing the reported case alone would not close the class (L4-R20 item 3).** Cycles 20, 21 and 22 each closed one way a job could take the half-open probe from a run between the run's start and its manifest fetch's admission (a probe exit's re-admission, the `.breaker` turn's, now a queued job taking an exit's freed slot), and each fix's argument named the next way as bounded residue. The window itself stayed: the run's manifest fetch is admitted two task turns after the run starts (the run task's first turn begins it, the fetch task's turn admits it), and any turn in between can dispatch a job: a slot freed by an attempt started before the trip (the verifier's own residual), a pause's re-dispatch (`paused()` frees its slot only after its `resumePoint` await), a prefetch join's later batch (F-41's accepted residue), a retry that falls due, a user command. `dispatchOwed` orders the contenders only while the engine already knows the next dispatch; it cannot know of a slot that frees in a later turn. The verifier named the class-closing alternative: reserve the half-open probe for the run's manifest once the run starts (F-4). This cycle does that, and also applies the verifier's sketch.
- **The fix, part 1 (the class; `b3e8c53`): a run holds the probe from its start turn.** ARCHITECTURE §7.6/§7.7 and F-4: "the half-open probe is the next run's manifest fetch". `startRun` now admits the manifest fetch (`TransferID.manifest`) as the probe in the run's start turn whenever the breaker admits only a probe (`reserveManifestProbe`: open past its cooldown, half-open without a probe or past a silent probe's watchdog), before the run is in place, so from that turn on the breaker rejects every other transfer and a job dispatched in any later turn parks behind the probe. The fetch's own admission finds its probe (`CircuitBreaker.admit` admits a probe again within its watchdog, the path a paused probe's re-dispatch already took), so no other admission site changed.
  - *Edges.* Nothing is held when the origin is throttled at `now` (the fetch asks for nothing, and the throttle gates every job too). Nothing is held when the run joins a manifest fetch already past the gates (`ManifestFetch.admitted`): no gate can reject that fetch any more, and its outcome, admitted under a closed breaker, must not count as a probe's (a 200 sent before an auth trip would close the breaker). A fetch that finds the origin throttled at its admission lets the probe go (`releaseManifestProbe`, recorded as abandoned, so `recordBreaker` re-admits what parked behind it, F-40), as does a run whose task could not be spawned.
  - *Events and deadlines.* The probe's watchdog is scheduled at the reservation, as for any probe; `circuitChanged(.halfOpen)` is published right after `syncStarted` (the reservation happens before the run is in place, its event after), so the event order of a run that probes is unchanged.
  - *Why the contention is impossible now.* A run loses the probe only if a job is admitted as the probe after the run started and before its manifest's admission. From the start turn the breaker's verdict is `.reject` for every transfer but `.manifest` until the probe's watchdog, and the watchdog passes only when the clock moves. A pending run still starts only through its gate's reading (F-40); a run that starts while the breaker rejects (open before its cooldown, a live job probe) asks for nothing, as F-4 says.
- **The fix, part 2 (the reported case; the verifier's sketch).** `probeEndedWithoutVerdict` owes a pump follow-up whether or not anything was parked, and so does every `.breaker` alarm turn (a retry due in that same turn re-queues a job too). The pending run then waits for the pumps that dispatch the queued jobs: F-41's order, the re-admitted and queued jobs probe first and the run starts once none can start. With part 1 this order no longer prevents a loss; it keeps the behaviour F-41 chose, and each half is pinned by a test of its own.
- **A new invariant (`f54417c`).** `invariantViolations()` reports a probe held for the manifest fetch while no manifest fetch is in flight and no run is in place: a probe held for a fetch that neither runs nor is about to begin. The gate model checks it after every settled step; under mutant NORELEASE it fails at seed 74 (and at seed 744 with one reserved slot), three replays of three.
- **Failing tests first (`0e0de5f`), then the fix (`b3e8c53`).**
  - `R-SYN-5: prefetch members that wait for a slot and each end without a verdict start no run per probe while the clock does not move` (`EngineRegressionCycle22Tests`, the verifier's suggested test): `SyncTuning(maxConcurrentTransfers: 2, …)` with the default reserved slot, so the run's claims and ten prefetch members share one slot; the 403 parks the members and the launch run settles blocked; every member answers 400 and the clock moves once, to the cooldown's end. Requires one run after the cooldown, no member queued or parked in that run's start turn (read by a condition observer), at most one manifest request, and the run `.completeWithFailures`. At `5501c8c`: 10 runs, 9 members waiting at the first run's start.
  - `R-SYN-5: a run that starts while the half-open breaker admits only a probe holds it for its manifest fetch from its start turn`: one slot; the 403 opens the breaker and the run settles blocked and retires its other claim, so at the cooldown's end the pending run starts with nothing to dispatch. In the run's start turn the breaker's verdict for any other transfer must be `.reject` and its state `halfOpen(probe: .manifest, watchdog:)`; one manifest request; the run completes. At `5501c8c`: `.probe`, the breaker `open` past its cooldown.
  - The gate model: I6 (in the turn a run starts, a breaker that admits only a probe already holds it for the run's manifest fetch, unless the origin is throttled at `now` or the run joins a fetch already past the gates); a second test walks with one slot reserved for user-initiated work (seeds 0 to 159 and the verifier's 966); I2 reads a probe that is the manifest fetch as the run's own. At `5501c8c`: seed 0, I6, in both tests. The fix commit narrowed I6's exemption from "a fetch in flight" to "a fetch already past the gates" (`ManifestFetch.admitted` did not exist before it).
- **Pins written after the fix (L4-R14; `eb936d2`), each failing under its mutant.**
  - `R-SYN-6: a run's held manifest probe is let go when its fetch finds the origin throttled, so the jobs parked behind it wait for the throttle`: the alarm's turns are held at the cooldown's end, and a member started before the trip answers 429; its turn starts the pending run (which holds the probe) and throttles the origin. Requires the parked member throttle-parked, no probe held and no `.breaker` deadline. NORELEASE: the member waits behind `halfOpen(probe: .manifest)` with a watchdog armed.
  - `R-SYN-5: a retry that falls due in the alarm turn of the cooldown's end probes before the pending run starts`: one alarm turn re-queues a timed-out member and handles the cooldown's end with nothing parked. NOBREAKERPUMP: one member queued at the run's start.
  - `R-SYN-5: a run that joins a manifest fetch admitted while the breaker was closed holds no probe for it, so that fetch's answer does not close the breaker` (`HeldManifestSource`). NOADMITTED: `halfOpen(probe: .manifest)` at the join.
- **Mutants (each applied to `Sources` at the final code, built in `.build-f`, run against the whole Sync suite, reverted; none committed).** NORESERVE (no reservation): the start-turn test, the throttle pin and both model tests (seed 0, I6). NOEXITPUMP (a probe exit owes a pump only with parked jobs): the slot test (9 waiting). NOBREAKERPUMP: the retry pin (1 waiting). NOADMITTED: the joined-fetch pin. NORELEASE: the throttle pin, and the model at seeds 74 and 744 through the new invariant. A reservation placed after the run is in place (this cycle's first draft) fails the start-turn test, because `spawn` already notifies with the run in place.
- **F-40 and F-41 amended.** F-40's site table gains the row "the pending run's start against a job dispatched before the run's manifest admission". F-41's "Why it no longer loops" is marked wrong for jobs waiting for a slot; its accepted residue (a join's later batches) and the cycle-21 verifier's residual (a slot freed by an unrelated transfer) are closed by part 1. The model's I4 bound stays at two runs while the clock does not move: a second run can still follow without the clock moving when the first settles blocked on claims parked behind a job's probe (not a lost manifest probe) and that probe then ends without a verdict.
- **Cost and REENTRANCY.** One `admission` read per run start; one extra pump turn per probe exit without a verdict and per `.breaker` deadline (each bounded by `parksPerPump`). No new `await`, `Task`, `@available` or unsafe construct; no f-n row changes.
- **L4-R17.** The new tests wait on conditions, a condition observer (`RunStartWatch`, built like the model's `Recorder`), held seams (`holdAlarmTurns`, `HeldManifestSource`) and `EventProbe.next`, each ending on cancellation; clock moves are made from an engine with nothing due, or with the alarm's turns held. The joined-fetch pin waits for the joining run's manifest deadline, scheduled in the turn the run registers as the fetch's waiter, after requiring that nothing earlier is due, so that scheduling it re-arms the alarm and notifies. Row added to F-31's table.
- **Production changes.** WP-F's files only: `EngineRuns.swift` (`startRun` holds the probe; the `.armed` comment), `EngineManifest.swift` (`reserveManifestProbe`, `releaseManifestProbe`, `admitted` set at the fetch's admission), `EngineState.swift` (`ManifestFetch.admitted`), `EngineFailures.swift` and `EngineTimers.swift` (the pump follow-ups), `EngineCommands.swift` (the invariant). No contract signature changed and no public declaration was added. Tests: `EngineRegressionCycle22Tests.swift` (new), `EngineGateModelTests.swift`.

- **For the orchestrator (unchanged requests).** (1) F-41's chain cost: rule the measured quadratic total of a chain of probe exits without a verdict acceptable, or order the lazy re-admission F-41 describes.
